Equivariant operads, symmetric sequences, and Boardman-Vogt tensor products
Natalie Stewart

Abstract
. We advance the foundational study of be Nardin-Shah’s ∞ \infty -category of G G -operads and their associated ∞ \infty -categories of algebras. In particular, we construct the underlying G G -symmetric sequence of a (one color) G G -operad, yielding a monadic functor; we use this to lift Bonventre’s genuine operadic nerve to a conservative functor of ∞ \infty -categories, restricting to an equivalence between categories of discrete G G -operads. Using this, we extend Blumberg-Hill’s program concerning 𝒩 ∞ {\mathcal{N}}_{\infty} -operads to arbitrary sub-operads of the terminal G G -operad, which we show are equivalent to weak indexing systems. We then go on to define and characterize a homotopy-commutative and closed Boardman-Vogt tensor product on Op G \mathrm{Op}_{G} ; in particular, this specializes to a G G -symmetric monoidal ∞ \infty -category of 𝒪 {\mathcal{O}} -algebras in a G G -symmetric monoidal ∞ \infty -category whose 𝒫 {\mathcal{P}} -algebras are objects with interchanging 𝒪 {\mathcal{O}} -algebra and 𝒫 {\mathcal{P}} -algebra structures.



Introduction

Within the burgeoning study of algebraic structures in
G
-equivariant homotopy theory, tensor products are generalized to
indexed tensor products
, leading to the notion of
G
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories

[
HH16
,
BH21
]
.
Naturally,
G
-equivariant algebraic theories are represented by
G
-operads
, including the equivariant little cubes/Seiner operads of
[
GM17
]
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads of
[
BH15
]
.
In this paper, we use
\infty
-categorical foundations to advance the homotopy theory of
G
-operads, both structurally on Nardin-Shah’s
\infty
-category of
G
-
\infty
-operads
\mathrm{Op}_{G}
(henceforth just
G
-operads) and individually on the
\infty
-categories of algebras
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})
of
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras for various examples of interest.
1
1

1

In this paper we will call
\infty
-categories
\infty
-categories
and
\infty
-categories with discrete mapping spaces
1-categories
, as their theory is equivalent to the traditional theory of categories.
More generally, we will call
\infty
-categories whose mapping spaces are
(d-1)
-truncated
d
-categories
.

Our first contribution generalizes the rudimentary theory of
G
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories to
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
, for
I
a
weak
indexing category in the sense of
[
Ste24a
]
;
these posses indexed tensor products over a collection of arities only under the assumptions that they can be restricted and composed.

We go on to generalize
G
-operads to
I
-operads
, which occur as a full subcategory
\mathrm{Op}_{I}\subset\mathrm{Op}_{G}
with a terminal object
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}
, which we refer to as a
weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operad
;
in particular, an
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
has an underlying (colored)
I
-operad of the same name, and
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
correspond with maps of
G
-operads
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
.
We combinatorially classify the weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads as
weak indexing systems
, generalizing
[
Rub21
,
GW18
,
BP21
,
NS22
]
.

One of our central constructions is a monadic
underlying
G
-symmetric sequence
functor

\operatorname{sseq}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{G}^{\mathrm{oc}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G},{\mathcal{S}}),

the former being the
one-colored

G
-operads.
The objects of
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G}
are identified with pairs
(H,S)
where
H\subset G
is a subgroup and
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{H}
is a finite
H
-set;
given this data, we write
{\mathcal{O}}(S)\coloneqq\operatorname{sseq}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}(S)
, which we call the
S
-ary structure space of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
.
This intertwines with Bonventre’s genuine operadic nerve, so the nerve lifts to a conservative functor of
\infty
-categories.

We use this data to characterize the compatible
(d+1)
-categories of
G
-symmetric monoidal
d
-categories
and
G
-
d
-operads
:
a
G
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a
G
-
d
-operad
if the
S
-ary structure space
{\mathcal{O}}(S)
is
(d-1)
-truncated for all subgroups
H\subset G
and finite
H
-sets
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{H}
.
These are a localizing subcategory, and the corresponding
homotopy
G
-
d
-operad
functor
h_{d}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{G,d}
acts on structure spaces as
(d-1)
-truncation.
We characterize the free
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra monad, showing that the functor
\mathrm{Alg}_{(-)}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{G,\leq(d-1)})
of algebras in
(d-1)
-truncated
G
-spaces detects
h_{d}
-equivalences between one color
G
-operads;
in particular, taking algebras in
G
-spaces is conservative.

When
d\leq 1
, we show that the restriction of Bonventre’s nerve to genuine
G
-operads with
(d-1)
-truncated structure spaces maps equivalently onto
G
-
d
-operads, and we classify the
G
-0-operads as the weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads.
Using this, we classify the
d
-connected
I
-operads as those whose algebras in
d
-truncated
G
-spaces lift canonically to weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-spaces.

Having done this, we define a homotopy-commutative tensor product on
\mathrm{Op}_{G}
called the
Boardman-Vogt tensor product
.
We show that this tensor product is
closed
, i.e. it has an associated
(colored)
G
-operad of algebras

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})
.
When
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
is an
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, we show that
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})
underlies an
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, which we give the same name;
in particular,
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})
is an
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category whose
{\mathcal{P}}
-algebras are characterized by the formula

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}}).

We thus interpret
{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras as
homotopy coherently interchanging pairs of
{\mathcal{P}}
-algebras and
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras
;
indeed we give a “bifunctor” presentation generalizing
[
HA
, § 2.2.5.3]
.

We end by developing an “inflation and fixed points” adjunction
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{G}\colon\mathrm{Op}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Op}_{G}\colon\Gamma^{G}
and showing that it is compatible with Boardman-Vogt tensor products.
We now move on to a more careful accounting of the background and main results of this paper.

Background and motivation

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a semiadditive 1-category, i.e. a pointed 1-category whose
norm
map
X\sqcup Y\rightarrow X\times Y
is an isomorphism for all
X,Y\in{\mathcal{C}}
.
Let
G
be a finite group and let
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
be the orbit category of
G
.
2
2

2

The
orbit category
is the full subcategory of
G
-sets
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}\subset\mathrm{Set}_{G}
spanned by the homogeneous
G
-sets
[G/H]
for
H\subset G
a subgroup.

Recall that a
semi-Mackey functor
valued in
{\mathcal{C}}
is the data of:

•

a contravariant functor
R\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{G}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
, and

•

a covariant functor
N\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{G}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}

subject to the conditions that

(a)

for all
H\subset G
, the values
R([G/H])
and
N([G/H])
are isomorphic, and

(b)

writing
R_{K}^{H}\colon R([G/H])\rightarrow R([G/K])
for the contravariant functoriality and
N_{K}^{H}\colon N([G/K])\rightarrow N([G/H])
for the covariant functoriality,
R
and
N
satisfy the
double coset formula

R_{J}^{H}N_{K}^{H}(-)\simeq\sumop\displaylimits_{g\in[J\backslash H/K]}N_{H\cap gKg^{-1}}^{H}\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}\left(-\right)_{g}

where
(-)_{g}
denotes the covariant conjugation action and
[J\backslash G/K]
is the set of
double cosets
.

Let
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})
be the effective Burnside 1-category, whose objects are finite
G
-sets, whose morphisms
R_{XY}\colon X\rightarrow Y
are given by isomorphism classes of spans
X\leftarrow R_{XY}\rightarrow Y
, and whose composition is given by pullback of spans

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{XZ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{XY}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{YZ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

It is an observation due to Lindner
[
Lin76
]
that (semi)-Mackey functors valued in
{\mathcal{C}}
are equivalently given by product preserving functors

\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}.

This appears as a straightforward generalization of the Lawvere theory
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}})
for commutative monoids, so we will refer to semi-Mackey functors as
G
-commutative monoids
.

Moreover,
any

{\mathcal{C}}
admits a universal map from a semiadditive category, given by the forgetful functor
U\colon\mathrm{CMon}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
;
since
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})
possesses an identity-on-objects anti-involution, it is semiadditive, and so
U
induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G}),\mathrm{CMon}({\mathcal{C}}))\xrightarrow{\;\;\sim\;\;}\operatorname{Fun}^{\oplus}(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G}),{\mathcal{C}});

in fact, replacing
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})
with the effective Burnside
2-
category of
[
Bar14
]
(whose 2-cells are isomorphisms of spans),
{\mathcal{C}}
with an
\infty
-category, and interpreting
\mathrm{CMon}({\mathcal{C}})
as
{\math@bb{E}}_{\infty}
-monoids in
{\mathcal{C}}
, the semiadditivization result for
\mathrm{CMon}({\mathcal{C}})
still holds
[
GGN15
]
, and
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})
is still semiadditive.
Thus we are justified in making the following definition.

Definition
.

The
\infty
-category of
G
-commutative monoids in
{\mathcal{C}}
is the product-preserving functor
\infty
-category

\mathrm{CMon}_{G}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G}),{\mathcal{C}});

the
\infty
-category of small
G
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
is

\mathrm{Cat}_{G}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\mathrm{CMon}_{G}(\mathrm{Cat}).

This recovers the notion of
[
NS22
]
, which generalizes the notion of
[
HH16
]
.
Recall that we define
G
-
\infty
-categories to be categorical coefficient systems

\mathrm{Cat}_{G}\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{G}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{C}}\right);

the
[G/H]
-value of a
G
-
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
will be written
{\mathcal{C}}_{H}
, and the contravariant functoriality along
[G/K]\rightarrow[G/H]
will be written
\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}\colon{\mathcal{C}}_{H}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{K}
.

G
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
have underlying
G
-
\infty
-categories
{\mathcal{C}}
defined by the precomposition

{\mathcal{C}}\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{G}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}}\mathrm{Cat}.

Given a subgroup
H\subset G
and a finite
H
-set
S
, we will write the value of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
on
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}S
as
{\mathcal{C}}_{S}
, noting that there is a canonical equivalence
{\mathcal{C}}_{S}\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{C}}_{K}
.

We may induce the unique map of
H
-sets
S\rightarrow*_{H}
to
G
to construct a structure map
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}S\rightarrow[G/H]
,
3
3

3

See
[
Die09
]
for a discussion of induced
G
-sets.

and covariant functoriality yields a natural
S
-indexed tensor product
operation

\bigotimesop\displaylimits^{S}\colon{\mathcal{C}}_{S}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{H}.

We may induce the
orbit set
factorization
S\rightarrow\coprodop\displaylimits_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}*_{H}\rightarrow*_{H}
to yield a natural equivalence

\bigotimesop\displaylimits^{S}_{K}X_{K}\simeq\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}N_{K}^{H}X_{K}.

Similarly, contravariant functoriality yields an
S
-indexed diagonal

\Delta^{S}\colon{\mathcal{C}}_{H}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{S}
satisfying

\Delta^{S}X\simeq\left(\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}X\right)_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}.

This allows us to define
S
-indexed tensor power
of an object
X_{H}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{H}
by

X_{H}^{\otimes S}\coloneqq\bigotimesop\displaylimits^{S}\Delta^{S}X_{H}\simeq\bigotimesop\displaylimits^{S}_{K}\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}X_{H}\simeq\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}N_{K}^{H}\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}X_{H}.

Akin to the discrete case, these satisfy a double coset formula by functoriality under the composite span

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{g\in[J\backslash H/K]}G/(K\cap gJg^{-1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G/J}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G/K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G/J}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G/H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G/K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Example
.

Write
\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{G}
for the
G
-
\infty
-category with
H
-value
\left(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{G}\right)_{H}\coloneqq{\mathcal{S}}_{H}\simeq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{O}}_{H}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{S}})
the
\infty
-category of genuine
H
-equivariant spaces.
This possesses a
G
-symmetric monoidal structure
\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{G}^{G-\times}
whose
S
-ary tensor product is the
S
-indexed product

[
NS22
]
;
in particular,
{\mathcal{S}}_{H}
is a cartesian symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category and
N_{K}^{H}\simeq\mathrm{CoInd}_{K}^{H}\colon{\mathcal{S}}_{K}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}_{H}
is right adjoint to restriction.

Example
.

There is a
G
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\underline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{G}^{\otimes}
whose
H
-value
\left(\underline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{G}\right)_{H}\simeq\operatorname{Sp}_{H}
is the
\infty
-category of genuine
H
-spectra with norms given by the Hill-Hopkins-Ravanel norm
[
NS22
,
BH21
]
.

We are concerned with algebraic structures
inside

G
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories, which we will control with a version of Nardin-Shah’s
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Op}_{G}
of
G
-
\infty
-operads, which we simply call
G
-operads
.
Work of Barkan, Haugseng, and Steinebrunner
[
BHS22
]
identifies these with functors of
\infty
-categories
\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})
possessing cocartesian lifts over backwards maps and satisfying a pair Segal conditions, which we may summarize in two cases of interest:

(1)

in the case that the fibers
\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{-1}(S)
are contractible for all
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{G}
(i.e.
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}

has one color
), cocartesian lifts over the backwards maps
(S\leftarrow[G/H]=[G/H])_{[G/H]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}
furnish an equivalence

\operatorname{Map}^{T\rightarrow S}_{\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}}\left(iT,iS\right)\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{[G/H]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\operatorname{Map}^{T_{H}\rightarrow[G/H]}_{\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}}(iT_{H},i[G/H]),

where we set
T_{H}\coloneqq T\times_{S}[G/H]
and we write
iS
for the unique object of
\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{-1}(S)
;
4
4

4

Given a functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
, and
\psi\colon FX\rightarrow FY
a map in
{\mathcal{D}}
, we write
\operatorname{Map}^{\psi}_{F}(X,Y)\subset\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{C}}}(X,Y)
for the disjoint union of the connected components consisting of maps
\varphi\colon X\rightarrow Y
such that
F\varphi
is homotopic to
\psi
.

(2)

in the case that
\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}
is a cocartesian fibration,
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a
G
-operad if and only if it is the unstraightening of a
G
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.

These span a localizing subcategory
[
BHS22
, Cor 4.2.3]

(1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Op}_{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Cat}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}},}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{Op}_{G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

the latter denoting the non-full subcategory
\mathrm{Cat}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}\subset\mathrm{Cat}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})}
whose objects possess cocartesian lifts over backwards maps and whose morphisms preserve these cocartesian lifts.

Given
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
a one-color
G
-operad,
H\subset G
a subgroup, and
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{H}
a finite
H
-set, we write

{\mathcal{O}}(S)\coloneqq\operatorname{Map}^{\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}S\rightarrow[G/H]}_{\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}}(i\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}S,i[G/H])

for the
S
-ary structure space of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
.

Example
.

Let
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{G}
be a pullback-stable and core-full subcategory.
In
Section

2.2
we show that the subcategory
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})\subset\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{G})
presents a
G
-operad if and only if
I
is a weak indexing category in the sense of
[
Ste24a
]
, in which case we refer to the resulting
G
-operad as
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}
.
We refer to these together as the class of
weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads
.
These are identified by their structure spaces

{\mathcal{O}}(S)\simeq\begin{cases}*&\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}S\rightarrow[G/H]\in I;\\
\varnothing&\mathrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

An
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
is defined to be a map of
G
-operads
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
;
these posses an underlying
G
-object
X_{\bullet}
(extending canonically to a cocartesian section of
{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{G}^{\operatorname{op}}
, with canonical equivalences
X_{K}\simeq\operatorname{Res}^{H}_{K}X_{H}
) together with action maps

(2)

{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{H}}\left(X^{\otimes S}_{H},X_{H}\right)

for each subgroup
H\subset G
and finite
H
-set
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{H}
, suitably functorial and compatible with cocartesian lifts of backwards maps.
In fact, as in
[
NS22
]
, we may lift these to a
G
-
\infty
-category

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})
whose
H
-value consists of algebras over the restricted
H
-operad
:

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})_{H}\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{\operatorname{Res}_{H}^{G}{\mathcal{O}}}(\operatorname{Res}_{H}^{G}{\mathcal{C}}).

Example
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{G}^{G-\times}
.
Note that there is a natural equivalence

\left(\prodop\displaylimits^{S}_{K}X_{K}\right)^{H}\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\left(\mathrm{CoInd}_{K}^{H}X_{K}\right)^{H}\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}X^{K}_{K},

for each
S
-equivariant tuple
(X_{K})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{S}
, where
X^{H}=\operatorname{Map}^{H}(*,X)
is the
H
-equivariant genuine fixed points functor.
Thus we may compose
Eq.

2
with genuine fixed points to acquire an action map

{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow\operatorname{Map}\left(\prodop\displaylimits_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}X^{K},X^{H}\right);

in particular, we may view
{\mathcal{O}}([H/K])
as the
space of transfers
X^{K}\rightarrow X^{H}
prescribed to an
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra.

In particular,
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}
prescribes a contractible space of maps
\prodop\displaylimits_{[H/K]\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}X^{K}\rightarrow X^{H}
for all
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{H}
whose structure map
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}S\rightarrow[G/H]
lies in
I
;
indeed we will verify in forthcoming work
[
Ste24
]
that
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}
-algebras in
\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{G}^{G-\times}
are (homotopy-coherent) incomplete
G
-commutative monoids.

Summary of main results

Write
\underline{\Sigma}_{G}
for the
G
-space core of the
G
-
\infty
-category of finite
G
-sets
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{G}
;
write
\operatorname{Tot}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
for the functor taking a
G
-
\infty
-category to the total
\infty
-category of its corresponding cocartesian fibration.
We identify objects with
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G}
with pairs
(H,S)
where
(H)\subset G
is a conjugacy class and
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{H}
is a finite
H
-set.

Theorem A
.

There exists a monadic functor

\operatorname{sseq}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{G}^{\mathrm{oc}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G},{\mathcal{S}})

whose composite functor
\mathrm{Op}_{G}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{sseq}}\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G},{\mathcal{S}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}_{(H,S)}}{\mathcal{S}}
recovers
{\mathcal{O}}(S)
.

In parallel, Bonventre-Pereira developed a model category
s\mathrm{Op}_{G}^{\mathrm{oc}}
of
genuine
G
-operads
which is right-transferred along a monadic
underlying
G
-symmetric sequence
functor
U\colon s\mathrm{Op}_{G,*_{G}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Fun}}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G},\mathrm{sSet}_{\mathrm{Quillen}})

[
BP21
, Thm II]
.
5
5

5

When we say a model category
{\mathcal{C}}
is
right-transferred along
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
, we mean that
F
preserves and reflects weak equivalences and fibrations.

We refer to the associated
\infty
-category as
g\mathrm{Op}_{G}^{\mathrm{oc}}\coloneqq s\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{G}[\mathrm{weq}^{-1}]
.

Unwinding definitions, we will see that
\operatorname{sseq}
is total right derived from a functor of 1-categories out of Nardin-Shah’s model structure
[
NS22
]
which preserves and reflects weak equivalences between fibrant objects, and Bonventre’s
genuine operadic nerve

N^{\otimes}
satisfies
{\mathcal{P}}(S)\simeq\left(N^{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}\right)(S)
.
We conclude by two-out-of-three that

Corollary B
.

Bonventre’s genuine operadic nerve possesses a conservative total right derived functor of
\infty
-categories.

Moreover, in

Notation and conventions

We assume that the reader is familiar with the technology of higher category theory and higher algebra as developed in
[
HTT
]
and
[
HA
, § 2-3]
, though we encourage the reader to engage with such technologies via a “big picture” perspective akin to that of
[
Gep19
, § 1-2]
and
[
Hau23
, § 1-3]
.
We will generally use the term
replete subcategory inclusion
to refer to functors
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
whose core
F^{\simeq}\colon{\mathcal{C}}^{\simeq}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}^{\simeq}
is a summand inclusion and whose effect on mapping spaces
F\colon\operatorname{Map}(X,Y)\rightarrow\operatorname{Map}(FX,FY)
is a summand inclusion for each
X,Y\in{\mathcal{C}}
.

1.
Equivariant symmetric monoidal categories

In this section, we review and advance the equivariant
\infty
-category theory of
homotopical incomplete (semi)-Mackey functors
for a weak indexing system
I
, which we call
I
-commutative monoids
.
To that end, we begin in
Section

1.1
by reviewing our equivariant higher categorical setup.
We go on to cite and prove some basic facts about
I
-commutative monoids in
Section

1.2
.
In
Section

1.3
we then endow the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of
I
-commutative monoids with its
mode
symmetric monoidal structure, and prove that this is uniquely determined as a presentable symmetric monoidal structure by the free functor from coefficient systems;
we use this to identify the resulting symmetric monoidal structure with the
localized Day convolution structure
.
Following this, in
Section

1.4
we quickly develop a framework for
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
d
-categories.

1.1.
Recollections on
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories

We center on the following definition.

1.0
.

An
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{T}}
is

(1)

orbital
if the finite coproduct completion
{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}:={\mathcal{T}}^{\coprodop\displaylimits}
has all pullbacks, and

(2)

atomic orbital
if it is orbital and every map in
{\mathcal{T}}
possessing a section is an equivalence.

We view the setting of atomic orbital
\infty
-categories as a natural axiomatic home for higher algebra centered around the Burnside category (see
[
Nar16
, § 4]
), generalizing the orbit categories of a finite group.
The reader who is exclusively interested in equivariant homotopy theory is encouraged to assume every atomic orbital
\infty
-category is the orbit category of a family of subgroups of a finite group.

1.0
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be an
\infty
-category.
Then, a full subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-family
if whenever
V\in{\mathcal{F}}
and
W\rightarrow V
is a map, we have
W\in{\mathcal{F}}
.
8
8

8

These are named
families
after subconjugacy closed families of subgroups, which frequently occur in equivariant homotopy; these are referred to as
sieves
in
[
BH15
,
NS22
]
and
upwards-closed subcategories
in
[
Gla17
]
.

The poset of
{\mathcal{T}}
-families under inclusion is denoted
\mathrm{Fam}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.

Similarly, a full subcategory
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-cofamily
if its opposite
{\mathcal{F}}^{\operatorname{op}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
-family.

Temporarily fix
G
be a topological group, let
{\mathcal{S}}_{G}
be the
\infty
-category of
G
-spaces, and let
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{G}
be the full subcategory spanned by homogeneous
G
-spaces
[G/H]
, where
H\subset G
is a closed subgroup.

1.0
.

The full subcategory
BG\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
is a family, and the contractible full subcategory
\left\{[G/G]\right\}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
is a cofamily.
More generally, if
{\mathcal{T}}
is an
\infty
-category and
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
an object, then the full subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}_{\geq V}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
consisting of objects admitting a map to
V
is a family and the full subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}_{\leq V}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
of objects admitting a map from
V
is a cofamily.

1.0
.

The following are all atomic orbital
\infty
-categories (see
[
Ste24a
]
).

(1)

The full subcategory
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}^{fin}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
spanned by
[G/H]
for
H
finite.

(2)

The wide subcategory
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}^{f.i.}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
whose morphisms are projections
[G/K]\rightarrow[G/H]
for
K\subset H
finite index inclusion of closed subgroups.

(3)

X
a space, considered as an
\infty
-category.

(4)

P
a meet semilattice.

(5)

If
{\mathcal{T}}
is an atomic orbital
\infty
-category,
\mathrm{ho}(T)
.

(6)

If
{\mathcal{T}}
is an atomic orbital
\infty
-category,
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
a full subcategory satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

For all
U,W\in{\mathcal{F}}
and paths
U\rightarrow V\rightarrow W
in
{\mathcal{T}}
,
V\in{\mathcal{F}}
.

(b)

For all
U,W\in{\mathcal{F}}
and cospans
U\rightarrow V\leftarrow W
in
{\mathcal{T}}
, there is a span
U\leftarrow V^{\prime}\rightarrow V
in
{\mathcal{F}}
.

For instance,
{\mathcal{F}}
may be the intersection of a family and a cofamily whose connected components have weakly initial objects, such as
{\mathcal{T}}_{\leq V}
or
{\mathcal{T}}_{\geq V}
.

(7)

If
{\mathcal{T}}
is an atomic orbital
\infty
-category and
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, the
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{T}}_{/V}
.

In this section, we briefly summarize some relevant elements of parameterized and equivariant higher category theory in the setting of atomic orbital
\infty
-categories.
Of course, this theory has advanced far past that which is summarized here; for instance, further details can be found in the work of Barwick-Dotto-Glasman-Nardin-Shah
[
BDGNS16
,
BDGNS16a
,
Sha22
,
Sha23
,
Nar16
]
, Cnossen-Lenz-Linskens
[
CLL23
,
CLL23a
,
CLL24
,
LNP22
,
Lin24
]
, Hilman
[
Hil24
]
, and Martini-Wolf

1.1.1.
The
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of small
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories

We are motivated by the following.

1.0
.

Let
G
be a finite group,
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
a family, and
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
be the
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{F}}
-spaces, constructed e.g. by inverting
{\mathcal{F}}
-weak equivalences between topological
G
-spaces.
Then, a version of Elmendorf’s theorem
[
Elm83
]
for families
[
DK84
, Thm 3.1]
states that the
total
{\mathcal{F}}
-fixed points
functor yields an equivalence

{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}\simeq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{F}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{S}}).

We extend this via the following definition.

1.0
.

The
\infty
-category of small
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories
is

\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathrm{Cat}),

where
\mathrm{Cat}
is the
\infty
-category of small
\infty
-categories.
If
\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}
is the (very large)
\infty
-category of
arbitrary

\infty
-categories, then the
very large
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories
is

\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}\left({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}},\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}\right).

1.0
.

Fix
{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
We refer to the value of
{\mathcal{C}}
at
V\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
as the
V
-value category of
{\mathcal{C}}
, written as
{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
;
given
f\colon V\rightarrow W
, we refer to the associated functor as
restriction

\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{W}\colon{\mathcal{C}}_{W}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V}.

1.0
.

We show in
Section

2.1.1
that
\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is equivalently presented as
complete Segal objects
in the
\infty
-topos

(7)

{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{S}}).

1.0
.

The Grothendieck construction, imported to
\infty
-category theory as the straightening unstraightening equivalence in
[
HTT
, Thm 3.2.0.1]
, produces an equivalence

\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\simeq\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}},

the latter denoting the (non-full) subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}}
whose objects are cocartesian fibrations and whose morphisms are functors over
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
which preserve cocartesian arrows.
Under this identification, the fiber of
\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
over
V
is identified with the
V
-value
{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
and the restriction functors are identified with cocartesian transport, where
\operatorname{Tot}
denotes the total
\infty
-category of the unstraightening.

Given
{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}
a pair of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories, we may define the
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor category
to be the full subcategory

\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}}\left({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}\right)\subset\operatorname{Fun}_{/{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})

consisting of functors over
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
which preserve cocartesian lifts of the structure maps.

1.0
.

For any object
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, the forgetful functor
\left({\mathcal{T}}_{/V}\right)^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
is a cocartesian fibration classified by the representable presheaf
\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(-,V)
.
We refer to the associated
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category as
\underline{V}
.
This is covariantly functorial in
V
, since postcomposition yields functors
f_{!}\colon{\mathcal{T}}_{/V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}_{/W}
for all maps
f\colon V\rightarrow W
.

The representable
{\mathcal{T}}
-categories are particularly nice in the atomic orbital setting.

Proposition 1.0

(
[
NS22
, Prop 2.5.1]
)
.

If an atomic orbital
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{T}}
has a terminal object, then it is a 1-category;
in particular,
{\mathcal{T}}_{/V}
is a 1-category.
9
9

9

To see this, note that this is equivalent to the condition that the (split) diagonal map
U\rightarrow U\times U
is an equivalence, which follows from the atomic assumption.

1.0
.

Section

1.1.1
provides an easy verification that
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
is not atomic orbital when
\dim G>0
;

{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
has a terminal object
[G/G]
, but it is not a 1-category, as
\operatorname{End}([G/e])\simeq G
is not discrete.

These play an important role in equivariant higher category theory.

1.0
.

Given
{\mathcal{C}}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category, we define the
restricted

{\mathcal{T}}_{/V}
-category by

\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\coloneqq{\mathcal{C}}_{\underline{V}}\coloneqq{\mathcal{C}}\times_{{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}}\left({\mathcal{T}}_{/V}\right)^{\operatorname{op}}.

Proposition 1.0

(
[
BDGNS16a
, Thm 9.7]
)
.

\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
has exponential objects
\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
classified by the functor

V\mapsto\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{/V}}\left({\mathcal{C}}_{\underline{V}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{V}}\right).

We refer to monomorphisms
10
10

10

Following
[
HTT
, § 5.5.6]
, we refer to a morphism
X\rightarrow Y
in
{\mathcal{C}}

The terminal

\infty

.
The poset of

The

There is an equivalence
*_{{\mathcal{T}}}=\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}*\in\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

We may additionally construct the

\infty

\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(*,{\mathcal{C}}),

For instance, if
{\mathcal{T}}

more generally, this implies that

(or the limit of
{\mathcal{C}}
viewed as a
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}

Defining the

, the adjunction between limits and diagonals immediately yields the following.

Proposition 1.0
.

The functor
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is left adjoint to
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
.

Using this adjunction, given

\infty

\mathrm{Coeff}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}\coloneqq\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}\simeq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{C}});

then, we have

{\mathcal{S}}_{G}\simeq\mathrm{Coeff}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{G}}{\mathcal{S}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}^{V}_{W}{\mathcal{C}}}

In many cases, these are familiar;

Proposition 1.1

(
[

if and only if for all

is a limit diagram for

.

Similarly, indexed (co)limits in coefficient systems may be converted into non-equivariant colimits.

\displaystyle\left(\underline{\lim}F\right)^{V}

\displaystyle\simeq\lim\left(\operatorname{Tot}^{V}{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Coeff}^{V}({\mathcal{D}})\xrightarrow{(-)^{V}}{\mathcal{D}}\right).

1.1
.

1.1.3.
Parameterized Kan extensions

Fix a (not necessarily commuting) triangle of
{\mathcal{T}}
-functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}

and
x\in{\mathcal{D}}_{V}
a
V
-object.
Assume
{\mathcal{D}}
has a final
V
-object.
We define the composite
V
-functor

G^{x}\colon\left({\mathcal{C}}_{\underline{V}}^{/x}\right)^{\underline{\triangleright}}\xrightarrow{\;\;\;\varphi\;\;\;}\left({\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{V}}^{/x}\right)^{\underline{\triangleright}}\xrightarrow{\left(H^{\prime},\pi\right)}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{V}}^{/x}\times\Delta^{1}\xrightarrow{\;\;\;H\;\;\;}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{V}}\xrightarrow{\;\;\;G\;\;\;}{\mathcal{E}}_{\underline{V}}

where
H^{\prime}
takes the cone point to a
\underline{V}
-final object and
H
is adjunct to the evident map
{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{V}}^{/x}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ar}({\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{V}})
.

Theorem/Definition 1.2

(
[
Sha22
, Thm 2.13]
)
.

The pullback
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor

\varphi^{*}\colon\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{E}})

has a partially defined left adjoint
\varphi_{!}
whose values are uniquely characterized by the property that
\left(\varphi_{!}F\right)^{x}
is a
V
-colimit diagram for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
x\in{\mathcal{D}}_{V}
. We call this the
{\mathcal{T}}
-left Kan extension of
F
along
\varphi
.

For instance,
{\mathcal{T}}
-left Kan extensions along
{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow*_{{\mathcal{T}}}
are precisely
{\mathcal{T}}
-colimits.
More generally, we will view the above colimit formula via the shorthand

\phi_{!}F(y)=\underline{\operatorname{colim}}_{\varphi(x)\rightarrow y}F(x).

Of course,
{\mathcal{T}}
-right Kan extensions are defined dually, and denoted
\varphi_{*}
.

1.1.4.
Parameterized adjunctions

Related to indexed colimits, there is a theory of
parameterized adjunctions

1.2
.

A
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
L\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
is
left adjoint
to
R\colon{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
if the associated functors
L_{V}\colon{\mathcal{C}}_{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{V}
are left adjoint to
R_{V}\colon{\mathcal{D}}_{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
.

These are the same as
relative adjunctions
over
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
by
[
HA
, Prop 7.3.2.1]
;

{\mathcal{T}}
-left adjoints strongly preserve small
{\mathcal{T}}
-colimits and
{\mathcal{T}}
-right adjoints strongly preserve small
{\mathcal{T}}
-limits
[
Hil24
, Thm 3.1.10]
, and they satisfy a parameterized version of the adjoint functor theorem
[
Hil24
, Thm 6.2.1]
.

1.2
.

By
[
Sha22
, Rmk 5.4]
,
{\mathcal{T}}
-limits form a (partially defined) right
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjoint
\underline{\lim}\colon\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(K,{\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
to the “diagonal”
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
\Delta^{K}\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(K,{\mathcal{C}})
, which itself may be computed as precomposition along the canonical
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
K\rightarrow*_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.

As observed in
[
Ste24a
]
, diagonals are functorial, so composing right adjoints to the diagonal of the “orbit set” factorization
\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S\rightarrow\coprodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}V\rightarrow V

thus yields natural equivalences

(8)

\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{U}^{S}X_{U}\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\mathrm{Ind}_{U}^{V}X_{U};\hskip 50.0pt\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{U}^{S}X_{U}\simeq\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\mathrm{CoInd}_{U}^{V}X_{U}.

We may construct many more
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjunctions using
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}
:

Lemma 1.2
.

Suppose
L\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{D}}\colon R
is an adjunction of
\infty
-categories.
Then,

\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}L\colon\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}\rightleftarrows\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{D}}\colon\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}R

is an adjunction of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

This follows from the fiberwise description of
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}(-)
;
indeed, the
V
-values

L_{*}:\operatorname{Fun}\left(({\mathcal{T}}_{/V})^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{C}}\right)\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Fun}\left(({\mathcal{T}}_{/V})^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{D}}\right):R_{*}

are adjoint.
∎

1.2
.

We may use
Section

1.1.4
to realize the full
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
-spaces whose fixed points are
d
-connected or
d
-truncated as (co)localizing
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},\geq d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

We will use this line of thought to understand
truncatedness and connectedness of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads and
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal categories
.

1.2
.

By
Section

1.1.4
, the
classifying space and core
double adjunction
(-)_{\simeq}\dashv\iota\dashv(-)^{\simeq}
yields a double
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(-)_{\simeq}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(-)^{\simeq}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Additionally, we can make genuine adjunction
non-genuine
using
[
HA
, Prop 7.3.2.1]
.

Proposition 1.2
.

If
L\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{D}}\colon R
are adjoint
{\mathcal{T}}
-functors, then
\operatorname{Tot}L\colon\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{C}}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{D}}\colon\operatorname{Tot}R
and
\Gamma L\colon\Gamma{\mathcal{C}}\rightleftarrows\Gamma{\mathcal{D}}\colon\Gamma R
are adjoint pairs.

Proof.

The adjunction on
\operatorname{Tot}
is
[
HA
, Prop 7.3.2.1]
, and it induces an adjunction

\operatorname{Tot}L_{*}\colon\operatorname{Fun}_{/{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{T}},\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{C}})\mathrel{\begin{subarray}{c}\xrightarrow{\rule{14.70003pt}{0.0pt}}\\[-3.87495pt]
\xleftarrow{\rule{14.70003pt}{0.0pt}}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Fun}_{/{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{T}},\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{D}})\colon\operatorname{Tot}R_{*},

which restricts to the full subcategories of cocartesian sections, and hence yields an adjunction

\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}L\colon\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\begin{subarray}{c}\xrightarrow{\rule{14.70003pt}{0.0pt}}\\[-3.87495pt]
\xleftarrow{\rule{14.70003pt}{0.0pt}}\end{subarray}}\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{D}}\colon\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}.\qed.

We will need the following lemma and proposition later.

Lemma 1.2
.

Suppose a
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
has
F_{V}:{\mathcal{C}}_{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{V}
conservative for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
;
then,
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}F
is conservative.

Proof.

Suppose
f_{\bullet}:X_{\bullet}\rightarrow Y_{\bullet}
is a map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
, i.e. a natural transformation of cocartesian sections of
\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
.
Then,
f_{\bullet}
is an equivalence if and only if
f_{V}
is an equivalence for each
V
;
by conservativity of
F_{V}
, this is true if and only if
F_{v}f_{v}
is an equivalence for each
V
, i.e. if and only if
Ff_{\bullet}
is an equivalence, so
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}F
is conservative.
∎

Proposition 1.2
.

Suppose
L\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{D}}\colon R
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjunction such that
R_{V}
is monadic for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
;
Then,
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}R\colon\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}
is monadic.

Proof.

We verify that
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}R
satisfies the conditions of the
\infty
-categorical Barr-Beck theorem
[
HA
, Thm 4.7.3.5(c)]
.
First, by
Sections

1.1.4
and
1.1.4
,
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}R
is a conservative right adjoint.
Second, note that a simplicial object
Z_{\bullet}(-)
in
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{D}}
corresponds to a family of simplicial objects
Z_{V}(-)
in
{\mathcal{D}}_{V}
, and a
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}R
-splitting of
Z_{\bullet}(-)
corresponds with a restriction-stable family of
R_{V}
-splittings of
Z_{V}(-)
.
Thus
R_{V}
creates a colimit of
Z_{V}
for all
V
, and the resulting cocartesian section creates a colimit for
Z_{\bullet}
, i.e.
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}R
createss
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}R
-split simplicial colimits, so
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}R
is monadic by
[
HA
, Thm 4.7.3.5(c)]
.
∎

1.1.5.
Language in the case
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}

When
G
is a finite group, the category
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
has objects the homogeneous
G
-sets
[G/H]
and morphisms the
G
-equivariant maps
[G/K]\rightarrow[G/H
];
tracking the image of the identity, the hom set from
[G/K]
to
[G/H]
may alternatively be presented as

\operatorname{Hom}([G/K],[G/H])\simeq\frac{\left\{a\in G\mid aKa^{-1}\subset H\right\}}{a\sim b\;\;\text{ when }\;\;ab^{-1}\in K}

(see e.g.
[
Die09
, Prop 1.3.1]
for details).
In particular, the endomorphism monoid of
[G/K]
is the Weyl group
W_{G}H=N_{G}(H)/H
.
Using this, one may see that when
G
is a finite group, the map
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{H}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{G,/(G/H)}
is an equivalence of categories.
Thus we may set the following notation without creating clashes.

1.2
.

In the setting that
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
, we use the following notation:

(1)

we refer to
\underline{\left[G/H\right]}
as
\underline{H}
;

(2)

we refer to
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
-
\infty
-categories as
G
-
\infty
-categories and
\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{G}}
as
\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{G}
;
we refer to
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
-spaces as
G
-spaces and
\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{G}}
as
\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{G}
;

(3)

we refer to
{\mathcal{C}}_{[G/H]}
as
{\mathcal{C}}_{H}
and
\operatorname{Res}_{[G/K]}^{[G/H]}
as
\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}
;
the superscripts and subscripts of
\mathrm{Ind}
,
\mathrm{CoInd}
,
\Gamma
,
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}
,
\star
,
(-)^{(-,{\mathcal{T}})/}
, and
*
are determined similarly.

(4)

we refer to
\coprodop\displaylimits_{[H/K]}^{S}X_{K}
as
\coprodop\displaylimits_{K}^{S}X_{K}
, and similar for
\prodop\displaylimits^{S}
.

1.2.
I
-commutative monoids

Following
[
Bar14
]
, we say that an
adequite triple
is the data of two core-preserving wide subcategories
{\mathcal{X}}_{b}\subset{\mathcal{X}}\supset{\mathcal{X}}_{f}
of an
\infty
-category such that cospans
X\xrightarrow{\varphi_{f}}Y\xleftarrow{\varphi_{b}}Z
satisfying
\varphi_{f}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{f}
and
\varphi_{b}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{b}
lift to pullback diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{Y}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{b}}

satisfying
\psi_{b}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{b}
and
\psi_{f}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{f}
.
Given an adequate triple
{\mathcal{X}}_{b}\subset{\mathcal{X}}\supset{\mathcal{X}}_{f}
, we define the
span category
to be

\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{X}})\coloneqq A^{eff}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{X}}_{b},{\mathcal{X}}_{f}),

the latter denoting the effective Burnside category of
[
Bar14
]
.
In particular, the objects of
\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{X}})
are precisely those of
{\mathcal{X}}
, and the morphisms from
X
to
Z
are the spans
X\xleftarrow{\varphi_{b}}Y\xrightarrow{\varphi_{f}}Z
with
\varphi_{b}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{b}
and
\varphi_{f}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{f}
, with composition defined by taking pullbacks.

12
12

12

Those readers more familiar with
[
EH23
]
may note that this specializes to the notion of a
span pair
, when backwards maps are
{\mathcal{X}}_{b}={\mathcal{X}}
, in which case
\operatorname{Span}_{f}({\mathcal{X}})
recovers that of
[
EH23
]
, and hence lifts to an
(\infty,2)
-category with a universal property that we will not use.

1.2
.

For
{\mathcal{T}}
an orbital
\infty
-category and
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
a pullback-stable wide subcategory with
I^{\simeq}\simeq{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\simeq}
,
{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}={\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\hookleftarrow I
is an adequate triple;
write

\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}):=\operatorname{Span}_{all,I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}).

More generally, if there exists some full subcategory
{\mathcal{C}}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
such that
I\subset{\mathcal{C}}
is a pullback-stable wide subcategory with
I^{\simeq}\subset{\mathcal{C}}^{\simeq}
, we write

\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}):=\operatorname{Span}_{all,I}({\mathcal{C}}).

1.2
.

Even when
{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is a 1-category (i.e.
{\mathcal{T}}
is a 1-category),
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
will seldom be a 1-category;
indeed, in this case,
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is a
2
-category whose 2-cells are the isomorphisms of spans

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

In this subsection, we review the cartesian algebraic theory
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
corepresents, called
I
-commutative monoids
.
We will find that, in the same way that
\mathrm{CMon}
is easily characterized via
semiadditivity
(c.f.
[
GGN15
]
),
\mathrm{CMon}_{I}
is easily characterized via
I
-semiadditivity
.
Little of this subsection is original; instead, the results concerning
I
-commutative monoids form a slight generalization of
[
Nar16
]
and a massive specialization of
[
CLL24
]
, and the results concerning weak indexing systems are largely review of
[
Ste24a
]
.

1.2.1.
Weak indexing systems

We begin by briefly reviewing the setting of
weak indexing systems
introduced in
[
Ste24a
]
, which we view as the combinatorial context for the intersection of category theoretic and algebraic notions of
I
-commutative monoids.

1.2
.

A
{\mathcal{T}}
-weak indexing category
is a subcategory
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
satisfying the following conditions:

(IC-a)

(restrictions)
I
is stable under arbitrary pullbacks in
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
;

(IC-b)

(segal condition)
T\rightarrow S
and
T^{\prime}\rightarrow S
are both in
I
if and only if
T\sqcup T^{\prime}\rightarrow S\sqcup S^{\prime}
is in
I
; and

(IC-c)

(
\Sigma_{{\mathcal{T}}}
-action) if
S\in I
, then all automorphisms of
S
are in
I
.

A
{\mathcal{T}}
-weak indexing system
is a full
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}\subset\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
satisfying the following conditions:

(IS-a)

whenever the
V
-value
{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}\coloneqq\left(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}\right)_{V}
is nonempty, we have
*_{V}\in\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I,V}
; and

(IS-b)

\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}\subset\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is closed under
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}
-indexed coproducts.

1.2
.

By a basic inductive argument, condition (IC-b) is equivalent to the condition that
S\rightarrow T
is in
I
if and only if
T_{U}=T\times_{S}U\rightarrow U
is in
I
for all
U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)
;
in particular,
I
is determined by its slice categories over
orbits
.

We denote the
I
-admissible sets
by
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{Set}}_{I}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
as in
Section

1.1.2
.
This is a full
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory.

1.2
.

By
Section

1.2.1
, in the presence of
Item

(IC-b)
,
Item

(IC-a)
is equivalent to the condition that for all Cartesian diagrams in
{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

(9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\times_{V}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

with
U,V\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
\alpha\in I
, we have
\alpha^{\prime}\in I
.

Inspired by
Sections

1.2.1
and
1.2.1
, in
[
Ste24a
, Thm A]
we prove the following.

Proposition 1.2
.

The assignment
I\mapsto\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}
implements an equivalence between the posets of
{\mathcal{T}}
-weak indexing categories and
{\mathcal{T}}
-weak indexing systems.

We additionally recall the following conditions, which may equivalently be restated for weak indexing categories by
[
Ste24a
, Thm A]
.
In view of
[
Ste24a
, § 2.4]
, we encourage the reader to think primarily of
unitality
.

1.2
.

We say that
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}

(i)

has one color if for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, we have
{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}\neq\varnothing
,

(ii)

is almost essentially unital if for all non-contractible
V
-sets
S\sqcup S^{\prime}\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}
, we have
S,S^{\prime}\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}
,

(iii)

is unital if it has one color and for all
V
-sets
S\sqcup S^{\prime}\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}
, we have
S,S^{\prime}\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}
, and

(iv)

is an
indexing system
if the subcategory
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I,V}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
is closed under finite coproducts for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
.

These lie in a diagram of embedded sub-posets

\mathrm{Index}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\mathrm{w}\mathrm{Index}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{uni}}\subset\mathrm{w}\mathrm{Index}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{aE\mathrm{uni}}\subset\mathrm{w}\mathrm{Index}_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

If a weak indexing category
I
corresponds with a weak indexing system satisfying property
P
, we say that
I
satisfies property
P
;
if
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}
is an indexing system, we say
I
is an indexing category.
When
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
,
{\mathcal{T}}
-indexing systems and indexing categories recover the notions given the same name in
[
BH15
,
BH18
]
(see
[
Ste24a
]
).
Some useful invariants of these include

(10)

\begin{split}c(I)&\coloneqq\left\{V\in{\mathcal{T}}\mid*_{V}\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}\right\};\\
\upsilon(I)&\coloneqq\left\{V\in{\mathcal{T}}\mid\varnothing_{V}\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}\right\};\\
\nabla(I)&\coloneqq\left\{V\in{\mathcal{T}}\mid 2\cdot*_{V}\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}\right\}.\end{split}

These are each families
[
Ste24a
, § 1.2]
, which we call the families of
colors
,
units
, and
fold maps
in
I
.

We will import these into the setting of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads in
Section

2.2
through
weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads
, which play an important structural role in
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
Narrowly, this role comes down to the fact that indexed coproducts appear as the arities of
compositions
of indexed operations, so weak indexing systems occur as the possible “arity supports” that
{\mathcal{T}}
-equivariant algebraic theories can have, so long as they possess identity operations and they allows for the formation of composite operations.

Moreover, in the setting of indexing systems, it is typical to make frequent reductions of
S
-ary operations to
[H/K]
-ary operations and binary operations.
In the setting of weak indexing systems, we say that a
sparse
V
-set
is a
V
-set of the form

\epsilon\cdot*_{V}\sqcup W_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup W_{n}

where
\epsilon\in\left\{0,1\right\}
and there exist no maps
W_{i}\rightarrow W_{j}
over
V
for
i\neq j
.
The relevant generation statement is the following.

Proposition 1.2

(
[
Ste24a
, § 3.1]
)
.

Suppose
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I},\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{J}
are weak indexing systems.

(1)

If
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}
is almost essentially unital then
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}\subset\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{J}
if and only if
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{J}
contains all sparse
I
-admissible
V
-sets.

(2)

If
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}
is an indexing system then
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}\subset\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{J}
if and only if for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
,
{\math@bb{F}}_{J,V}
contains
\varnothing_{V},2\cdot*_{V}
, and all transitive
I
-admissible
V
-sets.

1.2.2.
Indexed semiadditivity

One central source of weak indexing categories is
indexed semiadditivity
, which only makes sense to evaluate in the pointed setting.

1.2
.

Given
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-family, we say that
{\mathcal{D}}
is
{\mathcal{F}}
-pointed
if
{\mathcal{D}}_{V}
is pointed for all
V\in{\mathcal{F}}
.

Given
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
a finite
V
-set with a distinguished orbit
W\subset S
,
{\mathcal{D}}
a
{\mathcal{T}}_{\leq V}
-pointed
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category admitting
S
-indexed products and coproducts, and
(X_{U})\in{\mathcal{D}}_{S}
a
S
-tuple in
{\mathcal{D}}
,
[
Nar16
, Cons 5.2]
constructs a map

\chi_{W}\colon\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\coprodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}X_{U}\rightarrow X_{W}

by distinguishing a “diagonal”
X_{W}
-summand on the left hand side and dictating the map to be the indentity on this summand and 0 elsewhere;
then, the
norm map

\mathrm{Nm}_{S}\colon\coprodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}X_{U}\rightarrow\prodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}X_{W}

has projected map
\coprodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}X_{U}\rightarrow\mathrm{CoInd}_{W}^{V}X_{W}
adjunct to
\chi_{W}
.

1.2
.

Given
{\mathcal{D}}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category and
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
a finite
V
-set, we say that
S
is
{\mathcal{D}}
-ambidextrous
if
{\mathcal{D}}
admits
S
-indexed products and coproducts, is
{\mathcal{T}}_{\leq V}
-pointed, and for all
(X_{U})\in{\mathcal{D}}_{S}
, the norm map is an equivalence

\coprodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}X_{U}\xrightarrow{\sim}\prodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}X_{U}.

Given
I
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-weak indexing category, we say that
{\mathcal{D}}
is
I
-semiadditive
if
S
is
{\mathcal{D}}
-ambidextrous for all
S\in\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}
.

1.2
.

We’ve given an elementary presentation of this notion;
this has been generalized to encapsulate Hopkins-Lurie’s
higher semiadditivity
in
[
CLL24
]
(see Example 3.37 there).
In particular, we find that
T\rightarrow S
is
{\mathcal{D}}
-ambidextrous in the sense of
[
CLL24
]
if and only if the
U
-set
T\times_{S}U
is
{\mathcal{D}}
-ambidextrous for all orbits
U\subset S
, so we adpot their language for
ambidextrous maps
.
In particular, by
[
Cno23
, Prop 3.13,Prop 3.16]
, ambidextrous maps are closed under composition and base change.

Given
{\mathcal{D}}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category, we define the
semiadditive locus

s({\mathcal{D}})=\left\{f\colon T\rightarrow S\,\mid\,f\text{ is }{\mathcal{D}}\text{-ambidextrous}\right\}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

This is closed under composition by
Section

1.2.2
;
furthermore, it’s clear that an equivalence
T\simeq S
is
{\mathcal{D}}
-ambidextrous if and only if
{\mathcal{D}}
is
{\mathcal{T}}_{\leq V}
-pointed, so
s({\mathcal{D}})\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is a subcategory satisfying
Item

(IC-c)
.
In fact, we may say more.

Proposition 1.2
.

s({\mathcal{D}})
is a weak indexing category and
{\mathcal{D}}
is
I
-semiadditive if and only if
I\leq s({\mathcal{D}})
.

Proof.

By
Sections

1.2.1
and
1.2.2
,
s({\mathcal{D}})
satisfies
Item

(IC-b)
.
In fact, by
Section

1.2.2
, ambidextrous maps are closed under base change, i.e.
s({\mathcal{D}})
satisfies
Item

(IC-a)
.
We’re left with verifying that
{\mathcal{D}}
is
I
-semiadditive if and only if
I\leq s({\mathcal{D}})
, but this follows immediately by unwinding definitions.
∎

By
[
Ste24a
]
, the poset
\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
has joins, which we write as
-\vee-
.
The following is immediate.

Corollary 1.2
.

{\mathcal{D}}
is
I\vee J
-semiadditive if and only if it is
I
-semiadditive and
J
-semiadditive.

From
Section

1.2.1
, we acquire a proof of a familiar corollary:
in the setting of indexing categories,
I
-semiadditivity is a combination of fiberwise semiadditivity and
I
-admissible Wirthmüller isomorphisms.

Corollary 1.2
.

Let
I
be an almost essentially unital weak indexing category and
{\mathcal{D}}
a
c(I)
-pointed
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category.
Then,

(1)

{\mathcal{D}}
is
I
-semiadditive if and only if all sparse
V
-sets are
{\mathcal{D}}
-ambidextrous.

(2)

If
I
is an indexing category, then
{\mathcal{D}}
is
I
-semiadditive if and only if
{\mathcal{D}}_{V}
is semiadditive for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
and for all maps of orbits
U\rightarrow V
in
I
and objects
X\in{\mathcal{D}}_{U}
, the norm map

\mathrm{Ind}_{U}^{V}X\rightarrow\mathrm{CoInd}_{U}^{V}X

is an equivalence.

1.2.3.
I
-commutative monoids as the
I
-semiadditive completion

Let
\mathrm{Trip}^{\mathrm{adeq}}\subset\operatorname{Fun}(\bullet\rightarrow\bullet\leftarrow\bullet,\mathrm{Cat})
be the full subcategory spanned by adequate triples.
By definition
[
Bar14
, Def 3.6]
,
\operatorname{Span}_{-,-}(-)
forms a functor
\mathrm{Trip}^{\mathrm{adeq}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
.
Fix
I
a one-color weak indexing category.
Write
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{V}\coloneqq\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/V}\simeq\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{/V}}
and let
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{I}\subset\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
be the wide subcategory whose
V
-value is
\left(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{I}\right)_{V}\coloneqq I_{V}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{V}\simeq{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/V}
is the wide subcategory of maps whose underlying map in
{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
lies in
I
.

The wide
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory inclusion
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{I}\subset\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is fiberwise given by a (one object) weak indexing category
[
Ste24a
, § 2.1]
, so in particular, this yields a functor
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Trip}^{\mathrm{adeq}}
(c.f.
[
CLL24
, § 4.1]
).
We use this to define the composite
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor

\underline{\operatorname{Span}}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\colon{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\xrightarrow{(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{I})}\mathrm{Trip}^{\mathrm{adeq}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Span}}\mathrm{Cat}.

1.2
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category admitting
I
-indexed products, then the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of
I
-commutative monoids in
{\mathcal{C}}
is

\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{I-\times}\left(\underline{\operatorname{Span}}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}}\right).

The
\infty
-category of
I
-commutative monoids
is
\mathrm{CMon}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{I-\times}\left(\underline{\operatorname{Span}}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}}\right)
.

1.2
.

We say that a
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
is the
I
-semiadditive completion of
{\mathcal{C}}
if
{\mathcal{D}}
is
I
-semiadditive and for all
I
-semiadditive
{\mathcal{T}}
-categories
{\mathcal{E}}
, postcomposition along
F
yields an equivalence

\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}^{I-\times}({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}^{I-\times}({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{C}}).

Write
\mathrm{Cat}^{I-\times}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
for the non-full subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories with
I
-indexed products and
I
-product preserving functors;
write
\iota\colon\mathrm{Cat}^{I-\oplus}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\mathrm{Cat}^{I-\times}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
for the full subcategory spanned by
I
-semiadditive
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories.
The
I
-semiadditive completion
is, if it exists, the unit of a partially defined adjunction whose right adjoint is
\iota
.
In fact, it does exist, by the following fundamental theorem.
13
13

13

To see that the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
of
[
CLL24
]
agrees with ours, apply
[
CLL24
, Lem 4.7]
.

Theorem 1.3

(
[
CLL24
, Thm B]
)
.

U\colon\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
is the
I
-semiadditive completion.

1.2.4.
Commutative monoids in
{\mathcal{T}}
-objects

Let
I^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
be the minimal indexing category
[
Ste24a
]
.

1.3
.

I^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
-indexed products are precisely
trivially
indexed products;
by
Section

1.1.2
the
I_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\infty}
-indexed product preserving functors are precisely the fiberwise product-preserving
{\mathcal{T}}
-functors.
Furthermore, a
{\mathcal{T}}
-category is
I_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\infty}
-semiadditive if and only if, for each
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, the
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
is semiadditive.
Thus we have equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{I^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{T}}}-\times}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Coeff}^{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\times}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{I^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{T}}}-\oplus}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Coeff}^{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\oplus}),

compatible with the inclusions.

Sections

1.1.4
and
1.2.4
directly imply that the
I^{\infty}
-semiadditive closure satisfies

\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\left({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{Cat}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{CMon}}\mathrm{Cat}^{\oplus}\right);

Cnossen-Lenz-Linsken’s semiadditive closure theorem (i.e.
Theorem

1.3
) then yields the following.

Corollary 1.3
.

There is a canonical equivalence
\mathrm{CMon}_{I^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\mathrm{CMon}\left(\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}\right)
.

1.2.5.
I
-commutative monoids in
\infty
-categories

We recall a special case of Cnossen-Lenz-Linsken’s Mackey functor theorem.

Theorem 1.4

(
[
CLL24
, Thm C]
)
.

For every presentable
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
, there are canonical equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{CMon}_{I}(\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}\left(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}}\right);

\displaystyle\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}(\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))_{V}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}\left(\operatorname{Span}_{I_{V}}({\math@bb{F}}_{V}),{\mathcal{C}}\right).

Furthermore, given a map
f\colon V\rightarrow W
, the associated restriction functor

\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{W}\colon\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Span}_{I_{W}}({\math@bb{F}}_{W}),{\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Span}_{I_{V}}({\math@bb{F}}_{V}),{\mathcal{C}})

is given by precomposition along
\operatorname{Span}(\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{W}(-))
.

This motivates us to make the following definition.

1.4
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is an
\infty
-category with finite products, then the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of
I
-commutative monoids in
{\mathcal{C}}
is

\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}(\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})).

Similar to the case of
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}
, this construction is compatible with adjunctions.

Lemma 1.4
.

Let
I\subset{\mathcal{T}}
be a pullback-stable wide subcategory of an orbital
\infty
-category.

(1)

If
f:{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
is a product-preserving functor, then postcomposition yields a
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor

f_{*}\colon\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}{\mathcal{D}}.

(2)

If
L:{\mathcal{C}}\rightleftarrows:R
is an adjunction whose right adjoint
R
is product preserving, then

L_{*}\colon\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\begin{subarray}{c}\xrightarrow{\rule{14.70003pt}{0.0pt}}\\[-3.87495pt]
\xleftarrow{\rule{14.70003pt}{0.0pt}}\end{subarray}}\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}{\mathcal{D}}\colon R_{*}

is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjunction.

Proof.

(1) follows by noting that
f_{*}
exists since
f
is product preserving, and it is compatible with restriction because postcomposition and precomposition commute.
(2) follows by noting that the associated functors

L_{*}\colon\left(\mathrm{CMon}_{I}{\mathcal{C}}\right)_{V}\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}\left(\operatorname{Span}_{I_{V}}({\math@bb{F}}_{V}),{\mathcal{C}}\right)\mathrel{\begin{subarray}{c}\xrightarrow{\rule{14.70003pt}{0.0pt}}\\[-3.87495pt]
\xleftarrow{\rule{14.70003pt}{0.0pt}}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}\left(\operatorname{Span}_{I_{V}}({\math@bb{F}}_{V}),{\mathcal{D}}\right)=\left(\mathrm{CMon}_{I}{\mathcal{D}})_{V}\right):R_{*}

are adjoint.
∎

We may unpack the structure of
I
-commutative monoids more using the following.

1.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an
\infty
-category,
X\in\mathrm{CMon}_{I}{\mathcal{C}}
be an
I
-commutative monoid,
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
be an orbit, and
\iota_{V}\colon{\math@bb{F}}\rightarrow{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
the finite coproduct-preserving functor sending
*\mapsto V
.
Then, the
V
-value
is the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CMon}_{I}{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CMon}_{I\times_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\iota-V}{\math@bb{F}}}({\mathcal{C}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}(\operatorname{Span}_{I\times_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\iota_{V}}{\math@bb{F}}}({\math@bb{F}}),{\mathcal{C}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{V}^{*}}

In particular,
I
is an indexing category and
X
is an
I
-commutative monoid,
X_{V}
is a commutative monoid in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

1.4
.

Fix
X\in\mathrm{CMon}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
and
f\colon V\rightarrow W
a map in
I
.
There exists a natural transformation
\alpha_{f}\colon\iota_{V}\rightarrow\iota_{W}
whose value on
n
is the copower map
n\cdot V\rightarrow n\cdot W
;
this induces a natural transformation
N_{V}^{W}\colon(-)_{V}\implies(-)_{W}
, which we refer to as the
norm map
.

1.2.6.
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories

We refer to

\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{I}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}\mathrm{Cat}

as the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
, and write
\mathrm{Cat}_{I}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\mathrm{CMon}_{I}\mathrm{Cat}
.
In the case
I={\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, we refer to these simply as
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
and write
\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}^{\otimes}
and
\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\mathrm{Cat}_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}^{\otimes}
.

1.4
.

Suppose
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}
.
Associated with the structure map
\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S\rightarrow V
we have functors

\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U}^{S}:{\mathcal{C}}_{S}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V},\hskip 40.0pt\Delta^{S}:{\mathcal{C}}_{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{S}

called the
S
-indexed tensor product and
S
-indexed diagonal
.
We refer to the composite
(-)^{\otimes S}:{\mathcal{C}}_{V}\xrightarrow{\Delta^{S}}{\mathcal{C}}_{S}\xrightarrow{\otimes_{U}^{S}}{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
as the
S
-indexed tensor power
.
In the case
\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S=W
is an orbit (i.e.
S
is a
transitive
V
-set
), we write

N_{W}^{V}\coloneqq\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U}^{W}:{\mathcal{C}}_{W}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V}.

In general, we will use the inset notation
-\otimes-
for
\otimes_{U}^{2\cdot*_{V}}
, and when
\varnothing_{V}\in\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}
, we will refer to the
\varnothing_{V}
-ary operation
*\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
as the
V
-unit
and denote its essential image as
1_{V}
.

1.4
.

Suppose
S
,
\left|\mathrm{Orb}(S)\right|\cdot*_{V}
, and all orbits of
S
are
I
-admissible
V
-sets.
Then, the following path lies in
I

\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\left(U\xrightarrow{!}V\right)}\left|\mathrm{Orb}(S)\right|\cdot V\xrightarrow{\hskip 30.0pt\nabla\hskip 30.0pt}V,

In algebra, this yields the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{C}}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{C}}_{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{C}}_{V}^{\times\mathrm{Orb}(S)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U}^{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(N_{U}^{V}-\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}

i.e.
\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U}^{S}X_{U}\simeq\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}N_{U}^{V}X_{U}
.
Thus, when
I
is an indexing category, the indexed tensor products in an
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category is are determined by their binary tensor products and norms.

In
[
Ste24a
, § 1.2]
, we saw that
I
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories satisfy a version of the
double coset formula

\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}N_{U}^{V}Z\simeq\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{X}^{U\times_{V}W}\operatorname{Res}^{U}_{X}Z

for all cospans
U\rightarrow V\leftarrow W
in
{\mathcal{T}}
such that
U\rightarrow W
is in
I
.
Moreover,
\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{W}
and
N_{V}^{W}
preserve applicable trivially indexed tensor products;
when
I
is an indexing category, this and the double coset formula characterize
all
interactions between restrictions and indexed tensor products.

1.4
.

Right Kan extensions preserve product preserving functors;
applying this to the
orbits
functor
F_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon{\math@bb{F}}_{\mathcal{T}}\rightarrow{\math@bb{F}}
yields a functor

\Gamma\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}(F_{{\mathcal{T}}})_{*}\colon\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}^{\times}(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}),{\mathcal{C}}).

In particular,
\Gamma
is right adjoint to
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}(F_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{*}
.
When
{\mathcal{C}}=\mathrm{Cat}
, the counit of this adjunction is a natural
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal functor.

\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\Gamma{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}

We refer to the (symmetric monoidal)
V
-value of this as the
symmetric monoidal
V
-evaluation

\operatorname{ev}_{V}\colon\Gamma{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V}^{\otimes}.

1.2.7.
Symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories

The
\infty
-category of
symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories
is

\mathrm{Cat}_{I^{\infty},{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}\simeq\mathrm{Coeff}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\otimes}\simeq\mathrm{CMon}\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

1.4
.

Suppose
L{\mathcal{C}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}
is a localizing
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory of a symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category.
We say that
L
is
compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure
if for each
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, the localization
L_{V}
is compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure on
{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
in the sense of
[

, Def 2.2.1.6]
.

We will crucially use the following proposition in
Section

1.3
.

Proposition 1.4
.

If
L
is compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure, there exists a commutative diagram of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left({\math@bb{F}}_{*}\right)_{\mathrm{triv}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\otimes}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

L{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
is a symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category and
L^{\otimes}
is a symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor,

(b)

the underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor of
L^{\otimes}
is
L\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow L{\mathcal{C}}
, and

(c)

L^{\otimes}
possesses a fully faithful and lax symmetric monoidal right
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjoint extending the inclusion
L{\mathcal{C}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof.

This is the specialization of
[
NS22
, Thm 2.9.2]
to
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq{\math@bb{E}}_{\infty}^{\otimes}
.
∎

1.3.
The canonical symmetric monoidal structure on
I
-commutative monoids

We now explore the observation that the parameterized presentability results of
[
Hil24
]
are sufficiently strong to power non-indexed lifts of
[
GGN15
]
in the
I
-semiadditive setting.

1.4
c.f.
[
Hil24
, Thm 3.1.9(2),Thm 6.1.2]
.

A (large)
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
is
{\mathcal{T}}
-presentable
if it admits finite
{\mathcal{T}}
-coproducts and its straightening factors as

{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Pr}^{L,\kappa}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}

for some regular cardinal
\kappa
.
The (nonfull) subcategory

\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

has objects given by
{\mathcal{T}}
-presentable
\infty
-categories and morphisms given by
{\mathcal{T}}
-left adjoints.

1.4
.

The conditions of factoring through
\Pr^{L,\kappa}
, of strongly admitting finite
{\mathcal{T}}
-coproducts, and of being
{\mathcal{T}}
-left adjoints are preserved by restriction;
hence
\Pr_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{L}
canonically lifts to a (nonfull)
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory

\underline{\mathrm{Pr}}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\widehat{}\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

These satisfy an adjoint functor theorem
[
Hil24
, Thm 6.2.1]
and have analogous characterizations to the non-equivariant case;
in particular,
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is closed under functor
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories from small
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories
[
Hil24
, Lem 6.7.1]
and by
Section

1.3
,
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is closed under fiberwise
\kappa
-accessible
{\mathcal{T}}
-localizations.
Hence
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
is
{\mathcal{T}}
-presentable when
{\mathcal{C}}
is
{\mathcal{T}}
-presentable.

Additionally, in
[
Nar17
]
, a
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal structure was constructed on
\underline{\mathrm{Pr}}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
In order to characterize this structure, we use the following definition
(c.f.
[
QS19
, § 5.1]
).

1.4
[
QS19
, Def 5.14]
.

Fix
S
a finite
V
-set,
({\mathcal{C}}_{U})
an
S
-
\infty
-category,
{\mathcal{D}}
a
V
-
\infty
-category, and
F\colon\prodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}{\mathcal{C}}_{U}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
a
V
-functor.
Denote by
(-)_{*}
the indexed products in
\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
and
(-)^{*}
the restriction.
We say that
F
is
S
-distributive
if, for every pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\times_{V}S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

and
S
-colimit diagram
\overline{p}\colon K^{\underline{\triangleright}}\rightarrow g^{\prime*}{\mathcal{C}}
for
p\colon K\rightarrow g^{\prime*}{\mathcal{C}}
, the composite
T
-functor

\left(f^{\prime}_{*}K\right)^{\underline{\triangleright}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{can}}f^{\prime}_{*}\left(K^{\underline{\triangleright}}\right)\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}_{*}\overline{p}}f^{\prime}_{*}g^{\prime*}{\mathcal{C}}\simeq g^{*}f_{*}{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{g^{*}F}g^{*}{\mathcal{D}}

is a
T
-colimit diagram for the associated composite
f^{\prime}_{*}K\rightarrow g^{*}{\mathcal{D}}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\delta}\left(f_{*}{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}\right)\subset\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\left(f_{*}{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}\right)

the full subcategory spanned by
S
-distributive functors.

By the proof of
[
Nar17
, Prop 3.25]
, Nardin’s
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal structure on
\underline{\mathrm{Pr}}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
has
V
unit
\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{V}
and indexed tensor products characterized by the universal property

\operatorname{Fun}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\left(\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{E}}\right)\simeq\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\delta}\left(\prodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}\right).

1.4
.

The
\infty
-category of
presentably
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
is the (non-full) subcategory
\mathrm{CAlg}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\left(\underline{\Pr}^{L,\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\right)\subset\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}
;
the
\infty
-category of
presentably symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories
