is the (non-full) subcategory
\mathrm{CAlg}\left(\Pr^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\right)\subset\mathrm{CMon}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\right)
.

1.4
.

By definition, a
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category whose underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category is presentable factors through the inclusion
\underline{\Pr}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
if and only if its structure maps
{\mathcal{C}}^{\times S}_{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
are in
\operatorname{Fun}^{\delta}_{V}({\mathcal{C}}^{\times S}_{V},{\mathcal{C}}_{V})
;
in the language of
[
NS22
]
, a presentably
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category is precisely a
distributive

{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category whose underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category is presentable.

1.4
[
Nar17
, Ex 3.17]
.

In the case
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{C_{2}}
, given a
[C_{2}/e]
-distributive
C_{2}
-functor
F\colon\mathrm{CoInd}_{e}^{C_{2}}{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
, the
e
-value of
F
is a functor
{\mathcal{C}}\times{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{e}
and the
C_{2}
-value of
F
turns coproducts into
\mathrm{CoInd}_{e}^{C_{2}}[2]\simeq[2]\sqcup[C_{2}/e]
-indexed coproducts:

F_{C_{2}}(X\sqcup Y)\simeq F_{C_{2}}(X)\sqcup F_{C_{2}}(Y)\sqcup\mathrm{Ind}_{e}^{C_{2}}F_{e}(X,Y).

In particular, the norms in a presentably
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category are often not compatible with coproducts.

1.4
.

By
[
NS22
, Prop 3.2.5]
, if
{\mathcal{C}}
is a cocomplete
\infty
-category with finite products such that finite products preserve colimits separately in each variable, then the cartesian symmetric monoidal structures on
\mathrm{Coeff}^{V}{\mathcal{C}}
lift to a distributive
{\mathcal{T}}
A-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\times}
, which we refer to as the
Cartesian structure
.
It follows from Hilman’s characterization of parameterized presentability
[
Hil24
, Thm 6.1.2]
that
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}
is presentable, so
Section

1.3
implies that
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\times}
is presentably symmetric monoidal.

Hilman used the universal property of
\otimes
in
[
Hil24
, Prop 6.7.5]
to prove the formula

{\mathcal{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{D}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{R}\left({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{D}}\right).

Using this, for any
{\mathcal{T}}
-presentable
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
, we have

\displaystyle\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}^{I-\times}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\operatorname{Span}}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}^{I-\times}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\operatorname{Span}}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}^{R}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}^{R}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}^{I-\times}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\operatorname{Span}}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{C}}\otimes\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}).

i.e. the functor
{\mathcal{C}}\mapsto\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
is
smashing
.
In fact, we can say more.

1.4
.

We say that a presentable
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
category is
I
-semiadditive
if its underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category is
I
-semiadditive, and we let
\mathrm{Pr}^{L,I-\oplus}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\Pr^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
be the full subcategory spanned by
I
-semiadditive presentable
{\mathcal{T}}
-categories.

It follows from
Theorem

1.3
that a
{\mathcal{T}}
-presentable
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category is fixed by
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}(-)
if and only if it’s
I
-semiadditive, i.e. the smashing localization corresponding with
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}(-)
is left adjoint to the inclusion
\Pr_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{L}\subset\Pr_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{L,I-\oplus}
.
By
[
GGN15
, Lemma 3.6]
, this implies that given
{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
, there is a unique compatible commutative algebra structure on its localization
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
.
In other words, we’ve shown the following.

Theorem 1.5
.

The localizing subcategory

\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Pr}^{L,I-\oplus}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\iota

is smashing;
in particular, if
{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}
is a presentably symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-category, then there is an essentially unique presentably symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}^{\otimes-\mathrm{mode}}({\mathcal{D}})
possessing a (necessarily unique) symmetric monoidal lift

\operatorname{Fr}^{\otimes}\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}^{\otimes-\mathrm{mode}}({\mathcal{D}})

of
\operatorname{Fr}\colon{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{D}})
.

1.5
.

Theorem

1.5
is not as
genuinely equivariant
as the user may want, as it constructs
symmetric monoidal structures
, but never norm maps.
The author is content with this for the purposes of this paper, as the algebraic interpretation of indexed tensor products of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads is unclear.
She hopes to address the indexed case in forthcoming work.

1.5
.

Under the equivalence of
Theorem

1.4
, writing
{\mathcal{D}}=\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})
,
Theorem

1.5
constructs an essentially unique presentably symmetric monoidal structure on
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
subject to the condition that the free functor
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
bears a symmetric monoidal structure under the Cartesian structure.

1.5
.

The
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is freely generated under
{\mathcal{T}}
-colimits by one
{\mathcal{T}}
-point, in the sense that evaluation at the
V
-units
(*_{V})
yields an equivalence
[
Sha23
, Thm 11.5]

\operatorname{Fun}^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},{\mathcal{C}})\simeq\Gamma{\mathcal{C}}.

In particular, every symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category receives at most one symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-left adjoint from
\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
;
in the case
{\mathcal{C}}=\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\times}
the condition of
Theorem

1.5
then may be read as saying that there is a unique presentably symmetric monoidal structure on
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
with
V
-unit
1^{\mathrm{mode}}_{V}=\operatorname{Fr}(*_{V})
for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
.

Furthermore, by Yoneda’s lemma, these
V
-units are characterized by the property that

\operatorname{Map}_{V}(1_{V}^{\mathrm{mode}},X_{V})\simeq\operatorname{Map}(*_{V},X_{V}(*_{V}))\simeq X_{V}(*_{V}).

We’d like to identify this symmetric monoidal structure via a familiar formula.
We have a candidate:

Proposition 1.5

(
[
BS24b
, Prop 4.24]
, via
[
CHLL24
, Prop 3.3.4]
)
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, then the Day convolution structure on
\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{V}),{\mathcal{C}})
with respect to the smash product on
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})
is compatible with the localization

L_{\mathrm{Seg}}:\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{V}),{\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})_{V}

Proof.

By the general criterion
[
CHLL24
, Prop 3.3.4]
, it suffices to verify that
A_{+}\wedge-:\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})
is product-preserving, which follows by the fact that it is colimit preserving and
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})
is semiadditive.
∎

By
Section

1.2.7
,
Section

1.3
constructs a symmetric monoidal structure
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})^{\circledast}
on
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
.
We will show that this agrees with the mode symmetric monoidal structure.

Theorem 1.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
be a presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.
Then, there is a unique equivalence between the Day convolution and mode symmetric monoidal structures on
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
lifting the identity.

The proof of
[
BS24b
, Lemma 4.21]
and
[
CSY20
, Lemma 5.2.1]
apply identically to the following.

Lemma 1.6
.

Fix
{\mathcal{A}}_{0},{\mathcal{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
and
L:{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\rightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-localization functor which is compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure on
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
.
Then,
L\otimes\operatorname{id}_{{\mathcal{B}}}:{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-localization functor which is compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure on
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}
.

Proof of
Theorem

1.6
.

Set the temporary notation
\underline{\mathrm{PCMon}}_{I}(-)\coloneqq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\left(\underline{\operatorname{Span}}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),-\right)
.
Our argument follows along the lines of
[
BS24b
, Thm 4.26]
.
Repeating the argument of
Theorem

1.5
, for all presentably symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories
{\mathcal{D}}
, we acquire a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{PCMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{D}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{PCMon}}_{I}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\otimes{\mathcal{D}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{D}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\otimes{\mathcal{D}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Moreover, under the identification of tensor products and coproducts in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
, the top equivalence corresponds with the postcomposition symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
\underline{\mathrm{PCMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{PCMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{D}})
along the canonical symmetric monoidal left
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjoint
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
and the symmetric monoidal free
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{PCMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{D}})
pushforward functor (see
[
BS24b
, Prop 3.3,3.6]
).
Thus the top arrow can be lifted to a
symmetric monoidal
equivalence.
We may take adjoint functors to find the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{PCMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{D}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{PCMon}}_{I}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\otimes{\mathcal{D}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{D}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\otimes{\mathcal{D}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{L_{\mathrm{Seg}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{L_{\mathrm{Seg}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

of
[
CHLL24
, Prop 3.3.4]
.
The bottom functor is a symmetric monoidal localization of the top.
In particular, by
Section

1.3
, it suffices to prove this in the case
{\mathcal{D}}=\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.

The
{\mathcal{T}}
-Yoneda embedding is
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal for the
{\mathcal{T}}
-Day convolution by
[
NS22
, Thm 6.0.12]
, so
1^{\mathrm{Day}}_{V}\simeq y(*_{V})
.
Hence Yoneda’s lemma yields that

\operatorname{Map}_{V}(1_{V}^{\circledast},X_{V})\simeq\operatorname{Map}(y(*_{V}),X_{V})\simeq X_{V}(*_{V}),

which implies that
1^{\circledast}_{V}\simeq 1^{\mathrm{mode}}_{V}
, naturally in
V
.
The theorem then follows by
Section

1.3
.
∎

1.6
.

It is not likely that it is necessary for
{\mathcal{T}}
to be atomic orbital in the above argument;
indeed, for
\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\times}(\operatorname{Span}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}})
to implement
I
-semiadditivization
, it suffices to assume that
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is a weakly extensive subcategory whose slice categories
I_{/V}
are
n_{V}
-categories for some finite
n_{V}\in{\math@bb{N}}
in the sense of
[
CHLL24a
]
.

For instance, if
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
is an atomic orbital subcategory of an
\infty
-category, then weakly extensive subcategories
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}
are pre-inductible (and hence satisfy the semiadditive closure theorem)and represent a global version of one-color weak indexing categories.
Unfortunately, the author is not aware of a symmetric monodial structure on partially presentable
{\mathcal{T}}
-categories, and developing such a thing would lead us far afield from our current operadic goals,

1.4.
The homotopy
I
-symmetric monoidal
d
-category

Recall that a space is
(-2)
-truncated
if it is empty,
(-1)
-truncated
if it is empty or contractible, and for
d\geq 0
, a space
X
is
d
-truncated
if it is a disjoint union of connected spaces
(X_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in A}
such that
\pi_{m}\left(X_{\alpha}\right)=0
for all
m>d
and
\alpha\in A
.

Recall that a
(d+1)
-category
is an
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
such that the space
\operatorname{Map}(X,Y)
is
d
-truncated for all
X,Y\in{\mathcal{C}}
.
We say that an
\infty
-category is a
(-1)
-category
if it is either
*
or empty.
In general, we write
\mathrm{Cat}_{d}\subset\mathrm{Cat}
for the full subcategory spanned by the
\infty
-categories with the property that they are
d
-categories.

1.6
.

The
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of small
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-categories is

\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}:=\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\mathrm{Cat}_{d}.

A
{\mathcal{T}}
-poset
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-0-category.
If
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is pullback-stable, the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of small
I
-symmetric monoidal
d
-categories
is

\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{I,d}^{\otimes}:=\underline{\mathrm{CMon}}_{I}\mathrm{Cat}_{d}.

We write
\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}\coloneqq\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}
and
\mathrm{Cat}_{I,d}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{I,d}^{\otimes}
.

By the following lemma,
\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
(d+1)
-category and
\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}
is a
(d+1)
-category.

Lemma 1.6

(
[
HTT
, Cor 2.3.4.8,Prop 2.3.4.12,Cor 2.3.4.19]
)
.

\mathrm{Cat}_{d}
is a
(d+1)
-category and the inclusion

\mathrm{Cat}_{d}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}

has a right adjoint
h_{d}\colon\mathrm{Cat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{d}
.

1.6
.

By
Sections

1.1.4
and
1.4
, the functor
\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is an inclusion of a localizing
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory;
let
h_{d}\colon\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}
be the associated
{\mathcal{T}}
-left adjoint.

The mapping spaces in a product of categories are the product of the mapping spaces;
in particular, the inclusion
\mathrm{Cat}_{d}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is product-preserving.
Hence
Sections

1.2.5
and
1.4
construct a
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{I}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{I,d}^{\otimes}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

whose right adjoint is fully faithful.
We refer to
h_{d}
as the
homotopy
I
-symmetric monoidal
d
-category
.

The remainder of this subsection will be dedicated to recognition results for
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
d
-categories, which will be useful throughout the remainder of the paper.
We first reduce this consideration to that of plain
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories;
the following proposition follows by unwinding definitions and noting that
\mathrm{Cat}_{d}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is closed under products.

Proposition 1.6
.

If
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is a one-object weak indexing system, then
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{I}^{\otimes}
is a
I
-symmetric monoidal
d
-category if and only if its underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-category.

Often in equivariant higher algebra, we will find that our objects come with natural
{\mathcal{T}}
-functors to
{\mathcal{T}}
-
1
-categories, and we’d like to develop a recognition theorem in this case in terms of mapping spaces.

Proposition 1.6
.

A
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-category if and only if

\operatorname{Mor}_{V}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{V})^{\simeq}

is
(d-2)
-truncated for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
.

Proof.

By definition, it suffices to prove this in the case
{\mathcal{T}}=*
.
Fix
f,g\in\operatorname{Mor}_{V}({\mathcal{C}})
.
Then, we may present
\operatorname{Map}(f,g)
as a disjoint union over
a,b
of homotopies

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

For fixed
a,b
, this is either empty or equivalent to the component of the space
\operatorname{Map}(S^{1},\operatorname{Map}(W,Z))
whose underlying map is homotopic to
bf
.
If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
d
-category, then this is
(d-2)
-truncated;
conversely, choosing
a,b=\mathrm{id}
and
f=g
, if this is
(d-2)
-truncated for all
f
, then the mapping spaces of
{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
are
(d-1)
-truncated for all
V
, i.e.
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-category.
∎

Given a
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
and a map
\psi\colon\Delta^{1}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
, define the pullback space

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mor}_{F}^{\psi}({\mathcal{C}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mor}_{V}({\mathcal{C}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\operatorname{Aut}_{\psi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mor}_{V}({\mathcal{D}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

so that
\operatorname{Mor}_{F}^{\psi}({\mathcal{C}})
is the disjoint union of the connected components of
\operatorname{Mor}_{V}({\mathcal{C}})
whose image in
\operatorname{Mor}_{V}({\mathcal{D}})
is equivalent to
\psi
.
We say that
F
has
(d-1)
-truncated mapping fibers
if
\operatorname{Mor}_{F}^{\psi}({\mathcal{C}})
is
(d-2)
-truncated for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
\psi\in\operatorname{Mor}_{V}({\mathcal{C}})
.

Corollary 1.6
.

Suppose
F:{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor and
{\mathcal{D}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-1-category.
Then, the following are equivalent for
d\geq 1
:

(1)

F
has
(d-1)
-truncated mapping fibers.

(2)

{\mathcal{C}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-category.

Additionally, the following are equivalent.

(1’)

F^{\simeq}\colon{\mathcal{C}}^{\simeq}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}^{\simeq}
is fully faithful and
F
has
(-1)
-truncated mapping fibers.

(2’)

F
includes
{\mathcal{C}}
as a (replete)
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory of
{\mathcal{D}}
.

Proof.

After
Section

1.4
, the only remaining part is the equivalence between (1’) and (2’).
Note that
B\operatorname{Aut}_{\psi}
is
(-1)
-truncated by
Section

1.4
, so (1’) is equivalent to the conditions that
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
1
-category and
F_{V}\colon{\mathcal{C}}_{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{V}
is a faithful functor which is fully faithful on cores, i.e. it is a (replete) subcategory inclusion.
∎

2.
Equivariant operads and symmetric sequences

In
Section

2.1
, we begin by recalling rudiments of the theory of
algebraic patterns and Segal objects
of
[
CH21
]
and the theory of
fibrous patterns and the Segal envelope
of
[
BHS22
]
;
in the case of
{\mathfrak{O}}=\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
, we show in
Section

A.1
that this recovers the theory of
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories,
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-operads (henceforth
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads), and the
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal envelope of
[
NS22
]
.
We go on in
Section

2.2
to specialize several results of
[
CH21
,
BHS22
]
to this setting and construct the family of
weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads
.

After this, we go on to study the
underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal sequence
functor in
Section

2.3
, showing in
Section

2.3.2
that it forms a fiberwise-monadic
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor

\underline{\operatorname{sseq}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}:\underline{\mathrm{Op}}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}});

in particular, this implies that it is a conservative right
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjoint and confirms an atomic orbital lift of
Theorem

A
.
In
Section

2.7
, we use this to confirm
Corollary

B
.

In
Section

2.4
we go on to compute the monad
T_{{\mathcal{O}}}
for
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras in arbitrary
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories;
in particular, when
{\mathcal{C}}\simeq\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
for a structure whose indexed tensor products are indexed products, we naturally split off a
{\mathcal{O}}(S)
-summand from
T_{{\mathcal{O}}}(S)
;
using our atomic orbital lift of
Theorem

A
, we conclude that
\mathrm{Alg}_{(-)}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\colon\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is conservative.

Last, in preparation for forthcoming work, we initiate in
Section

2.5
the study of the localizing subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads whose underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequence is
(d-1)
-truncated, called
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-operads
;
we show in particular that
\mathrm{Alg}_{(-)}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq n+1})
detects
n
-equivalences.
Moreover, in
Section

2.6
, we confirm that the full subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
-0-operads agrees with the poset of subterminal objects, which themselves agree with the weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads.

We finish in
Section

2.7
by verifying that Bonventre’s nerve restricts to an equivalence between categories of
G
-
1
-operads and explicitly describing algebras over
{\mathcal{T}}
-1-operads.
We assure the reader exclusively interested in
using

{\mathcal{T}}
-operads that the relevant interpretations of the results of
Section

2.1
will be restated throughout the following subsections, so these sections may be black-boxed at the cost of completeness of proofs.

2.1.
Recollections on algebraic patterns

An algebraic pattern is a collection of data encoding
Segal conditions
for the purpose of homotopy-coherent algebra.
Given an algebraic pattern
{\mathfrak{O}}
and a complete
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
, there is an
\infty
-category of
Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
, which we view as
{\mathfrak{O}}
-monoids in
{\mathcal{C}}
;
these are presented as functors
{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
satisfying a Segal condition.

We may view Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-objects in
\mathrm{Cat}
(aka Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-categories) as
{\mathfrak{O}}
-monoidal
\infty
-categories;
these straighten to cocartesian fibrations over
{\mathfrak{O}}
satisfying conditions.
As in
[
HA
, § 2]
, the condition of
being a cocartesian fibration
may be relaxed to construct a form of operads parameterized by
{\mathfrak{O}}
, called
fibrous
{\mathfrak{O}}
-patterns.

In contrast to the categorical patterns of
[
HA
, § B]
, these are manifestly
\infty
-categorical, and it is relatively easy to construct push-pull adjunctions between categories of fibrous patterns over different algebraic patterns;
we found our theory of
I
-operads in this syntax for this reason, as the Boardman-Vogt tensor product is most easily defined in terms of pushforward along maps of algebraic patterns.

The author would like to emphasize that the program surrounding algebraic patterns has achieved many results not mentioned here, as fibrous patterns only play a foundational role.
For a significantly more thorough and elegant treatment, we recommend
[
CH21
,
BHS22
,
CH23
]
.

2.1.1.
Algebraic patterns and Segal objects

2.0
.

An
algebraic pattern
is a triple
({\mathfrak{B}},({\mathfrak{B}}^{\operatorname{in}},{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{act}}),{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}})
, where
({\mathfrak{B}}^{\operatorname{in}},{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{act}})
is a factorization system on
{\mathfrak{B}}
and
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}\subset{\mathfrak{B}}^{\operatorname{in}}
is a full subcategory.
14
14

14

Throughout this paper, we adopt the definition of
factorization system
used in
[
CH21
, Rmk 2.2]
, which does not assert any lifting properties;
that is, a factorization system on
{\mathcal{C}}
is a pair of wide subcategories
{\mathcal{C}}^{L},{\mathcal{C}}^{R}\subset{\mathcal{C}}
satisfying the condition that, for all maps
X\xrightarrow{f}X^{\prime}
, the space of factorizations
X\xrightarrow{l}Y\xrightarrow{r}X^{\prime}
with
l\in{\mathcal{C}}^{L}
and
r\in{\mathcal{C}}^{R}
is contractible.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{AlgPatt}\subset\operatorname{Fun}(\mathbf{Q},\mathrm{Cat})
is the full subcategory spanned by algebraic patterns, where

(11)

\mathbf{Q}:=\bullet\rightarrow\bullet\rightarrow\bullet\leftarrow\bullet.

We refer to the morphisms in
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\operatorname{in}}
as “inert morphisms,” morphisms in
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{act}}
as “active morphisms,” and objects in
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}
as “elementary objects.”
When it is clear from context, we will abusively refer to the quadruple
\left({\mathfrak{B}},({\mathfrak{B}}^{\operatorname{in}},{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{act}}),{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}\right)
simply as
{\mathfrak{B}}
.
The following is our primary source of examples.

2.0
[
BHS22
, Def 3.2.6]
.

An
adequate quadruple
is the data of an adequate triple
{\mathcal{X}}_{b},{\mathcal{X}}_{f}\subset{\mathcal{X}}
in the sense of
Section

1.2
together with a full subcategory
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{b}
;
the
\infty
-category of adequate quadruples
is the full subcategory

\mathrm{Quad}^{\mathrm{adeq}}\subset\operatorname{Fun}(\mathbf{Q},\mathrm{Cat})

spanned by adequate quadruples, where
\mathbf{Q}
is defined by
Eq.

11
.

Given an adequate quadruple
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{b}\subset{\mathcal{X}}\supset{\mathcal{X}}_{f}
, let
{\mathcal{X}}_{b}^{\operatorname{op}}\subset\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{X}})
be the wide subcategory spanned by the spans
X\xleftarrow{\psi_{b}}R\xrightarrow{\psi_{f}}Y
with
\psi_{f}
an equivalence, and similarly
{\mathcal{X}}_{f}\subset\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{X}})
the wide subcategory of spans with
\psi_{b}
an equivalence.
This yields a factorization system
[
HHLN23
, Prop 4.9]

{\mathcal{X}}_{b}^{\operatorname{op}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{X}})\hookleftarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{f}.

We define the span pattern
\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}\left({\mathcal{X}};{\mathcal{X}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{0}\right)
via the data

•

underlying
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{X}})
,

•

inert morphisms
{\mathcal{X}}_{b}^{\operatorname{op}}\subset\operatorname{Span}({\mathcal{X}})
,

•

active morphisms
{\mathcal{X}}_{f}\subset\operatorname{Span}({\mathcal{X}})
, and

•

elementary objects
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}^{\operatorname{op}}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{b}^{\operatorname{op}}
.

Given a map of adequate quadruples
\left({\mathcal{X}},({\mathcal{X}}_{b},{\mathcal{X}}_{f}),{\mathcal{X}}_{0}\right)\rightarrow\left({\mathcal{Y}},({\mathcal{Y}}_{b},{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}),{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}\right)
the associated functor
\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{Y}})

preserves inert morphisms, active morphisms, and elementary objects by definition;
hence the functor
\operatorname{Span}_{-,-}(-;-)\colon\mathrm{Quad}^{\mathrm{adeq}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\mathbf{Q},\mathrm{Cat})
descends to a functor

\operatorname{Span}_{-,-}(-;-)\colon\mathrm{Quad}^{\mathrm{adeq}}\rightarrow\mathrm{AlgPatt}.

The central example for equivariant higher algebra is the following.

2.0
.

When
{\mathcal{T}}
is an orbital
\infty
-category,
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-weak indexing system (e.g.
I={\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
), and
c(I)
its
color family
in the sense of
Eq.

10
, we define the
effective
I
-Burnside pattern

\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}):=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathrm{all},I}\left({\math@bb{F}}_{c(I)};c(I)\right)

2.0
.

Given
{\mathcal{T}}
an orbital
\infty
-category, we may define the
algebraic pattern of finite pointed
{\mathcal{T}}
-sets
as

\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}_{\mathrm{si},\mathrm{tdeg}}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}};{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\right),

where morphisms are in
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{tdeg}}
if their projection to
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
is homotopic to an identity, morphisms are in
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{si}}
if they’re a composition of cocartesian arrows and target-degenerate summand inclusions, and the inclusion
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
corresponds with the
{\mathcal{T}}
-object
*_{{\mathcal{T}}}\in\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
Note that the
target functor

\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
determines a functor
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
;
this corresponds with the structure functor of the free pointed
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
on
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
as defined in
[
NS22
,
CLL24
]
.
Moreover, there is a composite map of algebraic patterns

(12)

\varphi\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{\mathrm{all},\mathrm{tdeg}}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}};{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}})\xrightarrow{U}\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}).

Algebraic patterns provide a general framework for algebraic structures satisfying the associated
Segal conditions
, which are encoded in the notion of
Segal objects
.

2.0
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a complete
\infty
-category and
{\mathfrak{O}}
an algebraic pattern.
Then, the
\infty
-category of
Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
is the full subcategory
\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})\subset\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{O}},{\mathcal{C}})
consisting of functors
F\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
such that, for every object
O\in{\mathfrak{O}}
, the natural map

F(O)\rightarrow\lim_{E\in{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{O/}}F(E)

is an equivalence, where
{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{O/}\coloneqq{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}\times_{{\mathfrak{O}}^{\operatorname{in}},\operatorname{ev}_{1}}{\mathfrak{O}}^{\operatorname{in}}_{O/}
is the
\infty
-category whose objects consist of inert morphisms from
O
to an elementary object.

2.0
.

By
[
CH21
, Lem 2.9]
, a functor
F\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-object if and only if the associated functor
F|_{{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{int}}}
is right Kan extended from
F|_{{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}}
along the inclusion
{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{int}}
.

2.0
.

We show in
Section

A.1.2
that
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}\simeq\left({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/S}\right)^{\operatorname{op}}
contains the set of orbits
\mathrm{Orb}(S)
as an initial subcategory.
Hence there is an equivalence of full subcategories

\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Span}_{I}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\mathrm{CMon}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})\hskip 10.0pt\subset\hskip 10.0pt\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}}).

One benefit of the framework of Segal objects is the following monadicity result.

Proposition 2.0

(
[
CH21
, Cor 8.2]
)
.

if
{\mathfrak{O}}
is an algebraic pattern and
{\mathcal{C}}
a presentable
\infty
-category, then the forgetful functor

U\colon\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}},{\mathcal{C}})

is monadic;
in particular, it is conservative.

Corollary 2.0
.

A morphism of
I
-commutative monoids is an equivalence if and only if its underlying morphism of
c(I)
-objects is an equivalence;
in particular, an
I
-symmetric monoidal functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}
is an equivalence if and only if the underlying
c(I)
-functor is an equivalence.

Another benefit of Segal objects is a rich framework for functoriality.

2.0
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{P}},{\mathfrak{O}}
are algebraic patterns.
A functor
f\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{O}}
is
compatible with Segal objects
if it preserves the inert-active factorization system and
f^{*}\colon\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{O}},{\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{P}},{\mathcal{C}})
preserves Segal objects in any complete
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}
.
Moreover, a morphism of algebraic patterns
f\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{O}}
is a called a:

•

Segal morphism
if it is compatible with Segal objects, and a

•

strong Segal morphism
if the associated functor
f^{\mathrm{el}}_{X/}\colon{\mathfrak{P}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{X/}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{f(X)/}
is initial for all
X\in{\mathfrak{P}}
.

2.0
.

The conditions for Segal morphisms and strong Segal morphisms are each compatible with compositions and equivalences;
that is, there are
core-preserving
wide subcategories

\mathrm{AlgPatt}^{\mathrm{Seg}},\mathrm{AlgPatt}^{\mathrm{Strong-}\mathrm{Seg}}\subset\mathrm{AlgPatt}

whose morphisms are the Segal morphisms and strong Segal morphisms, respectively.

There is a universal example of coefficients, which we can use to verify that a functor is a Segal morphism.

2.0
.

[
CH21
, Lem 4.5]
concludes that
f
is a Segal morphism if
f^{*}
preserves Segal objects in
spaces
.

2.0
.

We show in
Section

A.3
that, given any functor
{\mathcal{T}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
of atomic orbital
\infty
-categories, the associated functor

\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}})

is a Segal morphism.
Additionally, in
Section

A.1.3
, we show that the map
\varphi
of
Eq.

12
is a segal morphism, constructing a pullback map

\mathrm{CMon}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}({\mathcal{C}}).

In
[
Bar23
, Cor 2.64]
, conditions for a strong Segal morphism were developed concerning when their pullback maps are equivalences, and these conditions were checked in
[
BHS22
, Prop 5.2.14]
in the case
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
;
we review their argument and extend it to arbitrary atomic orbital
\infty
-categories in
Section

A.1
.
The existence of such an equivalence (not necessarily induced by a map of patterns) is not new, and to the author’s knowledge, first appeared as
[
Nar16
, Thm 6.5]
.

Limits of patterns
construct a large number of examples according to the following lemma.

Lemma 2.0

(
[
CH21
, Cor 5.5]
)
.

\mathrm{AlgPatt}\subset\operatorname{Fun}(\mathbf{Q},\mathrm{Cat})
is a localizing subcategory;
in particular,
\mathrm{AlgPatt}
has small limits.

2.0
.

In particular,
\mathrm{AlgPatt}
has products.
By
[
CH21
, Ex 5.7]
, there is an equivalence

\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{C}}).

In particular, this combined with
Section

2.1.1
gives a complete segal space model for
I
-symmetric monoidal categories;
indeed, the pattern
\Delta^{\operatorname{op},\natural}
of
[
CH21
, Ex 4.9]
has Segal
\Delta^{\operatorname{op},\natural}
-objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
given by
complete Segal objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
, specializing to the fact that
\mathrm{Seg}_{\Delta^{\operatorname{op},\natural}}({\mathcal{S}})\simeq\mathrm{Cat}
, and hence

\mathrm{Seg}_{\Delta^{\operatorname{op},\natural}}({\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op},\mathrm{el}}\times\Delta^{\operatorname{op},\natural}}({\mathcal{S}})\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op},\mathrm{el}}}(\mathrm{Cat})\simeq\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}},

where
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op},\mathrm{el}}
is the algberaic pattern with
\left({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op},\mathrm{el}}\right)^{\mathrm{el}}=\left({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op},\mathrm{el}}\right)^{\mathrm{int}}={\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}=\left({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op},\mathrm{el}}\right)^{\mathrm{act}}
.
Additionally,

\mathrm{Seg}_{\Delta^{\operatorname{op},\natural}}(\mathrm{CMon}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{S}}))\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{\Delta^{\operatorname{op},\natural}\times\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}({\mathcal{S}})\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}(\mathrm{Cat})\simeq\mathrm{CMon}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Cat}).

Cartesian products of patterns play nicely with well-structured maps of patterns.

Lemma 2.0
.

Suppose
f\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
and
f^{\prime}\colon{\mathfrak{O}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
are (resp. strong) Segal morphisms.
Then,

f\times f^{\prime}\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{O}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}

is a (strong) Segal morphism.

Proof.

The case of Segal morphisms follows immediately from
Section

2.1.1
, so we assume that
f,f^{\prime}
are strong Segal.
Then, the induced map

f^{\mathrm{el}}_{X/}\times f^{{}^{\prime}\mathrm{el}}_{X^{\prime}/}=\left(f\times f^{\prime}\right)^{\mathrm{el}}_{(X,X^{\prime})/}:\left({\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{O}}^{\prime}\right)^{\mathrm{el}}_{(X,X^{\prime})/}\rightarrow\left({\mathfrak{P}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\right)^{\mathrm{el}}_{(fx,fx^{\prime})/}

is a product of initial maps; it follows that it is initial, since limits in product categories are computed pointwise.
∎

2.1.2.
An interlude on soundness and extendability

We will move on to describe the theory of operads corresponding with an algebraic pattern, but to do so, we make some technical assumptions.
Let
{\mathfrak{O}}
be an algebraic pattern and
\omega\colon X\rightarrow Y
an active map.
Define the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}(\omega)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ar}({\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{X/})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{Y/}\times{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{X/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{X/}\times{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{X/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s,t)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\omega_{(-)},\operatorname{id}\right)}

where
\omega_{(-)}\colon{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{Y/}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{X/}
sends
\alpha:Y\rightarrow E
to the inert map
\omega_{a}
of the inert-active factorization of
X\xrightarrow{\omega}Y\xrightarrow{a}E
.

2.0
.

{\mathfrak{O}}
is
sound
if, for all
\omega:X\rightarrow Y
active, the associated map
{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}(\omega)\rightarrow{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{X/}
is initial.
A sound pattern
{\mathfrak{O}}
is
soundly extendable
if
\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathfrak{O}})\xrightarrow{t}{\mathfrak{O}}
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-category, where
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathfrak{O}})\subset\mathrm{Ar}({\mathfrak{O}})=\operatorname{Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathfrak{O}})
is the full subcategory spanned by active arrows.

Soundness as a condition allows one to simplify Segal conditions;
sound extendibility reduces many instances of
relative Segal objects
in the sense
[
BHS22
, Def 3.1.8]
to a morphism with Segal domain by
[
BHS22
, Obs 3.1.9]
.
A condition of
extendability
was originally introduced in
[
CH21
, Def 8.5]
for the sake of explicit formulas for the free Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-object monad, and is equivalent to sound extendability in the presence of soundness
[
BHS22
, Rmk 3.3.17]
;
we will not consider the reasoning for this notion further, but instead remark that it is true of our main examples.

2.0
.

/
We verify in
Section

A.1.2
that
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is soundly extendable;
moreover, we verify in
Section

A.1.1
that
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
is sound, and one may verify that it is soundly extendable.

2.1.3.
Fibrous patterns

The unstraightening functor of
[
HTT
]
realizes
\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}(\mathrm{Cat})
as a non-full subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathfrak{O}}}
consisting of cocartesian fibrations satisfying Segal conditions;
we relax this for the following definition, which is equivalent to the original definition stated in
[
BHS22
, Def 4.1.2]
by
[
BHS22
, Prop 4.1.6]
.

2.0
.

Let
{\mathfrak{B}}
be a sound algebraic pattern.
A
fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-pattern
is a functor
\pi:{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}
such that

(1)

(inert morphisms)
{\mathfrak{O}}
has
\pi
-cocartesian lifts for inert morphisms of
{\mathfrak{B}}
,

(2)

(Segal condition for colors) For every active morphism
\omega\colon V_{0}\rightarrow V_{1}
in
{\mathfrak{B}}
, the functor

{\mathfrak{O}}_{V_{0}}\rightarrow\lim_{\alpha\in{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{V_{1}/}}{\mathfrak{O}}_{\omega_{\alpha,!}V_{1}}

induced by cocartesian transport along
\omega_{\alpha}
is an equivalence, where
\omega_{(-)}\colon{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{Y/}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{X/}
is the inert morphism appearing in the inert-active factorization of
\alpha\circ\omega
, and

(3)

(Segal condition for multimorphisms) for every pair of objects
V_{1},V_{2}\in{\mathfrak{B}}
and colors
X_{i}\in{\mathfrak{O}}_{V_{i}}
, the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}(X_{0},X_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim\limits_{\alpha\colon V_{1}\rightarrow E\in{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{V_{1}/}}\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}(X_{0},\alpha_{!}X_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(V_{0},V_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim\limits_{\alpha\colon V_{1}\rightarrow E\in{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{V_{1}/}}\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(V_{0},E)}

is cartesian.

We denote by
\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})\subset\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}}
the full subcategory spanned by the fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-patterns, where the latter category has objects the functors to
{\mathfrak{B}}
possessing cocartesian lifts over inert morphisms and morphisms the functors preserving such cocartesian lifts.

2.0
.

As noted in
[
BHS22
, Rmk 4.1.8]
, in the presence of condition (3) above, condition (2) may be weakened to assert that the functor
{\mathfrak{O}}_{V_{0}}\rightarrow\lim_{\alpha\in{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{V_{1}/}}{\mathfrak{O}}_{\omega_{\alpha,!}V_{1}}
is a
\pi_{0}
-surjection without changing the resulting notion.
To match
[
BHS22
, Prop 4.1.6]
, we may even take the intermediate assumption that this functor induces an equivalence on cores.

2.0
.

Fibrous
{\math@bb{F}}_{*}
-patterns are equivalent to
\infty
-operads (c.f.
[
HA
]
), and in
Section

A.1
we will extend a proof due to
[
BHS22
]
(in the case
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
) that fibrous
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
-patterns are equivalent to the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-operads of
[
NS22
]
.

The fully faithful functor
U\colon\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}}
is a reflective subcategory inclusion.

Proposition 2.0

(
[
BHS22
, Cor 4.2.3]
)
.

U
participates in an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{Fbrs}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

In terms of functoriality, we prove the following in
Section

A.2
, extending
[
BHS22
, Lem 4.1.19]
.

Proposition 2.0
.

Suppose
f\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{O}}
is a Segal morphism and either
{\mathfrak{O}}
is soundly extendable or
f
is strong Segal.
Then, the pullback functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathfrak{P}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathfrak{O}}}
preserves fibrous patterns;
furthermore, the functor

f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{O}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{P}})

has a left adjoint given by
L_{\mathrm{Fbrs}}f_{!}
.

Sections

2.1.1
and
2.1.3
together yield a functor

\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*});

we review a proof that this is an equivalence (originally due to
[
BHS22
]
when
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
) in
Section

A.1.3
.

A fibrous pattern
\pi\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}
inherits a structure of an algebraic pattern whose inert morphisms consist of
\pi
-cocartesian lifts of inert morphisms in
{\mathfrak{B}}
, whose active morphisms are arbitrary lifts of active morphisms in
{\mathfrak{B}}
, and whose elementary objects are spanned by lifts of elementary objects.
This is canonical:

Proposition 2.0

(
[
BHS22
, Cor 4.1.7]
)
.

Fibrous patterns are closed under composition for the above pattern structure, inducing an equivalence

\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{O}})\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})_{/{\mathfrak{O}}}.

We construct many Segal morphisms in
Section

A.3
.
Many more are constructed in the following.

Proposition 2.0

(
[
BHS22
, Obs 4.1.14]
)
.

Fibrous patterns are strong Segal morphisms.

2.1.4.
The Segal envelope

In
[
BHS22
, Lem 4.2.4]
it was verified that a cocartesian fibration to
{\mathfrak{O}}
is a fibrous
{\mathfrak{O}}
-pattern if and only if it’s the straightening of a Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-category (assuming
{\mathfrak{O}}
is sound);
this lifts the fact that an operad
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category if and only if the corresponding functor
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\math@bb{F}}_{*}
is a cocartesian fibration.
We would like to describe adjunctions relating fibrous patterns to Segal objects, but to do so, we need a few constructions.

2.0
.

Given
{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}
a map of algebraic patterns, the
Segal envelope of
{\mathfrak{O}}
over
{\mathfrak{B}}
is the horizontal composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}{\mathfrak{O}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathfrak{B}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

where
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathfrak{B}})\subset\mathrm{Ar}({\mathfrak{B}})=\operatorname{Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathfrak{B}})
is the full subcategory spanned by active arrows and
s,t
are the
source and target
functors.
We denote the envelope of the terminal
{\mathfrak{B}}
-pattern as

\mathscr{A}_{\mathfrak{B}}:=\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathfrak{B}})\xrightarrow{t}{\mathfrak{B}}.

Given
f\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{O}}
a Segal morphism between algebraic patterns, we then define the composite functor

f^{\circledast}\colon\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}\xrightarrow{f^{*}}\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{/f^{*}\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}\xrightarrow{q^{*}}\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}}

where
q
is the map fitting into the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{*}\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

This participates in the following theorem, which was proved under a
strong Segal
assumption which is rendered unnecessary by
Section

2.1.3
.

Theorem 2.1

(
[
BHS22
, Prop 4.2.1,Prop 4.2.5,Thm 4.2.6,Rem 4.2.8]
)
.

Let
{\mathfrak{O}}
be a soundly extendable pattern.
Then,
\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}
participates in an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{O}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}(\mathrm{Cat}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Un}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

By taking slice categories, this induces an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{O}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}(\mathrm{Cat})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}^{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

whose left adjoint is fully faithful.
Furthermore, if
f\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
is a Segal morphism between soundly extendable patterns, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{O}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{O}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{P}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{P}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Un}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Un}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}^{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\circledast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Un}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Un}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

We will make frequent use of product patterns, so we observe their interaction with Segal envelopes.

2.1
.

If
{\mathfrak{O}},{\mathfrak{P}}
are fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-patterns, then their Segal envelopes satisfy

\displaystyle\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}})

\displaystyle\simeq\left({\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\right)\times_{{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}})

\displaystyle\simeq\left({\mathfrak{O}}\times_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathfrak{B}})\right)\times\left({\mathfrak{P}}\times_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathfrak{B}})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{O}})\times\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{P}})

We finish with a right handed construction which will be useful in
Section

3
.

2.1
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{B}}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\prime}
are soundly extendable algebraic patterns, modelled within quasicategories.

{\mathfrak{B}}
has an associated categorical pattern

\mathrm{CatPatt}({\mathfrak{B}})\coloneqq\left({\mathfrak{B}},\mathrm{inert},\mathrm{all},\left\{{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{O/}\right\}_{O\in{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)

Unwinding definitions, fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
patterns are presented by the model structure on
\mathrm{Set}^{+}_{\Delta,/\mathrm{CattPatt}({\mathfrak{B}})}
constructed in
[
HA
, Thm B.0.20]
.
In particular, we may apply
[
HA
, Rmk B.2.5]
to conclude that cartesian products furnish a distributive bifunctor

\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})\times\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}^{\prime});

the restriction
\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})\times\left\{{\mathfrak{B}}^{\prime}\right\}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}^{\prime})
is then seen to be both equivalent to pullback along the projection
p\colon{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}
and a left adjoint.
We will write
p_{*}
for its right adjoint.
The same result applies for Segal
{\mathfrak{B}}
-
\infty
-categories using the pattern

\mathrm{CatPatt}^{\otimes}({\mathfrak{B}})\coloneqq\left({\mathfrak{B}},\mathrm{all},\mathrm{all},\left\{{\mathfrak{B}}^{\mathrm{el}}_{O/}\right\}_{O\in{\mathfrak{B}}}\right).

2.2.
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads and
I
-operads

We’re finally ready to specialize to equivariant operads.
Fix
{\mathcal{T}}
an atomic orbital
\infty
-category.

2.1
.

The
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads is

\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}:=\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})).

More generally, when
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is a weak indexing category, the
\infty
-category of
I
-operads
is

\mathrm{Op}_{I}:=\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})).

The associated localization functors are
L_{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
and
L_{\mathrm{Op}_{I}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{/\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{I}
.

By
Section

2.1.3
, if
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is an
I
-operad, then it has a natural pattern structure such that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is a morphism of patterns;
the inert morphisms are cocartesian lifts of backwards maps, and the active maps are
arbitrary
lifts of forwards maps.

2.1
.

If
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
are
I
-operads, then an
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra in
{\mathcal{P}}
is a map of
I
-operads
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
;
the
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras in
{\mathcal{P}}
is written

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}).

2.1
.

It follows by unwinding definitions that
\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Op}_{I}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes})\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})^{\simeq}
.

The following proposition verifies that the pushforward functor
\mathrm{Op}_{I}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is simply given by postcomposition along the canonical functor
\iota_{I}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
(c.f.
[
NS22
, Ex 2.4.7]
).

Proposition 2.1
.

Let
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
be a pullback-stable replete subcategory.
Then, the functor

{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\left(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\xrightarrow{\pi_{I}}\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\right)

presents a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad if and only if
I
is a weak indexing category.

We will delay the proof of this until
Section

2.2.1
.
If
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}
arises from
Section

2.2
, we say that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a
weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}

{\mathcal{T}}
-operad
(or simply a weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operad), and if
I
is an indexing category, then we say that
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}
is an
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operad
; in either case, we write

\mathrm{CAlg}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}}({\mathcal{C}})

for the
\infty
-category of
I
-commutative algebras in
{\mathcal{C}}
.
This fits nicely into the theory of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads:

Corollary 2.1
.

Pushforward along
\iota_{I}^{{\mathcal{T}}}
yields an equivalence of
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{Op}_{I}\simeq\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},/{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}}
.

Proof.

Unwinding definitions, this is
Section

2.1.3
for
{\mathfrak{O}}\coloneqq{\mathcal{N}}^{\otimes}_{I\infty}
and
{\mathfrak{B}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
.
∎

In
Sections

2.5
and
2.5
we will show that the morphism
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathrm{Comm}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}
is monic, so pushforward
\mathrm{Op}_{I}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is fully faithful.
Until then, we will largely consider
\mathrm{Op}_{I}
and
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
separately.

2.1
.

The terminal
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad is presented by
\mathrm{Comm}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}}}=\left(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}}\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\right)
, and hence it is an
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operad;
we write
\mathrm{CAlg}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\mathrm{CAlg}_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}({\mathcal{C}})
, and call these
{\mathcal{T}}
-commutative algebras
.
For any
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
, pullback along the unique map
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathrm{Comm}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}
determines a unique natural transformation

\mathrm{CAlg}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}}),

so we view
{\mathcal{T}}
-commutative algebras as the universal
{\mathcal{T}}
-equivariant algebraic structure.

2.1
.

If
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is an
I
-operad, then the
\infty
-category of small
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal
\infty
-categories is

\mathrm{Cat}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathrm{Cat}).

If
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}
are
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal
\infty
-categories, then the
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal functors from
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
to
{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}
is

\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\left({\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}\right).

A
lax
{\mathcal{O}}
-symmetric monoidal functor
is a functor of
I
-operads
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}
over
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
.

In particular, we write
\mathrm{Cat}_{I}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}}^{\otimes}
and
\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\mathrm{Cat}_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}^{\otimes}
;

Section

A.1.2
constructs an equivalence

\mathrm{Cat}_{I}^{\otimes}\simeq\mathrm{CMon}_{I}(\mathrm{Cat}).

Note that a lax
I
-symmetric monoidal functor is an
I
-symmetric monoidal functor if and only if it is a morphism in
\mathrm{Cat}_{I}^{\otimes}
, i.e. if and only if it preserves cocartesian lifts for arbitrary maps in
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
.

2.1
.

Sections

2.2
and
2.2
appear to depend on
I
, but we omit
I
from our notation;
we will show in
Section

2.5
that
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathrm{Comm}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}
is monic, obviating this dependence.

The rest of this subsection proceeds through a series of vignettes.
In
Section

2.2.1
, we explicitly describe the structure of
I
-operads through the lens of their underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-categories and their
structure spaces
.
Following this, in
Section

2.2.2
, we summarize the comparison between
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads and
[
NS22
]
’s
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads, and we derive
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categorical lifts of
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
and
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})
.
Then, in
Section

2.2.3
, we summarize the specialization of the Segal envelope to
I
-operads.
Finally, in
Section

2.2.4
we describe the family of
trivial
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
, which form the left adjoint to the underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category.

2.2.1.
The structure of
I
-operads

The Segal conditions for fibrous
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
-patterns were characterized in
[
BHS22
]
in the case
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
;
we generalize this to weak indexing systems over general atomic orbital
\infty
-categories in
Section

A.1.2
, and summarize the results here.

2.1
.

Given
\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
an
I
-operad and
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
a finite
{\mathcal{T}}
-set, we define

{\mathcal{O}}_{S}:=\pi^{-1}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(S).

Inert arrows endow on
({\mathcal{O}}_{V})_{V\in{\mathcal{T}}}
the structure of a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category
U({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})
, formally given by the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tot}U({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

We call this the
underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
, and refer to it as
{\mathcal{O}}
when this won’t cause confusion.

Proposition 2.1
.

A functor
\pi:{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is an
I
-operad if and only if the following are satisfied:

(a)

{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
has
\pi
-cocartesian lifts for backwards maps in
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
;

(b)

(Segal condition for colors) for every
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{\mathcal{T}}
, cocartesian transport along the
\pi
-cocartesian lifts lying over the inclusions
\left(S\leftarrow U=U\mid U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)\right)
together induce an equivalence

{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}_{U};

(c)

(Segal condition for multimorphisms) for every map of orbits
T\rightarrow S
in
I
and pair of objects
(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{T}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
, postcomposition with the
\pi
-cocartesian lifts
\mathbf{D}\rightarrow D_{U}
lying over the inclusions

\left(S\leftarrow U=U\mid U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)\right)
induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Map}^{T\rightarrow S}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\operatorname{Map}^{T\leftarrow T_{U}\rightarrow U}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathbf{C},D_{U}).

where
T_{U}\coloneqq T\times_{S}U
.

Furthermore, a cocartesian fibration
\pi:{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is an
I
-operad if and only if its unstraightening
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is an
I
-symmetric monoidal category.

Proof.

Each of our conditions nearly matches with that of
Section

2.1.3
, with the exception being that we evaluate the limits on the sub-diagram
\mathrm{Orb}(S)\subset\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}
;
we show in
Section

A.1.1
that this is an initial subcategory, proving the proposition.
∎

2.1
.

Cocartesian lifts over backwards maps furnish an equivalence

\operatorname{Map}^{T\leftarrow T_{U}\rightarrow U}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathbf{C},D_{U})\simeq\operatorname{Map}^{T_{U}\rightarrow U}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathbf{C}_{T_{U}},D_{U}),

where
\mathbf{C}_{T_{U}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{U}}
is the
T_{U}
-tuple of colors underlying
\mathbf{C}
.
Hence in the presence of
Items

(a)
and
(b)
,
Item

(c)
may equivalently stipulate that the map

\operatorname{Map}^{T\rightarrow S}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\operatorname{Map}^{T_{U}\rightarrow U}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathbf{C}_{T_{U}},D_{U})

is an equivalence.
We will generally prefer this version, as the data of a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad is most naturally viewed as living over the
active
(i.e. forward) maps.

2.1
.

Practitioners of
[
HA
, Def 2.1.10]
should note that, by
Section

2.1.3
, we may weaken
Item

(b)
to assert only that cocartesian transport induces a
\pi_{0}
-surjection
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\rightarrow\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
.

We’re finally ready to prove
Section

2.2
.

Proof of
Section

2.2
.

Note that
Items

(IC-a)
and
(IC-c)
of
Section

1.2.1
are true by assumption (they were forced on us in order to make
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
definable).
We verify the conditions of
Section

2.2.1
for
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.

Note that
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
has
unique
lifts for backwards maps, so condition (a) follows always.
Furthermore,
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
always satisfies condition (b) by construction.
Lastly, by unwinding definitions and noting that there exists a map of spaces
X\rightarrow Y\times\varnothing=\varnothing
if and only if
X
is empty,
Section

1.2.1
implies that (c) is equivalent to
Item

(IC-b)
.
∎

Using
Section

2.2.1
, we gain access to the
structure spaces
of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.

2.1
.

Following
[
BP22
]
We will refer to tuples
(V\in{\mathcal{T}},S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V},(\mathbf{C};D)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{V})
as
{\mathcal{O}}
-profiles
, and we will often abusively refer to the profile
(V,S,(\mathbf{C};D))
as
(\mathbf{C};D)
.
Additionally, if
\psi\colon T\rightarrow S
is a map of finite
V
-sets, we will write
T_{U}\coloneqq T\times_{U}S
;
we refer to a
composable datum over
\psi
as the data of a
{\mathcal{O}}
-profile
(V,S,(\mathbf{C};D))
together with an
S
-tuple of profiles
\left((U,T_{U},(\mathbf{B}_{U},C_{U}))\right)_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}
;
in this case,
(\mathbf{B}_{U})_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}
assemble into a
T
-tuple
\mathbf{B}
, and we refer to
(V,T,(\mathbf{B};D))
as the
composite
{\mathcal{O}}
-profile
.

2.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
be a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad.
Given an
{\mathcal{O}}
-profile
(V,S,(\mathbf{C};D))
, we write

{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\coloneqq\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S\rightarrow V}(\mathbf{C},D).

Similarly, given
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
, we write

{\mathcal{O}}(S):=\coprodop\displaylimits_{(\mathbf{C},D)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{V}}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D);

we refer to this is the
space of
S
-ary operations in
{\mathcal{O}}
.

This simplifies in a particular setting:

2.1
.

A
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is:

•

at most one-colored
if
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}\in\left\{\varnothing,*\right\}
for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, i.e.
{\mathcal{O}}(*_{V})\in\left\{\varnothing,*\right\}
for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
,

•

at least one-colored
if
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}\neq\varnothing
for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, i.e.
{\mathcal{O}}(*_{V})\neq\varnothing
for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, and

•

one-colored
if
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is at least one-colored and at-most one colored.

We denote the associated full subcategories by
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{oc}}\subset\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\geq\mathrm{oc}},\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\leq\mathrm{oc}}\subset\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.

We acquire a simpler description for one-color
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads:
they are functors
\pi\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
with:

(a’)

cocartesian lifts of backwards maps,

(b’)

contractible fibers, and

(c’)

for whom cocartesian transport induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{T\rightarrow S}(iT,iS)\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(T_{U}),

where
\pi^{-1}(T)=\left\{iT\right\}
and
T_{U}=T\times_{S}U\rightarrow U
, considered as a
U
-set.

For most applications, algebras over one-color
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads suffice.
For now, we describe the general setting.

2.1
.

Given
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
and
\left(S,V,(\mathbf{C};D)\right)
an
{\mathcal{O}}
-profile, for any
T\leftarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S
, we have an equivalence

{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\simeq\operatorname{Map}^{T\leftarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S\rightarrow V}_{\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}}(\mathbf{C};D)

due to the existence of cocartesian lifts for inert morphisms.
Given a map
U\rightarrow V
in
{\mathcal{T}}
and a finite
V
-set
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
, postcomposition with the cocartesian lift of the backwards map
V\leftarrow U=U
yields a restriction map

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}\mathbf{C};\;\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}D\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}}^{\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S\rightarrow V}(\mathbf{C},D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}}^{\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S\leftarrow\mathrm{Ind}^{{\mathcal{T}}}_{V}S\times_{V}U\rightarrow U}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}\mathbf{C},\;\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}D\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the right hand side corresponds with the profile
\left(U,\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}S,\left(\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}\mathbf{C};\;\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}D\right)\right)
induced by restriction.

Moreover, given a composable datum
\left((\mathbf{C};D),(\mathbf{B}_{U};C_{U})_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\right)
lying over a map of
V
-sets
\varphi_{TS}\colon T\rightarrow S
, writing
\varphi_{TV}
for the structure map of
T
, composition in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
restricts to a map

(14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\times\displaystyle\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{B}_{U};C_{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{B};D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{SV}}(\mathbf{C},D)\times\operatorname{Map}_{\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}}^{\varphi_{TS}}(\mathbf{B},\mathbf{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{TV}}(\mathbf{B},D)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}

Lastly, define the
color preserving automorphism group
to be the subgroup
\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(\mathbf{C})\subset\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)
consisting of automorphisms
\sigma
such that
C_{U}\simeq C_{\sigma U}
for all
U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)
.
Note that
\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}
-cocartesian lifts of
\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(\mathbf{C})
preserve
\mathbf{C}
;
cocartesian transport then yields an action

(15)

\rho_{S}:\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(\mathbf{C})\times{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D).

We refer to
\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}
as
restriction
,
\gamma
as the
composition
, and
\rho_{S}
as
\Sigma
-action
.

2.1
.

Let
I
be a weak indexing category.
Recall the example
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}=\left(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\right)
of
Section

2.2
.
Then, it follows by definition that
U{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}\simeq*_{c(I)}
;
that is,
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}
always has at most one color, and it has one color if and only if
I
has one color in the sense of
[
Ste24a
]
.

Moreover, we have

{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}(S)\simeq\begin{cases}*&S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V};\\
\varnothing&S\notin{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}.\end{cases}

Each of the maps
\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V},\gamma,\rho_{S}
are uniquely determined by their domain and codomain.

For the following observation, we restrict to the one-color case simply for notational clarity;
the general case follows identically.

2.1
.

The structures of
Eqs.

13
,
14
and
15
are compatible in the following ways:

(1)

The restriction maps are Borel
\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)
-equivariant, i.e. the following commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{\text{cocart lifts of }\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)\right\}\times\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{SV}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iS,iV)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{SV}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iS,iV)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)\times{\mathcal{O}}(S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Aut}_{W}(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S)\times{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{\text{cocart lifts of }\operatorname{Aut}_{W}(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S)\right\}\times\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{SV}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(i\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S,iW)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{SV}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(i\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S,iW)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}

Here we write
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}=\left\{iS\right\}
.

(2)

The composition maps are Borel
\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)\times\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}{S}}\operatorname{Aut}_{U}(T_{U})
-equivariant in an analogous way.

(3)

The identity map on
*_{V}
yields an element
1_{V}\in*_{V}
which is taken to
1_{V}
by
\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}
.

(4)

The composition maps are unital, i.e. the following commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{SV}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iS,iV)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{SV}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iS,iV)\times\operatorname{Map}^{\operatorname{id}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iS,iS)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(S)\times\hskip-9.0pt\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(*_{U})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(*_{V})\times{\mathcal{O}}(S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}^{\operatorname{id}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iV,iV)\times\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{SV}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iS,iV)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{SV}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iS,iV)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{id},\left\{\operatorname{id}\right\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\left\{\operatorname{id}\right\},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{id},(\left\{1_{U}\right\}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\left\{1_{V}\right\},\operatorname{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}

(5)

The map
\gamma
is compatible with restriction, i.e. given a composable pair of morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{SV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{TS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{TV}}

and
W\rightarrow V
a map in
{\mathcal{T}}
, the following diagram commutes, where
T\coloneqq\coprodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}T_{U}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{SV}}(iS,iV)\times\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{TS}}(iT,iS)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{TV}}(iT,iV)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(S)\times\hskip-9.0pt\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(T_{U})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(T)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S\right)\times\hskip-9.0pt\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{U^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Orb}(\operatorname{Res}^{V}_{W}S)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(T_{U^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}T\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\varphi_{SV}}\left(i\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S,iW\right)\times\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\varphi_{TS}}(i\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}T,i\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\varphi_{TV}}(i\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}T,iW)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}

(6)

The composition maps are associative, i.e. given a collection of maps and composites

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}R}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{RT}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{RS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{RV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{TS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{TV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{SV}}

the following commutes, where
