:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{SV}}(iS,iV)\times\operatorname{Map}^{\varphi_{TS}}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(iT,iS)\times\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{RT}}(iR,iT)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{TV}}(iT,iV)\times\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}^{\varphi_{RT}}(iR,iT)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left({\mathcal{O}}(S)\times\hskip-9.0pt\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(T_{U})\right)\times\hskip-9.0pt\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}}{{W\in\mathrm{Orb}(T_{U})}}}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(R_{W})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(T)\times\hskip-9.0pt\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{W\in\mathrm{Orb}(T)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(R_{W})}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}\left(\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{W}^{T}R_{W}\right)}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circ}

Thus, passing to the homotopy category, the data of a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad supplies a discrete genuine
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad in
\mathrm{ho}{\mathcal{S}}
in the sense of
Section

2.7.1
.

2.2.2.
The
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads

Recall the map of algebraic patterns
\varphi\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
of
Eq.

12
.
By assumption, if
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a fibrous
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
-pattern, it possesses cocartesian lifts over
all
morphisms in the composite
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}
.
Thus, fibrous
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
-patterns possess total
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories
;
we refer to the associated functor as

\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

In
Sections

A.1.1
,
A.1.3
and
A.1.4
, we prove the following generalization of the contents of
[
BHS22
, §5.2]
, which identifies our
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads with those of
[
NS22
]
.

Proposition 2.1
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{T}}
is an atomic orbital
\infty
-category.
Then, pullback along
\varphi\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
implements equivalences of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\left(\mathrm{Cat}\right);

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\right).

Moreover,
\mathrm{Fbrs}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},}\right)
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-operads of
[
NS22
]
and
\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathrm{Cat})
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of small
{\mathcal{O}}
-symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories of
[
NS22
]
.

This enables us to make the following definition.

2.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
be
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads,
Then, the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras in
{\mathcal{P}}
is the full subcategory

\displaystyle\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}\left(\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right)

\displaystyle\subset\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}\left(\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right)

with
V
-values spanned by the
V
-functors
\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
preserving cocartesian lifts over inert arrows in
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{V,*}
.

We lift
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
to a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category by the following.

2.1
.

We show in
Section

A.3
that
\operatorname{Span}\left(\mathrm{Ind}_{U}^{V}\right)\colon\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{U})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})
is a Segal morphism for all maps
U\rightarrow V
in
{\mathcal{T}}
.
We refer to the resulting
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category

\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{(-)})}\mathrm{AlgPatt}^{\mathrm{SE},\mathrm{Seg},\operatorname{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Fbrs}}\mathrm{Cat}.

as the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads,
where
\mathrm{AlgPatt}^{\mathrm{SE},\mathrm{Seg}}\subset\mathrm{AlgPatt}
is the non-full subcategory of soundly extendable patterns and Segal morphisms.

2.1
.

The
V
-value of
\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{\mathcal{T}}
is
\mathrm{Op}_{V}\coloneqq\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{/V}}
;
the restriction functor
\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{V}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{U}
is implemented by the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V}).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

with bottom functor is
\operatorname{Span}(\mathrm{Ind}_{U}^{V})
.
Moreover,
\varphi\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is natural in
{\mathcal{T}}
, i.e. it yields a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{U,*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{U})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{V,*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})}}

Thus
\varphi^{*}
identifies
\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{V}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{W}
with pullback along
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{U,*}\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{V,*}
.

2.1
.

Via
Section

2.2.2
, we find that
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})
.
Furthermore, we find that

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})_{V}\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})}(\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes})\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}}(\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}})

with restriction functors induced by functoriality of
\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}
.
Moreover, applying
Section

2.2.2
and unwinding definitions yields an equivalence

\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\simeq\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

2.2.3.
Envelopes

In
[
NS22
]
, a left adjoint to the inclusion
\mathrm{CMon}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\mathrm{Cat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
was constructed, called the
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal envelope
.
This was greatly generalized by
Theorem

2.1
in view of
Section

2.2.2
.
For convenience, we spell this out here.

Corollary 2.1
.

If
{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads, then the following diagram consists of maps of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

and the top horizontal composition is an
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal
\infty
-category.
The corresponding functor

\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},/{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{O}}}

is left adjoint to the inclusion of
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal
\infty
-categories into
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads over
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
, and the induced functor

\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathcal{O}}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},/{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{O}},/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathcal{O}}}}^{\otimes}

is fully faithful, with image spanned by equifibrations in the sense of
[
BHS22
, Thm C]
.

We will simply write
\mathrm{Env}_{I}(-)\coloneqq\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}}(-)
and
\mathrm{Env}(-)\coloneqq\mathrm{Env}_{\mathrm{Comm}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}(-)
.

2.1
.

Let
I
be a weak indexing category.
Then, unwinding definitions, we find that

\mathrm{Env}_{I}{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}\simeq\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}^{I-\sqcup},

where
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I}^{I-\sqcup}\subset\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{T}}-\sqcup}
is the
I
-symmetric monoidal full
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory defined in
Section

1.2
, i.e. it is the
I
-symmetric monoidal subcategory generated by
\left\{*_{V}\mid V\in c(I)\right\}
.

We record a convenient property of
\mathrm{Env}_{I}(-)
here, which follows by unwinding definitions.

Lemma 2.1

(
[
HA
, Rmk 2.4.4.3]
)
.

If
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{I}
and
\psi\colon T\rightarrow S
is a map of
V
-sets, then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mor}^{\psi}_{\mathrm{Env}_{I}({\mathcal{O}})_{V}\rightarrow{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}}(\mathrm{Env}_{I}({\mathcal{O}})_{V})

\displaystyle\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{T}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}\operatorname{Map}^{\psi}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})

\displaystyle\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{T}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C}_{U};D_{U})

In particular, if
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
has one color, then

\operatorname{Map}^{\psi}_{\mathrm{Env}_{I}({\mathcal{O}})_{V}\rightarrow{\math@bb{F}}_{I,V}}(iT,iS)\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(T_{U}).

2.2.4.
Trivial
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads

We now construct a simple family of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads:

2.1
.

Given
{\mathcal{C}}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category, we define the
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad

\mathrm{triv}({\mathcal{C}})^{\otimes}\coloneqq L_{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\left(\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\right).

The following property follows by unwinding definitions.

Proposition 2.1
.

U
implements an equivalence

\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{triv}({\mathcal{C}})}({\mathcal{O}})\xrightarrow{\;\;\sim\;\;}\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}},U{\mathcal{O}});

in particular,
\mathrm{triv}(-)^{\otimes}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is a fully faithful left adjoint to
U
.

In order to state a corollary, set the notation
\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\coloneqq\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{\simeq}
, the latter denoting the
{\mathcal{T}}
-space core of
Section

1.1.4
.
We acquire the following identification from uniqueness of left adjoints and
[
NS22
, Cor 2.4.5]
.

Corollary 2.1
.

The equivalence
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*})
identifies
\mathrm{triv}({\mathcal{C}})^{\otimes}
with the trivial
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-operads of
[
NS22
, Cor 2.4.5]
;
in particular,
\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\mathrm{triv}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\simeq\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, and the functor
\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\mathrm{triv}({\mathcal{C}})^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\mathrm{triv}(*_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is unstraightened from the right Kan extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\mathrm{triv}({\mathcal{C}})}

2.3.
The underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequence

We now study a forgetful functor to
symmetric sequences
.
We begin in
Section

2.3.1
by defining the multi-colored variant of
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequences, then move on in
Section

2.3.2
to construct the underlying
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequence, verifying that it is monadic in the one-color setting.
We reframe this somewhat in
Section

2.3.3
to introduce the
n
-ary
B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}
-space
construction.

2.3.1.
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequences

2.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}
be a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category.
The
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequences in
{\mathcal{D}}
is
\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},{\mathcal{D}})
.
In the case
{\mathcal{C}}=\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, we refer to
\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},{\mathcal{S}})
simply as the
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequences
.

More generally, if
{\mathfrak{C}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-coefficient system of sets, we set the notation
\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})^{\otimes}
;
we refer to
\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},{\mathcal{D}})
and
\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},{\mathcal{S}})
as the
\infty
-categories of
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequences in
{\mathcal{D}}
and
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequences, respectively.

2.1
.

For any adequate triple
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{X}}_{b},{\mathcal{X}}_{f})
, the inclusion

{\mathcal{X}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{b,f}({\mathcal{X}})

induces an equivalence on cores.
In particular, choosing
(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{s.i.},\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
, we find that the inclusion
(-)_{+}:\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
induces an equivalence

\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\simeq}\simeq\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{\simeq}\simeq\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

In particular, unwinding definitions, we find that

\Sigma_{V}\coloneqq\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}},V}\simeq{\math@bb{F}}_{V}^{\simeq}\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}}B\operatorname{Aut}_{V}S

and the restriction map
\Sigma_{V}\rightarrow\Sigma_{W}
is induced by the forgetful maps
B\operatorname{Aut}_{V}S\rightarrow B\operatorname{Aut}_{W}S
.

We may similarly explicitly understand
\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}
.

2.1
.

A map of coefficient systems
{\mathfrak{C}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}
induces a map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{D}})^{\otimes}
, i.e. a cocartesian fibration of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories
\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\rightarrow\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
.
Applying this in the case
{\mathfrak{D}}=*_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, we acquire a canonical natural cocartesian fibration of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories

\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\rightarrow\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}};

taking
V
-values yields a natural cocartesian fibration

\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\rightarrow\Sigma_{V}

whose straightening has discrete values

S\mapsto{\mathfrak{C}}^{S}\times{\mathfrak{C}}^{V}

with functoriality along
S
-automorphisms given by permuting the factors of the
{\mathfrak{C}}^{S}
part (see
Section

2.2.4
).
In particular, the classical Grothendieck construction describes
\Sigma_{V}
as having;

•

Objects: profiles
(V,S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V},(\mathbf{C};D)\in{\mathfrak{C}}^{S}\times{\mathfrak{C}}^{V})
with orbit
V

•

Morphisms:
V
-equivariant automorphisms of
S
whose action fixes
\mathbf{C}
, i.e. color-preserving
S
-automorphisms.

In short, we find that

\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}=\coprodop\displaylimits_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}}}{{(\mathbf{C};D)\in{\mathfrak{C}}^{S}\times{\mathfrak{C}}^{V}}}}B\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(\mathbf{C}).

Moreover, unwinding definitions, we find that the restriction functor
\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\colon\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\rightarrow\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{C}},W}
is induced from the forgetful maps
B\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(\mathbf{C})\rightarrow B\operatorname{Aut}_{W}(\mathbf{C})
.

2.3.2.
The underlying
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequence

We will restrict to the following setting.

2.1
.

Given
{\mathfrak{C}}\colon{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Set}
a coefficient system of sets, we define the full subcategory of
{\mathfrak{C}}
-colored
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
as the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{{\mathfrak{C}}\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Coeff}^{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Set})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}U}

For instance,
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{oc}}=\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{*_{{\mathcal{T}}}}
as full subcategories.
Thus the following construction recovers an
underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequence
on one-color
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.

2.1
.

Given
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}
,
there is a structure map

\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})\simeq\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})^{\otimes}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})\rightarrow\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})^{\otimes},

where
\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})\subset\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})
is the full subcategory of active arrows whose codomain is elementary;
this is an inert-cocartesian fibration by pullback-stability of inert-cocartesian fibrations
[
BHS22
, Obs 2.1.7]
.
The
underlying
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequence of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is the unstraightening

{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{sseq}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\mathrm{Un}_{\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})}\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})\in\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},\mathrm{Cat}).

Unwinding definitions, we find that there exists a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\{(\mathbf{C};D)\right\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})^{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

so that
{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{sseq}}^{\otimes}
is indeed an
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequence.
The associated functor is denoted

\displaystyle\operatorname{sseq}\colon\mathrm{Op}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},{\mathcal{S}}).

We will often use the following to reduce questions about
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads to
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequences.

Proposition 2.1
.

Suppose a functor of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
\varphi:{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
satisfies the following conditions:

(a)

\varphi
induces surjective maps
\pi_{0}{\mathcal{O}}_{V}\rightarrow\pi_{0}{\mathcal{P}}_{V}
for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, and

(b)

for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, all
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
, all
\mathbf{C}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
, and all
D\in{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
, the map
\varphi
induces equivalences
\varphi:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi\mathbf{C};\varphi D)
.

Then
\varphi
is an equivalence of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads;
in particular, the functor

\operatorname{sseq}:\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},{\mathcal{S}})

is conservative.

In particular, specializing to
{\mathfrak{C}}=*_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, we find out that
\left\{{\mathcal{O}}(S)\mid S\in\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\right\}
is jointly conservative.
To prove this, we proceed by reduction to the following observation.

2.1
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}
is an equivalence of categories over
{\mathcal{E}}
, then it preserves and reflects cocartesian lifts of arrows in
{\mathcal{E}}
;
in particular, if
\varphi:{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
is a morphism of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads who induces an equivalence
\operatorname{Tot}\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
between the total
\infty
-categories of the associated functors to
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
, then its inverse is also a morphism of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.
Said another way, we’ve observed that the functor
U\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}
is fully faithful on cores, hence it is conservative, so
\operatorname{Tot}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is conservative.
Similar arguments show that
\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is conservative.

Proof of
Section

2.3.2
.

The second statement follows immediately from the first, since morphisms of
{\mathfrak{C}}
-colored
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads are
\pi_{0}
-isomorphisms by assumption.
Fixing
\varphi
satisfying (a) and (b), we will prove that
\varphi
is an equivalence of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.
Using
Section

2.3.2
, it suffices to prove that
\operatorname{Tot}\varphi
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

By the Segal condition for colors, we have an equivalence of arrows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prodop\displaylimits_{V\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\pi_{0}{\mathcal{O}}_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}{\mathcal{P}}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prodop\displaylimits_{V\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\pi_{0}{\mathcal{P}}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prodop\displaylimits\varphi_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Since
\pi_{0}{\mathcal{O}}\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{S}\pi_{0}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
, (a) implies that
\varphi
is essentially surjective.
Furthermore, the Segal condition for multimorphisms yields equivalences of arrows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip-9.0pt\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{f:\pi\mathbf{C}\leftarrow S\rightarrow\pi\mathbf{D}}\hskip-9.0pt\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{f}(\mathbf{C};\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{f}\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{V\in\mathrm{Orb}(\pi(D))}\hskip-9.0pt\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{f_{V}}(\mathbf{C}_{f^{-1}_{V}};D_{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{f}\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{V}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C}_{f^{-1}V};D_{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}}(\varphi\mathbf{C},\varphi\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{f:\pi\mathbf{C}\leftarrow S\rightarrow\pi\mathbf{D}}\hskip-9.0pt\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{P}}}^{f}(\varphi\mathbf{C};\varphi\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{f}\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{V\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\hskip-9.0pt\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{P}}}^{f^{\prime}}(\varphi\mathbf{C}_{f^{-1}V},\varphi D_{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{f}\prodop\displaylimits\limits_{V}{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi\mathbf{C}_{f^{-1}V};\varphi D_{V}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprodop\displaylimits\varphi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprodop\displaylimits\prodop\displaylimits\varphi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprodop\displaylimits\prodop\displaylimits\varphi(T_{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

the right arrow is an equivalence by (b), so the leftmost arrow is an equivalence, hence
\varphi
is fully faithful.
∎

The author learned the
U_{\circ}
portion of the following argument from Thomas Blom.

Corollary 2.1
.

The functor
\operatorname{sseq}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{oc}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},{\mathcal{S}})
is monadic and preserves sifted colimits.

Proof.

By
[
BHS22
, Cor 4.2.2]
,
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\operatorname{red}}
and
\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},{\mathcal{S}})
are presentable, so by Barr-Beck
[
HA
, Thm 4.7.3.5]
and the adjoint functor theorem
[
HTT
, Cor 5.5.2.9]
, it suffices to prove that
\operatorname{sseq}
is conservative and preesrves limits and sifted colimits.
Conservativity is
Section

2.3.2
, and (co)limits in functor categories are computed pointwise by
[
HTT
, Prop 5.1.2.2]
, so it suffices to prove that
{\mathcal{O}}\mapsto{\mathcal{O}}(S)
preseres limits and sifted colimits.
We separate this into manageable chunks via the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Int-cocart,core-iso}}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{core-iso}}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{core-iso}}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Fun}\left(\left(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})^{\simeq}\right)^{\times 2},{\mathcal{S}}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}\mapsto{\mathcal{O}}(S)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\mathrm{Seg}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\mathrm{cocart}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\circ}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S,V}}

\pi
and
\operatorname{ev}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S,V}
preserve (co)limits since they are evaluation of functor categories
[
HTT
, Prop 5.1.2.2]
.

U_{\mathrm{Cocart}}
preserves limits and sifted colimits by
[
BHS22
, Cor 2.1.5]
.

U_{\mathrm{Seg}}
preserves limits and sifted colimits, as each commute with finite products.

\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}
preserves limits and sifted colimits, as it is a left and right adjoint.

By
[
Hau20
, Prop 3.12]
,
U_{\circ}
is equivalent to the forgetful functor

\mathrm{Alg}({\mathcal{S}}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\simeq},\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\simeq}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\simeq},\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\simeq}},

where
{\mathcal{S}}^{\otimes}_{/Y,Y}
is a monoidal structure on
{\mathcal{S}}_{/Y,Y}\simeq{\mathcal{S}}_{Y\times Y}\simeq\operatorname{Fun}(Y\times Y,{\mathcal{S}})
.
This functor preserves limits and sifted colimits by
[
HA
, Prop 3.2.3.1]
, completing the argument.
∎

In particular, this constructs a left adjoint

\operatorname{Fr}:\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})=\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{oc}}

to
\operatorname{sseq}
.
We lift this to a
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjunction in the following construction.

2.1
.

The functor
\operatorname{sseq}
is associated with a
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
\underline{\operatorname{sseq}}
as in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{oc}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}},\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\left(\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\Lambda_{2}^{2},\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\right)\times_{\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\left\{\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\left(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\left(\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\Delta_{1}^{2},\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\right)\times_{\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\left\{\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\operatorname{sseq}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\operatorname{sseq}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\in}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.0549,0.8}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

By
[
HA
, Prop 7.3.2.1]
, the pointwise left adjoints
\operatorname{Fr}
lifts to a
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjunction

\underline{\operatorname{sseq}}:\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{oc}}\leftrightarrows\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}):\underline{\operatorname{Fr}},

i.e.
\underline{\operatorname{Fr}}
is compatible with restriction.

2.3.3.
Other persiectives on
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequences

2.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{G\times\Sigma_{n},\Gamma_{n}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{G\times\Sigma_{n}}
be the full subcategory spanned by
[G\times\Sigma_{n}/\Gamma_{S}]
for
\phi_{S}:H\rightarrow\Sigma_{n}
a map with associated graph subgroup
\Gamma_{S}=\left\{(h,\phi_{S}(h))\mid h\in H\right\}\subset H\times\Sigma_{n}\subset G\times\Sigma_{n}
.
This possesses an evident forgetful functor
{\mathcal{O}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{G\times\Sigma_{n}/\Gamma_{S}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{G}
taking
[G\times\Sigma_{n}/\Gamma_{S}]\rightarrow[G/H]
;
in
[
NS22
, Ex 4.3.7]
, this was shown to be a cocartesian fibration factoring through an equivalence

\coprodop\displaylimits_{n\in{\math@bb{N}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{G\times\Sigma_{n}/\Gamma_{S}}\simeq\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{G}^{\operatorname{op}}

taking
[G\times\Sigma_{n}/\Gamma_{S}]\mapsto(H,S)
, and hence taking the
G
-space presented by
{\mathcal{O}}_{G\times\Sigma_{n}/\Gamma_{S}}
equivalently onto the summand
B_{G}\Sigma_{n}\subset\underline{\Sigma}_{G}
, the classifying
G
-space for equivariant principle
\Sigma_{n}
-bundles.

More generally, we say that an atomic orbital
\infty
-categories
{\mathcal{T}}
is
EI
if the inclusions
\operatorname{Aut}(V)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{End}(V)
are equivalences.
If
{\mathcal{T}}
is EI and admits a weakly initial object
e\in{\mathcal{T}}
, we may define the
{\mathcal{T}}
-subspace
B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}\subset\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
as corresponding with the
{\mathcal{T}}
-sets
S
whose restriction
\operatorname{Res}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}S
is a set with
n
elements;
then, we acquire a splitting

\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{n\in{\math@bb{N}}}B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}.

Under the above equivalence, given
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{oc}}
, we define the
n
-ary
B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}
-space

\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(n)\colon B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}\subset\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{sseq}{\mathcal{O}}}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

For instance, if
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
is a family of subgroups, then
{\mathcal{F}}
is EI with a weakly initial object, and
B_{{\mathcal{F}}}\Sigma_{n}
is the classifying
{\mathcal{F}}
-space for
{\mathcal{F}}
-genuine
G
-equivariant principal
\Sigma_{n}
-bundles.
In the case
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
,
\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(n)
is characterized by its
\Gamma_{S}
-fixed points
\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(n)^{\Gamma_{S}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(S)
, with restriction functors along
\Gamma_{\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}S}\subset\Gamma_{S}
corresponding with restriction map
{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}S)
.

We will see in
Section

2.7.3
that this intertwines with a nerve functor from operad objects in topological
G
-spaces.
We will also need the following notation.

2.1
.

Given an orbit
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, and a finite
V
-set
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
, we may define a natural “
S
-ary”
V
-space in
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequences

\underline{(-)}(S)\colon\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}_{V}(\underline{\Sigma}_{V},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{V})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}_{S}}{\mathcal{S}}_{V}.

More generally, we may define the analogous
V
-space for an
{\mathcal{O}}
-profile:

\underline{(-)}(\mathbf{C};D)\colon\mathrm{Op}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}_{V}(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}},V},\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{V})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}_{(\mathbf{C};D)}}{\mathcal{S}}_{V}.

2.4.
The monad for
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras

We now take a detour into studying the free
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra monad.
Our main application for this is the following theorem.

Theorem 2.2

(“Equivariant
[
HM23
, Thm 4.1.1]
”)
.

A map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
is an equivalence if and only if it satisfies the following conditions:

(a)

the
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
U(\varphi)\colon{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
is essentially surjective, and

(b)

the pullback functor
\varphi^{*}\colon\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Fix
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
a one-object
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad, fix
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
a distributive
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal category in the sense of
Section

1.3
(e.g. it may be presentably
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal) and let
\mathrm{triv}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
be the functor of operads associated with a
{\mathcal{T}}
-object
X\in\Gamma{\mathcal{C}}
.
Denote by
X^{\otimes}\colon\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
the associated
{\mathcal{O}}
-symmetric monoidal functor, and denote by

{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{sseq}}(X)\colon\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}

the underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor.
Given
Y
a
V
-space and
X\in{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
, we will write
Y\cdot X
for the indexed colimit of the constant
Y
-indexed diagram
Y\rightarrow*_{V}\rightarrow\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}
at
X
.

Proposition 2.2

(“Equivariant
[
SY19
, Lem 2.4.2]
”)
.

The forgetful
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
U\colon\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
is monadic, and the associated monad
T_{{\mathcal{O}}}
acts on
X\in\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}
by the indexed colimit

\displaystyle T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X

\displaystyle\coloneqq\underline{\operatorname{colim}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{sseq}}(X),

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\operatorname{colim}}_{S\in\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(S)\cdot X^{\otimes S}.

Proof.

Monadicity is precisely
[
NS22
, Cor 5.1.5]
, so it suffices to compute the associated monad.
By
[
NS22
, Rem 4.3.6]
, the left adjoint
\operatorname{Fr}\colon{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})
is computed on
X
by
{\mathcal{T}}
-operadic left Kan extension of the corresponding map
\mathrm{triv}^{\otimes}\xrightarrow{X}{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
along the canonical inclusion
\mathrm{triv}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
, and the underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor of this is computed by the composite
{\mathcal{T}}
-left Kan extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{*_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{T}_{{\mathcal{O}}}X}

{\mathcal{T}}
-left Kan extension diagrams to
*_{{\mathcal{T}}}
are
{\mathcal{T}}
-colimit diagrams by definition, so the underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-object is

T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X\simeq\underline{\operatorname{colim}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{sseq}}(X).

Additionally, the
{\mathcal{T}}
-left Kan extension
\widetilde{T}_{{\mathcal{O}}}X
has values given by the indexed colimit

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{{\mathcal{O}}}X(S)

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\operatorname{colim}}_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}(T,x\in{\mathcal{O}}(T))\rightarrow S}X^{\otimes T};

in fact, the inclusion
\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(S)\simeq\left\{S\right\}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}})\subset\left(\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}})\right)^{/\underline{S}}
is
{\mathcal{T}}_{/V}
-final, so it induces an equivalence

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{{\mathcal{O}}}X(S)

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\operatorname{colim}}_{x\in\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(S)}X^{\otimes S}

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(S)\cdot X^{\otimes S}

and the result follows by composition of
{\mathcal{T}}
-left Kan extensions.
∎

2.2
.

In view of
Section

2.3.3
, we may rewrite
Section

2.4
in the case
{\mathcal{T}}
is EI with a weakly initial object as

T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{n\in{\math@bb{N}}}\underline{\operatorname{colim}}_{S\in B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}}\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(S)\cdot X^{\otimes S}.

We would like to interpret this in a more traditional way, so define the
B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}
-space
X^{n}
by

X^{n}\colon B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}\subset\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\operatorname{op}}\xrightarrow{S\mapsto X^{S}}\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

In the case
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
, this is characterized by its graph subgroup fixed points
\left(X^{n}\right)^{\Gamma_{S}}\simeq X^{S}
.
The
B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}
-space corresponding with
S\mapsto\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(S)\cdot X^{\otimes n}
is
\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(n)\cdot X^{n}
.
Using the notation
\left(-\right)_{h_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}}
for
B_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}
-indexed colimits, we may then write the formula

T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{n\in{\math@bb{N}}}\left(\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(n)\times X^{n}\right)_{h_{{\mathcal{T}}}\Sigma_{n}}.

For instance, when
{\mathcal{O}}={\math@bb{E}}_{V}^{\otimes}
, one may check that this agrees with the monad
\math@bb{K}_{V}
for free algebras over the
V
-Steiner operad considered in
[
GM17
]
, so it satisfies an approximation theorem to
{}^{V}\Sigma^{V}
.

By
[
NS22
, Prop 3.2.5]
, the Cartesian
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal structure on
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})
is distributive whenever
{\mathcal{C}}
is a cocomplete Cartesian closed category.
In this setting, we may easily characterize the associated monad.

Corollary 2.2
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
a cocomplete cartesian closed
\infty
-category.
Then, the forgetful functor

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Coeff}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})

is monadic, and the associated monad
T_{{\mathcal{O}}}
has fixed points

\displaystyle\left(T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X\right)^{V}

\displaystyle\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}}\left({\mathcal{O}}(S)\cdot\left(X^{S}\right)^{V}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)}

\displaystyle\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}}\left({\mathcal{O}}(S)\cdot\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}X^{U}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)}

Proof.

This follows from
Section

2.4
by combining the fixed points of indexed colimits formula of
Section

1.1.2
with the description of
\Sigma_{V}
in
Section

2.3.1
.
∎

In fact, we may say more;
on the summand corresponding with
S
, the restriction map on
(T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X)^{V}\rightarrow(T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X)^{U}
is induced from the restriction map

{\mathcal{O}}(S)\cdot\left(X^{S}\right)^{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}S)\cdot\left(X^{S}\right)^{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}S)\cdot\left(X^{\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}}S\right)^{U}

Corollary 2.2
.

The functor
\mathrm{Alg}_{(-)}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\mathrm{oc}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is conservative.

Proof.

Suppose
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
.
Then
\varphi
induces a natural equivalence
T_{{\mathcal{O}}}\implies T_{{\mathcal{P}}}
respecting the summand decomposition in
Section

2.4
.
Choosing
X=S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
, note that the
V
-equivariant automorphisms embed as a summand
\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)\subset\operatorname{End}_{V}(S)\simeq\left(S^{S}\right)^{V}
, yielding a natural coproduct decomposition

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{O}}(S)\times\left(S^{\times S}\right)^{V}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}_{V}S}

\displaystyle\simeq\left({\mathcal{O}}(S)\times\operatorname{Aut}_{V}S\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}_{V}S}\sqcup J_{{\mathcal{O}},S}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(S)\sqcup J_{{\mathcal{O}},S}

for some
J_{{\mathcal{O}},S}
;
hence the summand-preserving equivalence
T_{\varphi}\colon T_{{\mathcal{O}}}S\implies T_{{\mathcal{P}}}S
implies that
\varphi(S)\colon{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}(S)
is an equivalence for all
S
, i.e.
\operatorname{sseq}\varphi\colon\operatorname{sseq}{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\operatorname{sseq}{\mathcal{P}}
is an equivalence of
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric sequences.
Thus
Section

2.3.2
implies that
\varphi
is an equivalence.
∎

2.2
.

Section

2.4
agrees with the free Segal
\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
-object monad of
[
CH21
, Cor 8.12]
in view of
Sections

A.1.2
,
A.1.2
and
A.1.2
, so
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
-algebras in the Cartesian structure on
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}
are interpretable as
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
-monoids
(c.f.
[
HA
, Prop 2.4.2.5]
in view of
Sections

A.1.1
and
A.1.4
).
We do not study this further at present, as we will cover the general Cartesian case in forthcoming work
[
Ste24
]
.

To finish the section, we repeat the above work without the one-color assumption.

2.2
.

Analogously to
[
HM23
]
, let
f\colon{\mathfrak{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}
be a
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor from a coefficient system of sets, and let
\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
be the corresponding map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.
Then,
[
NS22
, Thm 5.1.4]
constructs a left
{\mathcal{T}}
-adjoint to the pullback functor
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{D}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Fun}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathfrak{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
, whose associated
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor has value on the
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
X
given by the
{\mathcal{T}}
-left Kan extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathrm{triv}({\mathfrak{C}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\mathrm{Ar}^{\mathrm{act},/\mathrm{el}}({\mathcal{O}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T}_{{\mathcal{O}}}X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X}}}}

By an analogous argument to
Section

2.4
, we have

\widetilde{T}_{{\mathcal{O}}}X(\mathbf{C},D)\simeq\underline{{\mathcal{O}}}(f\mathbf{C};fD)\cdot\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{U}^{\pi f\mathbf{C}}X_{C_{U}};

moreover, when
{\mathcal{D}}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})
for
{\mathcal{C}}
a cocomplete cartesian closed
\infty
-category, we have

\displaystyle\left(T_{{\mathcal{O}}}X\right)_{D}^{V}

\displaystyle\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{(\mathbf{C},D)\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}}\left({\mathcal{O}}(f\mathbf{C};fD)\times\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\pi\mathbf{C}}X_{C_{U}}^{U}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\mathbf{C}}.

Momentarily choose
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{S}}
, and note that, if
X_{C_{U}}=\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}S
for
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
for all
U
, then

\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\pi\mathbf{C}}X_{C_{U}}^{U}\simeq\left(S^{\times\pi\mathbf{C}}\right)^{V}\simeq\operatorname{Map}^{V}(\pi\mathbf{C},S),

with
\operatorname{Aut}_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\mathbf{C}
-action given by the composite map
\operatorname{Aut}_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\mathbf{C}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{V}(\mathbf{C})\rightarrow\operatorname{End}\operatorname{Map}^{V}(\pi\mathbf{C},S)
.
In particular, choosing
X
to be the functor
{\mathfrak{C}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Coeff}^{V}({\mathcal{S}})
which is constant at
\pi\mathbf{C}
,
we acquire a natural equivalence

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{O}}(f\mathbf{C};fD)\times\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\pi\mathbf{C}}X_{C_{U}}^{U}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle\simeq\left({\mathcal{O}}(f\mathbf{C};fD)\times\left(\pi\mathbf{C}^{\times\pi\mathbf{C}}\right)^{V}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle\simeq\left({\mathcal{O}}(f\mathbf{C};fD)\times\operatorname{Aut}_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\mathbf{C}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}_{{\mathfrak{C}},V}\mathbf{C}}\sqcup J_{{\mathcal{O}},(\mathbf{C};D)}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(f\mathbf{C};fD)\sqcup J_{{\mathcal{O}},(\mathbf{C};D)}

for some object
J_{{\mathcal{O}},(\mathbf{C};D)}\in{\mathcal{S}}
.

Now, note that there is a unique symmetric monoidal functor
\operatorname{Fr}_{{\mathcal{C}}}\colon{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
under the Cartesian structure, and the induced map
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})
preserves fiberwise products and indexed coproducts.
In particular, we acquire a natural splitting

(16)

\operatorname{Fr}_{{\mathcal{C}}}{\mathcal{O}}(f\mathbf{C};fD)\sqcup J^{\prime}\simeq T_{{\mathcal{O}}}\operatorname{Fr}_{{\mathcal{C}}}(\underline{\pi\mathbf{C}}).

We now conclude a proof of
Theorem

2.2
.
When
\varphi
is an equivalence, conditions (a) and (b) are obvious,
Conversely, assume conditions (a) and (b);
it suffices to argue that
\varphi(\mathbf{C};D)\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi\mathbf{C};\varphi D)
is an equivalence for all
(\mathbf{C};D)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
by
Section

2.3.2
.
This follows from the following stronger proposition.

Proposition 2.2
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
is a presentable and cartesian closed
\infty
-category and
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
a map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads whose pullback functor
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}(\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))
is an equivalence.
Then, for all
(\mathbf{C};D)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
, the induced map

\operatorname{Fr}_{{\mathcal{C}}}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\rightarrow\operatorname{Fr}_{{\mathcal{C}}}{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi\mathbf{C};\varphi D)

is an equivalence, where
\operatorname{Fr}_{{\mathcal{C}}}\colon{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
is the (unique) left adjoint sending
*\in{\mathcal{S}}
to the terminal object of
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof.

We will study the sequence of adjunctions on algebras in
{\mathcal{T}}
-spaces associated with the sequence

\mathrm{triv}(\pi_{0}{\mathcal{O}})^{\otimes}\xrightarrow{\gamma}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\xrightarrow{\varphi}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}

using
Section

2.4
.
Condition (b) guarantees that
\varphi^{*}
is an equivalence
over
\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\pi_{0}{\mathcal{O}},\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}(\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\pi_{0}{\mathcal{O}},\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\gamma\varphi\right)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}

this induces a natural equivalence between the associated monads for
(\gamma\varphi)^{*}
and
\gamma^{*}
respecting the splitting of
Eq.

16
for each
(\mathbf{C},D)
, and hence yields an equivalence
\varphi\colon\operatorname{Fr}_{{\mathcal{C}}}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\rightarrow\operatorname{Fr}_{{\mathcal{C}}}{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi\mathbf{C};\varphi\mathbf{D})
, as desired.
∎

2.5.
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras in
I
-symmetric monoidal
d
-categories

Recall that a space
X
is said to be
d
-truncated
if it is empty or
\pi_{n}(X,x)=*
for all
x\in X
and
n>d
;
in particular,
X
is
(-1)
-truncated precisely if it is either empty or contractible.
In
Section

1.4
, we applied this to mapping spaces to define
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
d
-categories
.
In this section, we define a compatible notion of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-operads
, centered on the following result.

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
be a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad and let
d\geq-1
.
Then, the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

{\mathcal{O}}(S)
is
d
-truncated for all
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
.

(b)

The
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
has
d
-truncated mapping fibers.

Proof.

Let
\psi\colon T\rightarrow S
be a map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-sets over
V
.
Then, by
Section

2.2.3
, we have a equivalences

(17)

\begin{split}\operatorname{Mor}^{\psi}_{\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}(\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}})&\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{\mathbf{C}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{T},\mathbf{D}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}\operatorname{Map}^{\psi}_{\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})\\
&\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{\mathbf{C}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{T},\mathbf{D}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\operatorname{Map}^{\psi}_{\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}(\mathbf{C}_{U},D_{U})\\
&\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{\mathbf{C}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{T},\mathbf{D}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C}_{U};D_{U}),\end{split}

natural in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
.
First, in the case
d=-1
, note that conditions (a) and (b) both imply that
{\mathcal{O}}
has at most one color, so
Eq.

17
specializes to

\operatorname{Mor}^{\psi}_{\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}(\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}})\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(S).

Thus it suffices to note that a product is
-1
-truncated if and only if its factors are.

Next, in the case
d\geq 0
, note that a coproduct of spaces is
d
-truncated if and only if its factors are;
hence
Eq.

17
shows that (b) is equivalent to the condition that
\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C}_{U};D_{U})
is
d
-truncated for all
S,\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D}
.
In fact, the equation

{\mathcal{O}}(S)\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{(\mathbf{C},D)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{V}}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)

shows that this (b) equivalent to the condition that
{\mathcal{O}}(S)
is
d
-truncated for all
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
, as desired.
∎

We define the full subcategory of
d
-operads

\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}\subset\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

to be spanned by
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads satisfying the condition that
{\mathcal{O}}(S)
is
(d-1)
-truncated for all
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
as in
Section

2.5
.
The following corollary follows from
Section

2.5
and the mapping fiber truncation characterizations of
Section

1.4
.

Corollary 2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
be a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad and let
d\geq 1
.
The following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-operad, and

(b)

\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
d
-category.

Furthermore, the following conditions are equivalent:

(a’)

{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
0
-operad, and

(b’)

the
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is a replete
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory inclusion.

In general, these form a well-behaved subcategory.

Corollary 2.2
.

The inclusion
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
has a left adjoint
h_{d}
satisfying

\left(h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}\right)(S)\simeq\tau_{\leq d-1}{\mathcal{O}}(S).

Furthermore, when
d\geq 1
, this fits into the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{d}}

In particular, when
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
d
-category, the canonical map
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}}).

Proof.

By
[
BHS22
, Prop 4.2.1]
, the image of the fully faithful functor
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{T}}-\sqcup}}^{\otimes}
is spanned by the
equifibered

{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories, i.e.
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
such that, given
T\rightarrow S
a map of finite
{\mathcal{T}}
-sets, the associated diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{C}}_{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{C}}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\math@bb{F}}_{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\math@bb{F}}_{S}}}

is cartesian.
We separately argue in the case
d\geq 1
and
d=0
that the image of this is closed under
h_{{\mathcal{T}},d}
;
this will imply that
h_{d}\mathrm{Env}^{/\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
corresponds with a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-operad
h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
, which computes the left adjoint to the inclusions
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}\subset\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
by fully faithfulness of
\mathrm{Env}^{/\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
.
In particular, the counit
\varepsilon\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
will induce the counit
\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow h_{d}\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{O}}
, which by
Eq.

17
shows that
{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}(S)
is the Postnikov
(d-1)
-truncation map.

To prove compatibility with equifibrations, we first consider the case
d\geq 1
.
In this case, since
h_{{\mathcal{T}},d}:\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}^{\otimes}
is applied pointwise, it preserves equifibrations, so
h_{{\mathcal{T}},d}\mathrm{Env}^{/\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
corresponds with a
d
-operad
h_{{\mathcal{T}},d}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
.

The case
d=0
is similar, except that we are tasked with replacing equifibered
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal functors with an equifibered (replete) subcategory.
In fact, replete subcategories are precisely
(-1)
-truncated maps in
\mathrm{Cat}_{1}
, so we may do this by taking the pointwise
(-1)
-truncation functor and applying
[
HTT
, Prop 5.5.6.5]
to see that the result is equifibered.
∎

We acquire a simple lift of
[
BH15
, Prop 5.5]
.

Corollary 2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
be a
{\mathcal{T}}
-
d
-operad.

(1)

if
d\geq 1
, then
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})
is a
d
-category; hence
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}
is a
(d+1)
-category.

(2)

if
d=0
, then
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})
is either empty or contractible;
hence
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},0}
is a poset.

Proof.

In each case, the second statement follows from the first by noting that the mapping spaces in
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
are
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})^{\simeq}
.
For the first statements, note that

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{T}}-\sqcup}}(\mathrm{Env}h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes});

if
d\geq 1
, then this is a subcategory of a
d
-category, so it’s a
d
-category.
If
d=0
, then this category is either empty or contractible since we verified that the map
\mathrm{Env}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{T}}-\sqcup}
is monic.
∎

Corollary 2.2
.

{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-0-operad if and only if it’s a sub-terminal object of
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.

Proof.

The mapping space criterion of monomorphisms shows that this is equivalent to the condition that

\mathrm{Alg}_{h_{0}{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})^{\simeq}\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})^{\simeq}\rightarrow\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\mathrm{Comm}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes})^{\simeq}\simeq*

is a monomorphism, i.e.
\mathrm{Alg}_{h_{0}{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})^{\simeq}\in\left\{\varnothing,*\right\}
;
this follows from
Section

2.5
.

On the other hand,
Section

2.3.2
(together with Kan extensions) constructs a
free
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad on
*_{S}
characterized by the property

\mathrm{Alg}_{\operatorname{Fr}_{S}(*)}({\mathcal{O}})^{\simeq}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(S);

thus the mapping space criterion for a subterminal
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
implies that
{\mathcal{O}}(S)
is either empty or contractible for all
S
, so
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-0-operad.
∎

Corollary 2.2
.

Let
I\leq J
be related weak indexing categories.
Then, the unslicing functor

\mathrm{Op}_{I}\simeq\mathrm{Op}_{J,/{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{J}

is fully faithful.

Proof.

Fully faithful functors satisfy two-out-of-three, so we may replace
\mathrm{Op}_{I}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{J}
with the composite unslicing functor
\mathrm{Op}_{I}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{J}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, and assume
I={\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
The corollary is then equivalent to the statement that
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathrm{Comm}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}
is a monomorphism
[
HTT
, § 5.5.6]
.
In fact, by
Section

2.2.1
,
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-0-operad, so this follows from
Section

2.5
.
∎

We finish the subsection with a recognition result for
h_{d}
-equivalences;
we say that a map
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
is an
(d-1)
-equivalence
if any of the following equivalent conditions hold.

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
be a morphism of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.
Then, the following are equivalent:

(a)

The underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
U\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
is essentially surjective and for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, the induced map
\tau_{\leq(d-1)}{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow\tau_{\leq(d-1)}{\mathcal{P}}(S)
is an equivalence.

(b)

\varphi
is an
h_{d}
-equivalence.

(c)

The underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
U\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
is essentially surjective and for all
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
d
-categories
{\mathcal{C}}
, the pullback
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal functor

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})

is an equivalence.

(d)

The underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
U\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
is essentially surjective and the pullback functor

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq d-1})\rightarrow\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq d-1})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Suppose (a);
in view of
Section

2.3.2
, to prove (b), we’re tasked with proving that the maps
h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\rightarrow h_{d}{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbf{C};D)
are equivalences.
But by the natural equivalence

{\mathcal{O}}(S)\simeq\coprodop\displaylimits_{(\mathbf{C},D)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\times{\mathcal{O}}_{V}}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D),

it suffices to verify that
h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow h_{d}{\mathcal{P}}(S)
is an equivalence for each
S
.
This follows from (a) by
Section

2.5
.

Suppose (b);
by the factorization

\mathrm{Cat}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

of
Section

2.5
, given
{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathrm{Cat}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{T}},d}
, the top map in the following is an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{h_{d}{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{C}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{h_{d}{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{C}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

the bottom arrow is an equivalence from two-out-of-three, and (c) follows from
Section

2.1.1
.
Furthermore, (c) implies (d) by setting
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\underline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq d-1}^{{\mathcal{T}}-\times}
.

Suppose (d).
The unique symmetric monoidal left adjoint
{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}_{\leq d-1}
is
\tau_{\leq d-1}
, so
Section

2.4
implies that
\tau_{\leq d-1}{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow\tau_{\leq d-1}{\mathcal{P}}(S)
is an equivalence, i.e. (a).
∎

2.6.
Arity support and Borelification

We now introduce a support stratification in terms of
arity
.

2.2
.

Given
{\mathcal{O}}\in\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, the
arity support of
{\mathcal{O}}
is the collection of maps
A{\mathcal{O}}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
defined by

A{\mathcal{O}}:=\left\{\psi:T\rightarrow S\;\;\middle|\;\;\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(T\times_{U}S)\neq\varnothing\right\}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

2.2
.

There exists no map of spaces
X_{1}\times X_{2}\rightarrow Y_{1}\times Y_{2}
if and only if
X_{1},X_{2}\neq\varnothing
and
Y_{i}=\varnothing
for some
i
.
In particular,

•

the existence of the composition map
\operatorname{Map}^{\psi}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(T;S)\times\operatorname{Map}^{\psi^{\prime}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(R;T)\rightarrow\operatorname{Map}^{\psi\circ\psi^{\prime}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(R;S)
implies that
A{\mathcal{O}}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is closed under composition;

•

existence of identity operations implies that
A{\mathcal{O}}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
contains identity arrows of its elements; and

•

functoriality of
\operatorname{Map}_{(-)}^{\psi}(T;S)
implies that
A
forms a functor

A\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Sub}_{\mathrm{Cat}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}).

We will frequently compute
A
by noting that it factors as

A\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{sseq}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathcal{T}}},{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Sub}_{\mathrm{Cat}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}).

2.2
.

For all
I\in\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, we have
A{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}=I
, so
\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset A(\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
.

Proposition 2.2
.

For all
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, the subcategory
A{\mathcal{O}}\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is a weak indexing category;
hence

A(\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}})=\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\subset\operatorname{Sub}_{\mathrm{Cat}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}).

Proof.

The second statement follows from the first by
Section

2.6
, so it suffices to prove that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
satisfies
Items

(IC-a)
,
(IC-b)
and
(IC-c)
.
Indeed,
Item

(IC-a)
follows by unwinding definitions using existence of the arity restriction map of
Eq.

13
.
Similarly,
Item

(IC-b)
follows immediately by definition.
Lastly,
Item

(IC-c)
follows by existence of the
\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)
-action of
Eq.

15
.
∎

Corollary 2.2
.

A
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-0-operad if and only if it’s a weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operad.

Proof.

By
Section

2.2.1
,
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-0-operad, so fix
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-0-operad.
By definition,
\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}
factors as

{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\xrightarrow{can}\operatorname{Span}_{A{\mathcal{O}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),

i.e. there is a map
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{A{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}
.
Moreover, for all
S
, there exists an abstract equivalence
{\mathcal{O}}(S)\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{A{\mathcal{O}}}(S)
, and since
{\mathcal{O}}(S)\in\left\{*,\varnothing\right\}
, every endomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}}(S)
is an equivalence.
This implies that
{\mathcal{O}}(S)\rightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{A{\mathcal{O}}}(S)
is an equivalence for all
S\in\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
, and the result follows from
Section

2.3.2
.
∎

Corollary 2.2
.

Given
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad, there is an equivalence
h_{0}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{A{\mathcal{O}}\infty}^{\otimes}
.
Hence, for any weak indexing category
I
, there is a natural equivalence

(18)

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty})\simeq\begin{cases}*&A{\mathcal{O}}\leq I,\\
\varnothing&\mathrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

The first statement follows by combining
Sections

2.6
and
2.6
with the fact that
A{\mathcal{O}}=Ah_{d}{\mathcal{O}}
.
We’ve already shown that
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty})\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{A{\mathcal{O}}}}({\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty})
is either empty or contractible in
Section

2.5
, so it suffices to characterize when there exists a map
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{J\infty}^{\otimes}
, i.e. a factorization
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\subset\operatorname{Span}_{J}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\subset\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
;
this occurs if and only if
I\leq J
, yielding the corollary.
∎

The following generalization of the indexing systems theorems of
[
BP21
,
GW18
,
NS22
,
Rub21
]
then immediately follows from
Sections

2.6
,
2.5
and
2.6
.

Corollary 2.2
.

The functor of admissible maps admits a fully faithful right adjoint

(19)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{w}\mathrm{Index}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}}^{\otimes}_{(-)\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

whose image consists of the weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads;
furthermore, the following are equal full subcategories of
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
:

\mathrm{Op}_{I}=\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},/{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}}=A^{-1}(\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq I}).

2.2
.

By fully faithfulness of
{\mathcal{N}}_{(-)\infty}^{\otimes}
, the adjunction associated with
A
restricts to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\math@bb{E}}_{\infty}^{\otimes}/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Index}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}}^{\otimes}_{(-)\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Given
I\leq J
a related pair of weak indexing sysems, let
E_{I}^{J}:\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq I}\rightarrow\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq J}
be the evident inclusion, with right adjoint
\mathrm{Bor}_{I}^{J}=(-)\cap{\math@bb{F}}_{I}:\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq J}\rightarrow\mathrm{wIndexCat}_{{\mathcal{T}},\leq kI}
.
These are push-pull adjunctions;
following in form, we write the corresponding
unslicing functor
as

E_{I}^{J}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{I}\simeq\mathrm{Op}_{J,/{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{J}.

This has a right adjoint

\mathrm{Bor}_{I}^{J}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{J}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{J,/{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}}\simeq\mathrm{Op}_{I}

given by pullback along the unique map
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{N}}_{J\infty}^{\otimes}
.
These map to push-pull along the inclusion
\iota_{I}^{J}\colon\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{J}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
along
\operatorname{Tot}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{I}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{/\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}
and similar for
J

[
BHS22
, § 4]
.
Hence they intertwine with
A
, i.e.

E_{I}^{J}A{\mathcal{O}}=AE_{I}^{J}{\mathcal{O}};\hskip 50.0pt\mathrm{Bor}_{I}^{J}A{\mathcal{O}}=A\mathrm{Bor}_{I}^{J}{\mathcal{O}}.

Corollary 2.2
.

For
I\leq J
weak indexing systems, the functor
E_{I}^{J}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{I}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{J}
is an inclusion of a colocalizing
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{I}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{J}^{\otimes}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{J}_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bor}^{J}_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

whose terminal object is
{\mathcal{N}}_{I\infty}^{\otimes}
.
Furthermore, there is are equivalences

\displaystyle E_{I}^{I^{\prime}}{\mathcal{N}}_{J\infty}^{\otimes}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{E_{I}^{I^{\prime}}J\infty}^{\otimes}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Bor}_{I}^{I^{\prime}}{\mathcal{N}}_{J\infty}^{\otimes}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathrm{Bor}_{I}^{I^{\prime}}J\infty}^{\otimes}.

Proof.

The first sentence follows by the above argument.
The computations follow by examining the structure spaces of the resulting
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.
∎

2.7.
The genuine operadic nerve

We now concern ourselves with comparisons to other notions of equivariant operads.
Throughout this subsection, we assume
{\mathcal{T}}
is a 1-category;
for instance, we may specialize to
V
-operads for
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
an orbit.
We begin in
Section

2.7.1
by reviewing Bonventre’s genuine operadic nerve
N^{\otimes}
;
we detour in
Section

2.7.2
to verify that it is compatible with restriction.
Then, in
Section

2.7.3
we show that
N^{\otimes}
possesses a conservative total right derived functor of
\infty
-categories.
We end in
Section

2.7.4
by noting that this restricts to an equivalence on
{\mathcal{T}}
-1-operads and describing the corresponding discrete theory of algebras.
In all sections, we assume that
{\mathcal{T}}
is an atomic orbital 1-category.

2.7.1.
The 1-categorical nerve

Recall the
{\mathcal{T}}
-space
\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}
of
Section

2.3.1
.

[
Bon19
]
introduced a specialization of the following.

2.2
.

A
{\mathfrak{C}}
-colored genuine
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad in a symmetric monoidal 1-category
\mathcal{V}
the data of:

(1)

a
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequence
{\mathcal{O}}(-;-)\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
,

(2)

for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
and
C\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{V}
, a distinguished “identity” element
1_{C}\in{\mathcal{O}}(C;C)
, and

(3)

for all composable data
\left((\mathbf{C};D),(\mathbf{B}_{U};C_{U})_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\right)
lying over a map
T\rightarrow S
,
a Borel
\operatorname{Aut}_{V}(S)\times\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\operatorname{Aut}_{U}(T_{U})
-equivariant “composition” map

\gamma:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\otimes\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{B}_{U};C_{U})\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathbf{B};D\right)

subject to the following compatibilities:

(a)

(restriction-stability of the identity) for all
U\rightarrow V
and
C\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{V}
, the restriction map

\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}\colon{\mathcal{O}}(C;C)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}C;\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}C\right)

sends
1_{C}
to
1_{\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}C}
;

(b)

(restriction-stability of composition) for all
U\rightarrow V
, the following commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{B}_{U};C_{U})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{B};D)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\mathbf{C};\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}D\right)\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\mathbf{B}_{U};C_{U}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\mathbf{B};D\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{V}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

(c)

(unitality) for all
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(C_{U};C_{U})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(D;D)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\operatorname{id},\left(\left\{1_{U}\right\}\right)\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(1_{V},\operatorname{id}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

(d)

(associativity) For all
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
,
(T_{U})\in{\math@bb{F}}_{S}
writing
T\coloneqq\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{U}^{S}T_{U}
, and
(R_{W})\in{\math@bb{F}}_{T}
writing
R\coloneqq\coprodop\displaylimits\limits_{W}^{T}R_{W}
, the following diagram commutes
