{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left({\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathbf{C};D\right)\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S_{U})}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathbf{B}_{U};C_{U}\right)\right)\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}}{{W\in\mathrm{Orb}(T_{U})}}}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathbf{A}_{W};B_{W}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathbf{B};D\right)\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{W\in\mathrm{Orb}(T)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathbf{A}_{W};B_{W}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\left({\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{B}_{U};C_{U})\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{W\in\mathrm{Orb}(T_{U})}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{A}_{W};B_{W})\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\otimes\hskip-9.0pt\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}\hskip-9.0pt{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathbf{A}_{U};C_{U}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}\left(\mathbf{A};D\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma\;}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

A morphism of
{\mathfrak{C}}
-colored discrete
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads in
\mathcal{V}
is a map of
{\mathfrak{C}}
-symmetric sequences in
\mathcal{V}
preserving each
1_{C}
and intertwining
\gamma
;
we refer to the resulting 1-category as
g\mathrm{Op}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathcal{V})
.

2.2
.

Given a map of coefficient systems
f\colon{\mathfrak{C}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{C}}^{\prime}
, there is an induced map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
\mathrm{triv}\left({\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\otimes}\rightarrow\mathrm{triv}\left({\mathfrak{C}}^{\prime}\right)^{\otimes}
yielding a
{\mathcal{T}}
-functor
f\colon\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\rightarrow\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}^{\prime}}
, and hence a precomposition functor

f^{*}\colon\operatorname{Fun}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}^{\prime}},\mathcal{V}\right)\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},\mathcal{V}\right).

These are the cocartesian transport functors of a cocartesian fibration, which we call
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
A
morphism of colored discrete operads

{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
is a morphism of their underlying objects of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
which is compatible with identities and composition.
We refer to the resulting 1-category as
g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathcal{V})
.

We write
s\mathrm{Op}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\coloneqq g\mathrm{Op}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(s\mathrm{Set})
and
s\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\coloneqq g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(s\mathrm{Set})
.
In
[
BP21
]
, a model structure was given to
s\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{G}
;
this was later shown to be Quillen equivalent to several other model categorical variations on
G
-operads (e.g.
[
BP20
, Tab 1]
).
Complementarily,
[
Bon19
]
constructed a
genuine operadic nerve
functor of 1-categories

N^{\otimes}\colon s\mathrm{Op}_{G}\rightarrow s\mathrm{Set}^{+}_{/\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{G,*},\mathrm{Ne}\right)}

whose restriction
g\mathrm{Op}_{G}(\mathrm{Kan})
lands in fibrant objects in Nardin-Shah’s model structure
[
NS22
, § 2.6]
, and hence presents
G
-operads.

Moreover,
g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Kan})
agrees with the
fibrant simplicial colored
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
of
[
NS22
, Def 2.5.4]
;
Nardin-Shah
[
NS22
]
construct an analogous nerve functor

N^{\otimes}\colon g\mathrm{Op}_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathrm{Kan})\rightarrow s\mathrm{Set}^{+}_{/\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\right),\mathrm{NE}}

whose specialization to
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{O}}_{G}
agrees with Bonventre’s nerve.

These nerve functors can be understood as taking
{\mathcal{O}}\in g\mathrm{Op}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Kan})
to the
\mathrm{Kan}
-enriched category over
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
with
\operatorname{Ob}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{S}\right)={\mathfrak{C}}_{S}
and with mapping spaces

\operatorname{Map}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{D})\coloneqq\coprodop\displaylimits_{S\leftarrow\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathbf{C}\rightarrow\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathbf{D}}\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\mathbf{D}))}{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C}_{U};D_{U}),

with evident composition and mapping down to
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}(\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathbf{C},\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathbf{D})
via the evident forgetful map.

2.7.2.
Restriction and the nerve

2.2
.

Let
W\in{\mathcal{T}}
be a distinguished object.
Then, the
restriction functor

\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathcal{V})\rightarrow g\mathrm{Op}_{W}(\mathcal{V})\coloneqq g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}_{/W}}(\mathcal{V})

acts on underlying symmetric sequences via pullback along the map
\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}
, with the data
1_{V}
and
\gamma
defined in
\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
by specialization from
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
.

We define restriction
\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{s\mathrm{Set},/\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{s\mathrm{Set},/\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{W,*}}
by pullback along
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{W,*}\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
.

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}
be a one-color simplicial genuine
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad.
There is a natural isomorphism of simplicial categories
N^{\otimes}\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}\simeq\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}N^{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}
over
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
.

Proof.

We may construct a functor
N^{\otimes}\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow N^{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
over
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
sending the object over a
(V\rightarrow W)
-set
S_{V\rightarrow W}
to its underlying
V
-set
S
and acting on mapping spaces by taking coproducts of the tautological equivalence
\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}(S_{V\rightarrow W})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(S_{V})
.
This constructs a natural diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}N^{\otimes}\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}N^{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}N^{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{W,*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

Since
\pi_{N^{\otimes}\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}
and
\pi_{\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}N^{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}
are
\pi_{0}
-isomorphisms,
F
is as well, so it is essentially surjective.
It follows by unwinding definitions that
F
is fully faithful, and hence an equivalence.
∎

The above restriction functor implements restriction of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads, so we have the following.

Corollary 2.2
.

There is a natural equivalence of
W
-operads
\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}N^{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}\simeq N^{\otimes}\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}
.

The main reason we went to this trouble is for the following example.

2.2
.

Let
G
be a finite group and
V
be a real orthogonal
G
-representation.
Let
D_{V}
be a genuine
G
-operad which is equivalent to the little
V
-disks operad (see
[
Hor19
, § 3.9]
).
Then, given
K\subset H\subset G
, and
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{K}
, we have a tautological equivalence

\operatorname{Res}_{H}^{G}D_{V}(S)\simeq\operatorname{Conf}_{S}^{K}(\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{G}V)\simeq\operatorname{Conf}_{S}^{K}(\operatorname{Res}_{K}^{H}\operatorname{Res}_{H}^{G}V)\simeq D_{\operatorname{Res}_{H}^{G}V}(S)

which intertwines with the composition rule in
D_{V}
;
writing
{\math@bb{E}}_{V}^{\otimes}\coloneqq N^{\otimes}D^{V}
, we acquire an equivalence

\operatorname{Res}_{H}^{G}{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{V}\simeq{\math@bb{E}}_{\operatorname{Res}_{H}^{G}V}^{\otimes}.

2.7.3.
The conservative
\infty
-categorical lift

N^{\otimes}
has homotopical properties.

Proposition 2.2
.

N^{\otimes}
preserves and reflects weak equivalences between one-color genuine
G
-operads in Kan complexes.

Proof.

By
[
BP21
, Thm II,Prop 4.31]
, the functor
U:s\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{G}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\underline{\Sigma}_{G},\mathrm{sSet})
is monadic and
s\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{G}
admits the transferred model structure from the projective model structure on
\operatorname{Fun}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G},\mathrm{sSet}_{\mathrm{Quillen}}\right)
;
in particular,
U
preserves and reflects weak equivalences.

It is not hard to see that
\operatorname{sseq}
is right-derived from a functor

s\mathrm{sseq}:\mathrm{sSet}^{+,\mathrm{oc}}_{/(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},Ne)}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G},\mathrm{sSet}_{\mathrm{Quillen}}\right)_{\mathrm{Proj}}

setting
{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{sseq}}(S):=\pi_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{-1}(\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}S\rightarrow G/H)
;
by
Section

2.3.2

\operatorname{sseq}
is conservative, so
s\mathrm{sseq}
preserves and reflects weak equivalences between fibrant objects.
Hence it suffices unwind definitions and note that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}^{+,{\mathrm{oc}}}_{/(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{G},Ne)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{G},\mathrm{sSet})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\otimes}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathrm{sseq}}

∎

In fact, the one-color assumption was unnecessary.
We say that a map of genuine simplicial
G
-operads
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
is a
weak equivalence
if it is an isomorphism on coefficient systems and for all profiles
(\mathbf{C};D)
, the map
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbf{C};D)\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}(\varphi\mathbf{C};\varphi D)

is a weak equivalence.
These weak equivalences satisfy two-out-of-three (in fact, two-out-of-six) by the same property for isomorphisms and for weak equivalences of simplicial sets.

Proposition 2.2
.

N^{\otimes}
preserves and reflects weak equivalences between arbitrary genine
G
-operads in Kan complexes.

Proof.

It is not hard to see that
N^{\otimes}
preserves and reflects the property of
inducing isomorphism on coefficient systems of colors
, so we may fix a coefficient system of sets of colors
{\mathfrak{C}}
and verify that

N^{\otimes}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}\colon g\mathrm{Op}_{G}^{{\mathfrak{C}}}(\mathrm{Kan})\rightarrow s\mathrm{Set}^{+}_{/(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{G,*},NE)}

preserves and reflects weak equivalences.
Thankfully, we have the same tools as in the one-color case;

Section

2.3.2
constructs a functor
s\operatorname{sseq}:\mathrm{sSet}^{+,{\mathfrak{C}}}_{/(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},Ne)}\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},\mathrm{sSet}_{\mathrm{Quillen}}\right)_{\mathrm{Proj}}
which preserves and reflects weak equivalences between fibrant objects, and
N^{\otimes}_{{\mathfrak{C}}}
is a functor over
\operatorname{Fun}\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{\Sigma}_{{\mathfrak{C}}},\mathrm{sSet}_{\mathrm{Quillen}}\right)
;
by two-out-of-three for weak equivalences,
N^{\otimes}
preserves and reflects weak equivalences between fibrant objects.
∎

Defining the
\infty
-category
g\mathrm{Op}_{G}\coloneqq g\mathrm{Op}_{G}(\mathrm{Kan})[\mathrm{weq}^{-1}]
, we acquire a multi-color version of
Corollary

B
by functoriality of Hammock localization.

2.2
.

In
[
BP21
]
, another nerve functor
\iota_{*}\colon g\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{e}(\mathrm{sSet})^{BG}\rightarrow g\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{G}(\mathrm{sSet})
was constructed and shown to furnish a Quillen equivalence for a model structure on
g\mathrm{Op}^{\mathrm{oc}}_{e}(\mathrm{sSet})^{BG}
whose weak equivalences and fibrations are preserved and reflected by the
graph subgroup
fixed points
\prodop\displaylimits_{n\in{\math@bb{N}}}\left\{{\mathcal{O}}(n)^{\Gamma}\mid\Gamma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{G\times\Sigma_{n},\Gamma}\right\}
for the Quillen model structure on
\mathrm{sSet}
.
In particular, under the equivalence
\mathrm{Un}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{G}}{\mathcal{O}}_{G\times\Sigma_{n},\Gamma}\simeq B_{G}\Sigma_{n}
, an object
{\mathcal{O}}\in g\mathrm{Op}_{e}(\mathrm{sSet})^{BG}
has an
n
-ary
B_{G}\Sigma_{n}
space
{\mathcal{O}}(n)
.
In fact, unwinding definitions using
[
BP21
, Rmk 4.38]
, we find that there is an equivalence

\underline{N^{\otimes}\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}}(n)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(n).

2.7.4.
The discrete genuine nerve is an equivalence

Note that the fully faithful inclusion of discrete simplicial sets
\mathrm{Set}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{sSet}
is product-preserving, so it induces a fully faithful functor
g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Set})\hookrightarrow g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{sSet})
.
We refer to these as
discrete genuine
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
.
We’re concerned with relating this to
{\mathcal{T}}
-1-categories, beginning with the following.

2.2
.

For all
{\mathcal{O}}\in g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Set})
,
N^{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-1-operad.

Conversely, from the data of a
{\mathcal{T}}
-1-operad
{\mathcal{O}}
, the data of a discrete genuine
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}(-)
is supplied by
Section

2.2.1
.

Proposition 2.2
.

N^{\otimes}
descends to a functor
g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Set})\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},1}
with quasi-inverse
{\mathcal{O}}(-)
.

Proof.

By
Section

2.7.4
,
N^{\otimes}
restricts as above.
Thus it suffices to prove that the compositions
g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Set})\rightarrow g\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Set})
and
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},1}
are naturally equivalent to the identity;
this follows immediately after unwinding definitions.
∎

Now having an explicit combinatorial model for
{\mathcal{T}}
-1-operads, we focus on algebras using the following.

2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
be a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad and
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}
a full
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory.
Then, we define the full
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory
\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\subset\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
to be spanned by the tuples
\mathbf{C}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
such that, for each
U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)
,
C_{U}\in{\mathcal{P}}
.

{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad and
{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
a map of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
[

;
we call this the

.

In particular, if

2.2
.

Suppose

The following proposition follows by unwinding definitions.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{End}_{X}^{\otimes}}

(20)

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mu_{T_{U}}\right)}

.

Let

-commutative algebras and

For the rest of this subsection, we assume familiarity with the techniques of

We will say that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{V}^{\otimes S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{V}^{\otimes S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{V}}}

Define the collection

Proposition 2.2
.

\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{t(f)}
is a one-color weak indexing system.

Proof.

It follows by unwinding definitions that
c(t(f))={\mathcal{T}}
, so we’re left with proving that
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{t(f)}
is closed under self-indexed coproducts.
To that end, fix
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{t(f),V}
and
(T_{U})\in{\math@bb{F}}_{t(f),S}
and write
T\coloneqq\coprodop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}T_{U}
.
By the associativity condition, we’re tasked with proving that the outer rectangle of the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{V}^{\otimes T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}X_{U}^{T_{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{V}^{\otimes S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{V}^{\otimes T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigotimesop\displaylimits_{U}^{S}Y_{U}^{T_{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{V}^{\otimes S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The left inner rectangle is commutative by definition;
the right inner rectangle is commutative by the assumption
S\in{\math@bb{F}}_{t(f),V}
;
the middle inner rectangle is commutative by taking a (pointwise)
S
-indexed tensor product of the commutativity diagrams for each
T_{U}
.
∎

Recall the
