3.
Equivariant Boardman-Vogt tensor products

Using the language of fibrous patterns, in
Section

3.1
we define the
Boardman Vogt tensor product
, and we show that it’s closed and compatible with the Segal envelope in
Sections

3.1
and
3.1
.
Following this, in
Section

3.2
we specialize this to
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
;
moreover, we characterize the
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
-unit of
\mathrm{Op}_{I}
and leverage this to compute the
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories underlying operads of algebras.
Finally, in
Section

3.3
, we define the inflation adjunction
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Op}\colon\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}
and characterize its relationship with the Boardman-Vogt tensor product.

3.1.
Boardman-Vogt tensor products of fibrous patterns

3.0
.

A
magmatic pattern
is the data of a soundly extendable algebraic pattern
{\mathfrak{B}}
together with a functor
\wedge\colon{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}
which is compatible with Segal objects.

3.0
.

Let
({\mathfrak{B}},\wedge)
be a magmatic pattern.
Then, the
{\mathfrak{B}}
-Boardman-Vogt tensor product
is the bifunctor
-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}-\colon\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})\times\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})
defined by

{\mathfrak{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathfrak{P}}\coloneqq L_{\mathrm{Fbrs}}\left({\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}\xrightarrow{\wedge}{\mathfrak{B}}\right).

We defined this in order to have a mapping out property with respect to the following construction.

3.0
.

Let
({\mathfrak{B}},\wedge)
be a magmatic pattern and
{\mathfrak{O}},{\mathfrak{P}},{\mathfrak{Q}}
fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-patterns.
Then, a
bifunctor of fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
patterns

{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{Q}}
is a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{Cat}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge}

whose top horizontal arrow lies in
\mathrm{AlgPatt}
,
15
15

15

The lift to
\mathrm{AlgPatt}
is unique, since each structure map in an algebraic pattern is a replete subcategory inclusion, hence a monomorphism in
\mathrm{Cat}
.
where
{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}
is induced by the structure maps of
{\mathfrak{O}}
and
{\mathfrak{P}}
.
The collection of bifunctors fits into a full subcategory

\mathrm{BiFun}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{O}},{\mathfrak{P}};{\mathfrak{Q}})\subset\operatorname{Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Cat}).

3.0
.

Let
{\mathfrak{O}},{\mathfrak{P}}
be fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-patterns, and consider
{\mathfrak{B}}
to be a fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-pattern via the identity.
Then, the
\infty
-category of bifunctors
{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}
is contractible, as it is equivalent to composite arrows
{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}
.

3.0
.

There are natural equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{BiFun}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{O}},{\mathfrak{P}};{\mathfrak{Q}})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}},\wedge^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}}(\wedge_{!}({\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}),{\mathfrak{Q}})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathfrak{P}},{\mathfrak{Q}}).

As in
[
BV73
, Prop 2.19]
and the variety of recontextualizations of their ideas (e.g.
[
Wei11
,
HA
]
), we recognize this as
{\mathfrak{O}}
-algebras in
{\mathfrak{P}}
-algebras
, making
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
into a closed tensor product, using the following.

3.0
.

Fix
({\mathfrak{B}},\wedge)
a magmatic pattern, let
F\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{Q}}
be a bifunctor of fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-patterns, and let
{\mathfrak{C}}
be a fibrous
{\mathfrak{Q}}
-pattern.
We have a diagram

{\mathfrak{O}}\xleftarrow{p}{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\xrightarrow{F}{\mathfrak{Q}};

admitting push-pull adjunctions
p^{*}\dashv p_{*}
and
L_{\mathrm{Fbrs}}F_{!}\dashv F^{*}
on fibrous patterns, with compatible adjunctions on Segal objects by
Sections

2.1.3
,
2.1.3
and
2.1.4
.
We define the pattern

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{Q}}}({\mathfrak{C}})\coloneqq p_{*}F^{*}{\mathfrak{C}}\in\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{O}});

this is the
fibrous
{\mathfrak{O}}
-pattern of
{\mathfrak{P}}
-algebras in
{\mathfrak{C}}
over
{\mathfrak{Q}}
.

In most cases, we will have
{\mathfrak{Q}}={\mathfrak{O}}={\mathfrak{B}}
, in which case the information of a bifunctor
{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{B}}
is simply that of a fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-pattern
{\mathfrak{P}}
by
Section

3.1
.
In this case, we simply write

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}({\mathfrak{C}})\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{C}})\in\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}});

this is the
fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-pattern of
{\mathfrak{P}}
-algebras in
{\mathfrak{C}}
.

In the case
{\mathfrak{Q}}={\mathfrak{O}}={\mathfrak{B}}
, the above diagram refines to

{\mathfrak{B}}\xleftarrow{p}{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}\times\pi}{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}\xrightarrow{\wedge}{\mathfrak{B}},

so the functor
{\mathfrak{P}}\mapsto\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\otimes}({\mathfrak{C}})
has a left adjoint computed by
L_{\mathrm{Fbrs}}\wedge_{!}\left(\operatorname{id}\times\pi\right)_{!}p^{*}
;
explicitly, this is computed on
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
by the fibrous localization of the diagonal composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\times{\mathfrak{P}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p^{*}{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{{\mathfrak{Q}}}\times\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{{\mathfrak{Q}}}\times\pi_{{\mathfrak{P}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}\times\pi_{{\mathfrak{P}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge}

By definition, this is precisely
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\otimes^{\mathrm{BV}}{\mathfrak{P}}
, so we’ve proved the following.

Proposition 3.0
.

The functor
(-)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathfrak{O}}\colon\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})
is left adjoint to
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{\otimes}(-)
.

We additionally spell out a few useful characteristics
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
and
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{\otimes}(-)
here.
First, we describe functoriality.

3.0
.

Fix the fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-pattern
{\mathfrak{Q}}
.
Suppose we have bifunctors of fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-patterns

F\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{Q}}\longleftarrow{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\colon G

together with a morphism of fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-patterns
\varphi\colon{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
making the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}

The left triangle possesses a Beck-Chevalley transformation

p^{*}\varphi_{!}\implies\operatorname{id}_{!}p^{\prime*}=p^{\prime*},

which possesses a mate natural transformation
p^{\prime}_{*}\implies p_{*}\varphi^{*}
;
precomposing with
G^{*}
, this yields a “pullback” natural transformation

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}/{\mathfrak{Q}}}^{\otimes}(-)\implies\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{Q}}}^{\otimes}(-).

.We observe that, in all of the work above, we may have instead assumed that
{\mathfrak{C}}\in\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathfrak{B}}(\mathrm{Cat})
, in which case all of our constructions land in
\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathfrak{B}}(\mathrm{Cat})
.
Spelled out, this yields the following.

Proposition 3.0
.

Fix
{\mathfrak{O}},{\mathfrak{P}},{\mathfrak{Q}},{\mathfrak{C}}
as in
Section

3.1
. Then

(1)

if
{\mathfrak{C}}
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{Q}}
-
\infty
-category, then
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{Q}}}({\mathfrak{C}})
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-category;

(2)

if
{\mathfrak{C}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}
is a morphism of Segal
{\mathfrak{Q}}
-
\infty
-categories, then the induced map
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{Q}}}({\mathfrak{C}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{Q}}}({\mathfrak{D}})
is a morphism of Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-categories, i.e. it preserves cocartesian arrows; and

(3)

if
{\mathfrak{P}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
is a morphism of fibrous
{\mathfrak{B}}
-patterns and
{\mathfrak{C}}
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{Q}}
-
\infty
-category, then the induced map of fibrous patterns

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}/{\mathfrak{Q}}}^{\otimes}({\mathfrak{C}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathfrak{P}}/{\mathfrak{Q}}}^{\otimes}({\mathfrak{C}})

is a functor of Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-categories.

In analogy to
[
BS24
]
we show that this tensor product is compatible with Segal envelopes.

Proposition 3.0
.

The following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{B}},\mathrm{Cat})^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{B}},\mathrm{Cat})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(\mathrm{Cat})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circledast}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{Seg}}}

Proof.

Fix
{\mathfrak{C}}
a Segal
{\mathfrak{B}}
-
\infty
-category.
Then, there are natural equivalences

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(\mathrm{Cat})}\left(\mathrm{Env}\left({\mathfrak{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathfrak{P}}\right),{\mathfrak{C}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}}\left(\wedge_{!}{\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}},{\mathfrak{C}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}\left({\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}},\wedge^{*}{\mathfrak{C}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}\left(\mathrm{Env}_{{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}({\mathfrak{O}}\times{\mathfrak{P}}),\wedge^{*}{\mathfrak{C}}\right)

(21)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}\left(\mathrm{Env}_{\mathfrak{B}}({\mathfrak{O}})\times\mathrm{Env}_{\mathfrak{B}}({\mathfrak{P}}),\wedge^{*}{\mathfrak{C}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathrm{cocart}}_{/{\mathfrak{B}}}\left(L_{\mathrm{Seg}}\wedge_{!}\left(\mathrm{Env}_{\mathfrak{B}}\left({\mathfrak{O}})\times\mathrm{Env}_{\mathfrak{B}}({\mathfrak{P}}\right)\right),{\mathfrak{C}}\right)

(22)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(\mathrm{Cat})}\left(L_{\mathrm{Seg}}\left(\mathrm{Env}_{\mathfrak{B}}({\mathfrak{O}})\circledast\mathrm{Env}_{\mathfrak{B}}({\mathfrak{P}})\right),{\mathfrak{C}}\right)

Equivalence
Eq.

21
is
Section

2.1.4
;

Eq.

22
follows by symmetric monoidality of the Grothendieck construction
[
Ram22
, Thm B]
.
The result then follows by Yoneda’s lemma.
∎

We derive a uniqueness statement for
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
by an analogous argument to
[
BS24
]
.

Corollary 3.0
.

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
is the unique bifunctor on
\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})
making the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{B}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{B}},\mathrm{Cat})_{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}\right)^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{B}},\mathrm{Cat})_{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circledast\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{B}},\mathrm{Cat})_{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Seg}({\mathfrak{B}})_{/\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circledast}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Env}(\wedge)_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{Seg}}}

Proof.

Commutativity of the diagram follows by
Section

3.1
and uniqueness of
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
follows from the fact that the right vertical functor is fully faithful, hence a monomorphism in
\mathrm{Cat}
.
∎

3.2.
Boardman-Vogt tensor products of
V
-operads

Recall that
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))
.
We specialize the results of
Section

3.1
to the case that
{\mathcal{T}}
has a terminal object.

3.0
.

Fix an object
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
.
We show in
Section

A.3
that the Cartesian product in
{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
endows
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})
with the structure of a magmatic pattern via the
smash product

\wedge\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}(\times)\colon\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})\times\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V});

we refer to the resulting bifunctor as the
Boardman-Vogt tensor product of
V
-operads

{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq L_{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\times{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})\times\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})\xrightarrow{\wedge}\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})\right).

The
V
-operad of
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
is given by the right adjoint
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})\in\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
to the Boardman-Vogt tensor product constructed in
Section

3.1
.

Section

3.1
immediately implies the following.

Corollary 3.0
.

Fix
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
a map of
V
-operads and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}
a map of
V
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.
Then,
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})
is a
V
-symmetric monoidal category, and the canonical lax
V
-symmetric monoidal functors

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}}),\hskip 40.0pt\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{D}})

are
V
-symmetric monoidal.

Using this, in the one-color case we view
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
-algebras as
homotopy-coherently interchanging
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra and
{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra structures on a common
{\mathcal{T}}
-object
.
This takes an easy to understand form in the 1-categorical case by the following argument.

3.0
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
is an
I
-symmetric monoidal 1-category and
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
are one-color
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.
Then, an
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
-algebra structure on a
{\mathcal{T}}
-object
X\in\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}
is equivalently viewed as a pair of maps
{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{X}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})
and
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{X}^{\otimes}\left(\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{C}})\right)
via
Section

2.7.4
.
In particular, this consists of pairs of
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra and
{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra structures on
X
subject to the
interchange relation
that, for all
\mu_{S}\in{\mathcal{O}}(S)
and
\mu_{T}\in{\mathcal{O}}(T)
, the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{U}^{S}X^{\otimes\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}T}_{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{V}^{\otimes S\times T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigotimesop\displaylimits\limits_{W}^{T}X^{\otimes\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}S}_{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{\otimes T}_{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{\otimes S}_{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}\mu_{T}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\operatorname{Res}_{W}^{V}\mu_{S}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{S}}

A morphism of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
-algebras is equivalently expressed as a morphism of underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
objects
X\rightarrow Y
causing the following to commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{End}^{\otimes}_{X}({\mathcal{C}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{End}_{X}^{\otimes}\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{End}^{\otimes}_{Y}{\mathcal{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{End}_{Y}^{\otimes}\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})}}

By faithfulness of the forgetful functor
\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})_{V}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{V}
, this is simply a morphism of underlying
{\mathcal{T}}
-objects which is separately an
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra and
{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra map.

Section

3.1
specializes to the following.

Corollary 3.0
.

The
V
-symmetric monoidal envelope intertwines with the mode structure:

\mathrm{Env}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right)\simeq\mathrm{Env}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\right)\otimes^{\mathrm{Mode}}\mathrm{Env}\left({\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right).

In particular,
[
BS24
, Thm E]
shows that this property identifies the non-equivariant Boardman-Vogt tensor product, so we acquire the following.

Corollary 3.0
.

When
{\mathcal{T}}\simeq*
,
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
is naturally equivalent to the Boardman-Vogt tensor product of
[
BS24
,
HA
,
HM23
]
.

Additionally, we may characterize the
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
-unit.

Proposition 3.0
.

For all
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{V}
, we have
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}\mathrm{triv}_{V}^{\otimes}
;
hence there exists a natural equivalence

\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{\mathrm{triv}_{V}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}.

Proof.

The first statement implies the second by the usual folklore argument:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{\mathrm{triv}_{V}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{P}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}\mathrm{triv}_{V}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right),

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Map}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}),

so Yoneda’s lemma yields a natural equivalence
\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{\mathrm{triv}_{V}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{P}})\simeq{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}
.
The same argument in reverse shows that the second statement implies the first.

By the expression
\mathrm{triv}_{V}^{\otimes}\simeq L_{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}(*_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))
, bifunctors
\mathrm{triv}^{\otimes}_{V}\times{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
correspond canonically with functors of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
;
put another way, using the bifunctor presentation for algebras of
Section

3.1
, this demonstrates that the forgetful natural transformation

\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}\otimes^{\mathrm{BV}}\mathrm{triv}_{V}}({\mathcal{P}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})

is a natural equivalence for all
{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{V}
;
Yoneda’s lemma then demonstrates that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}\mathrm{triv}^{\otimes}_{V}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
.
∎

Using this, we have a sequence of natural equivalences

\displaystyle U\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{P}})

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{\mathrm{triv}_{V}}\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{P}})

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}\otimes\mathrm{triv}_{V}}({\mathcal{P}})

\displaystyle\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}});

in particular, we’ve proved the following corollary.

Corollary 3.0
.

There exists a natural equivalence

U\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}})\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{P}}).

We’ve shown in
Section

3.1
that
\mathrm{Env}
intertwines
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
with
\circledast
, and we’ve now seen that
\mathrm{triv}^{\otimes}_{V}
is the
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
-unit.
In fact,
\mathrm{Env}
intertwines units.

Proposition 3.0
.

\mathrm{Env}_{I}(\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is the
\circledast
-unit in
\mathrm{CMon}_{I}(\mathrm{Cat})^{\circledast}
.

Proof.

By
Theorem

1.5
, when
{\mathcal{C}}^{\times}
is cartesian, the free object
\operatorname{Fr}_{I}(*)\in\mathrm{CMon}_{I}({\mathcal{C}})
is the
\circledast
-unit, so

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}_{I}(\mathrm{Env}_{I}(\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\otimes},{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{triv}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes})

2.2.3

\displaystyle\simeq\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{D}}

2.2.4

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}_{I}(\operatorname{Fr}_{I}*,{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}_{I}\left(1^{\circledast},{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}\right);

hence the result follows from Yoneda’s lemma.
∎

In forthcoming work
[
Ste24
]
, we will use
Sections

3.2
and
3.2
and a variant of Barkan-Steinebrunner’s strategy
[
BHS22
]
to lift
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
to a canonical symmetric monoidal structure.

3.3.
Inflation and the Boardman-Vogt tensor product

Recall the adjunction
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}
of
Section

1.1
.
We briefly discuss an operadic version of this and relate it to
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
.

3.0
.

Given
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
a
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad, and
V\in{\mathcal{T}}
, we form the
V
-value operad

\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq i_{V}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},

where
i_{V}\colon\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is the map of patterns extending the coproduct preserving functor
{\math@bb{F}}\hookrightarrow{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
sending
*\mapsto*_{V}
.
Using this, we may set

\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\coloneqq\lim_{V\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes},

noting that this recovers
\Gamma^{V}
if
V
is terminal in
{\mathcal{T}}
.

3.0
.

In the case that
{\mathcal{C}}^{\otimes}
is a
{\mathcal{T}}
-symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, the structure map of the operad
\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{C}}
is the pullback of a cocartesian fibration, so it is a cocartesian fibration, i.e. it presents a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category;
unwinding definitions, this agrees with the construction
\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{C}}
of
Section

1.2.6
.
Since the forgetful functor
\mathrm{Cat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}
is a right adjoint, it preserves limits, so the two constructions of
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{C}}
also agree.

In
Section

A.1.5
, we show that
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\times\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{I^{\infty}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{Op}_{I^{\infty}}\simeq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathrm{Op}).

In particular, this yields the following.

Proposition 3.0
.

The functor
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{I^{\infty}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}
has a fully faithful left adjoint
\mathrm{Infl}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}_{I^{\infty}}
whose image is spanned by the
I^{\infty}
-operads whose corresponding functors
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}
are constant.

The map of patterns
i_{V}
induces a push-pull adjunction
E_{I^{\infty}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}_{I^{\infty}}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Bor}_{I^{\infty}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}
, and we will write
\mathrm{Infl}^{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}
for the composite adjunction as well.

Proposition 3.0
.

There exists a natural equivalence
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\simeq\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right)
.

Proof.

We can verify that
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}
is product-preserving, so we acquire a zigzag of maps

\displaystyle\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\xleftarrow{\;\;\;\;\eta_{\mathrm{Op}_{V}}\;\;\;\;}

\displaystyle\;\;\wedge_{!}\left(\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\times\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\;\;\wedge_{!}\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\times{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\;\;\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\wedge_{!}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\times{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\;\;\;\;\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\eta_{\mathrm{Op}}\;\;\;\;}

\displaystyle\;\;\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right),

with
\eta_{\mathrm{Op}_{V}}
an
L_{\mathrm{Op}_{V}}
-equivalence.
We’re tasked with proving that
\eta_{\mathrm{Op}}
is an
L_{\mathrm{Op}_{V}}
-equivalence;
then, the desired equivalence can be gotten by applying
L_{\mathrm{Op}_{V}}
and inverting arrows as needed.
In fact, if
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\otimes}
is a
V
-operad, then pullback along
\eta_{\mathrm{Op}}
furnishes an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}\left(\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\wedge_{!}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\times{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right),{\mathcal{Q}}^{\otimes}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}})}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}\left(\wedge_{!}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\times{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right),\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\otimes}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}})}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes},\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\otimes}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}_{/\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})}^{\mathrm{int}-\mathrm{cocart}}\left(\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right),{\mathcal{Q}}^{\otimes}\right),

so
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\eta_{\mathrm{Op}}
is an
L_{\mathrm{Op}_{V}}
-equivalence, yielding the desired natural equivalence.
∎

3.0
.

Let
G
be a finite group and
n_{G}
the trivial
n
-dimensional orthogonal
G
-representation.
Note that the bottom map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\math@bb{E}}_{n_{G}}(m\cdot*_{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\math@bb{E}}_{n_{G}}(m\cdot*_{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Conf}_{m\cdot*_{H}}^{H}(n_{G})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Conf}_{m\cdot*_{H}}^{H}(n_{G})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

is an equivalence for all
K\subset H\subset G
, as it intertwines the tautological identification of each side with
\operatorname{Conf}_{m}({\math@bb{R}}^{n})
.
In particular, the map
{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{n_{G}}\rightarrow{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{\infty_{G}}\simeq{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{\infty}
witnesses
{\math@bb{E}}_{n_{G}}
as an
I^{\infty}
-operad in the image of
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{G}
;
unwinding definitions, we have an equivalence
\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{G}{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{n}\simeq{\math@bb{E}}_{n_{G}}
.

In general, we define the
{\mathcal{T}}
-operad
{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{n}\coloneqq\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{{\mathcal{T}}}{\math@bb{E}}_{n}^{\otimes}
.

Corollary 3.0

(Trivially eqivariant Dunn additivity)
.

There is an equivalence
{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{m}\simeq{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{n+m}
.

Proof.

By
Sections

3.2
and
3.3
, it suffices to construct an equivalence of operads
{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{m}\simeq{\math@bb{E}}^{\otimes}_{n+m}
;
this is nonequivariant Dunn additivity
[
HA
, Thm 5.1.2.2]
.
∎

Moreover, we acquire compatibility between
\Gamma^{{\mathcal{T}}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads of algebras.

Corollary 3.0
.

There exists a natural equivalence of operads

\Gamma^{V}\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}^{\otimes}_{\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}(\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{C}})

Proof.

Once more, given
{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}
, there is a string of natural equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}\Gamma^{V}\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{P}}}\underline{\mathrm{Alg}}_{\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\left({\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}\right)}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}\mathrm{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}(\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{C}}),

so the result follows by Yoneda’s lemma.
∎

A similar statement to
Section

3.3
for
\mathrm{triv}(-)^{\otimes}
follows by either symbol pushing or examining the various localizations;
we take the former approach, constructing a string of natural equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\mathrm{triv}\left({\mathcal{C}}\right)}({\mathcal{O}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{triv}\left({\mathcal{C}}\right)}(\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{O}})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{C}},\Gamma^{V}{\mathcal{O}})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{O}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{triv}\left(\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{C}}\right)}({\mathcal{O}}).

That is, we’ve proved the following.

Proposition 3.0
.

There is a canonical natural equivalence

\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}\mathrm{triv}\left({\mathcal{C}}\right)^{\otimes}\simeq\mathrm{triv}\left(\mathrm{Infl}_{e}^{V}{\mathcal{C}}\right)^{\otimes}.

3.0
.

Sections

3.2
and
3.3
collected results about Boardman-Vogt tensor products of
V
-operads, which implies the corresponding results for
G
-operads as
{\mathcal{O}}_{G}
has a terminal object.
Nevertheless, for the sake of equivariance under families, we would like to prove the corresponding results for
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads.
Unwinding the arguments, it would suffice to lift
\left(\mathrm{Op}_{V},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}\right)
to an
{\math@bb{A}}_{2}
-
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-category, and thus develop a
Boardman-Vogt tensor product of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads
which restricts to our construction.
In fact, to do so simply requires constructing coherent natural equivalences

\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}\right)\simeq\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}\operatorname{Res}_{U}^{V}{\mathcal{P}}^{\otimes}

for all
U\rightarrow V\in{\mathcal{T}}
.
Inspired by the uniqueness of
Section

3.1
, two strategies come to mind:

(1)

Much of the work of
[
BS24a
]
is likely to hold for
{\mathcal{T}}
-commutative monoids;
in particular, one may expect that an equifibered map between envelopes of
{\mathcal{T}}
-operads canonically lifts to a map over
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{T}}-\sqcup}
, which would imply that the
unsliced
envelope
\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\otimes}
is a replete subcategory inclusion, and hence monic.
Thus
Section

3.2
and restriction-stability of
\otimes^{\mathrm{mode}}
would yield restriction-stability of
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny BV}}}{{\otimes}}
.

(2)

Alternatively, one may note that, in the nonequivariant case,
\mathrm{Comm}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}
is an idempotent algebra.
If
\mathrm{Comm}_{V}^{\otimes}\in\mathrm{Op}_{V}
is an idempotent algebra for all
V
, then their envelopes
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{V}^{V-\sqcup}
will be idempotent algebras under the mode structure by
Section

3.1
, compatibly with restriction (as the unit maps each live in a contractible mapping space).
This would yield a symmetric monoidal structure on
\underline{\mathrm{Cat}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{T}}-\sqcup}}^{\otimes}
under which
\underline{\mathrm{Op}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
would be a symmetric monoidal full
{\mathcal{T}}
-subcategory.

The author hopes to return fulfill the second strategy in forthcoming work
[
Ste24
]
.

Appendix A
Burnside algebraic patterns: the atomic orbital and global cases

The following appendix is not written to be particularly original;
most of its contents appear as straightforward technical extensions of beloved works in higher algebra, and they are included for the sake of mathematical completeness.
The contents herein do not depend on the results of the main body of this paper.

A.1.
I
-operads as fibrous patterns

This subsection deviates only slightly from
[
BHS22
, § 5.2]
, so we suggest that the reader first read their work.
We’re interested in proving a global equivariant generalization of
Section

2.2.2
, so we begin with the relevant patterns.
We assume familiarity with the terminology of finite pointed
{\mathcal{T}}
-sets and
{\mathcal{P}}
-sets of
[
CLL23
,
NS22
]
.

As noted in
[
Ste24a
]
,
{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
is an extensive category in the sense of
[
CHLL24a
, Def 2.2.1]
, an
extensive span pair

({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},I_{{\mathcal{P}}})
equivalent to an atomic orbital subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
(i.e. an indexing category), and a
weakly extensive span pair

({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},I)
equivalent to a one-color weak indexing category
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
In the case
({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},I)
is a weakly extensive span pair, we write

\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}_{all,I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}};{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}})

for the resulting pattern.
Moreover, given
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
an atomic orbital
\infty
-category, we write
\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
and

\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}_{s.i.,tdeg}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee},{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}),

where
(-)^{\vee}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathcal{C}}}^{\mathrm{cocart}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathcal{C}}}^{\mathrm{cart}}
is the
dual cartesian fibration
construction,
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee,s.i.}\subset\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee}
is the wide subcategory of morphisms
f\colon(S\rightarrow U)\rightarrow(T\rightarrow V)
whose associated morphism
f_{\circ}
is a summand inclusion:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\times_{V}U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces`}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{t}}

and
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee,tdeg}\subset\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee}
the wide subcategory with
f_{t}
homotopic to the identity.

The upshot of this is that we acquire a map of adequate quadruples

\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee},(s.i.,tdeg),{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\rightarrow\left({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},(all,{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}),{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)

lying over the source map

s\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},{\vee}}\rightarrow{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},

yielding a map of algebraic patterns

\varphi\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}).

We will prove the following theorem.

Theorem A.1
.

The map of patterns
\varphi\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
induces equivalences of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}({\mathcal{C}}),

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{CMon}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{C}});

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}).

Moreover, in the case
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{P}}
, there is an additional equivalence

\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*})\simeq\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},\infty},

the latter denoting Nardin-Shah
[
NS22
]
’s
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-operads.

A.1.1.
The pattern
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}

We may explicitly describe the Segal conditions for
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}
.

Lemma A.1

(
[
BHS22
, Obs 5.2.9]
)
.

Fix
\left[S\rightarrow U\right]
an object in
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}
.
Then, there are equivalences

(23)

\displaystyle\left(\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}\right)^{\mathrm{el}}_{\left[S\rightarrow U\right]/}\right)^{\operatorname{op}}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{T}}\times_{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}}\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee,s.i.}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\left[S\rightarrow U\right]}

(24)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{T}}\times_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\left[S\rightarrow U\right]}

(25)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{T}}\times_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}}{\math@bb{F}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/S}.

Furthermore, the full subcategory of
\left({\mathcal{T}}\times_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\math@bb{F}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/S}\right)^{\operatorname{op}}
consisting of morphisms
f\colon T\rightarrow S
such that
f
itself is the inclusion of an orbit is an initial subcategory equivalent to the set
\mathrm{Orb}(S)
.

Proof.

Eqs.

23
and
25
follows by definition.
For
Eq.

24
, this follows by noting that whenever
\left[U=U\right]\rightarrow\left[S\rightarrow V\right]
is a morphism in
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
out of an orbit, the associated morphism
U\rightarrow S\times_{V}U
is a summand inclusion, as it’s split by the projection
S\times_{V}U\rightarrow U
and
{\mathcal{P}}
is atomic.
For the remaining statement, the inclusion
\mathrm{Orb}(S)\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}\times_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}}{\math@bb{F}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/S}
has a right adjoint sending
f:T\rightarrow S
to
f(T)\subset S
, so it is initial.
∎

Moreover, the pattern is reasonably well behaved.

Lemma A.1

(
[
BHS22
, Cor 5.2.10]
)
.

The pattern
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
is sound.

Proof.

We verify the conditions of
[
BHS22
, Prop 3.3.23]
.
First, we must verify that
\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee,si}\right)_{/S}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee}_{{\mathcal{T}},/S}
is fully faithful,
i.e. if there is a pair of
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee}
-morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{t}}

such that the associated maps
gf_{\circ}\colon S_{2}\rightarrow S_{0}\times_{U_{0}}U_{2}
and
g_{\circ}\colon S_{1}\rightarrow S_{0}\times_{U_{0}}U_{1}
are summand inclusions, the map
S_{2}\rightarrow S_{1}\times_{U_{1}}U_{2}
is a summand inclusion.
Here, we use the orbitality property that a morphism in
{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}
is a summand inclusion if and only if it’s a section;
noting that
gf_{\circ}
may be decomposed as

S_{2}\xrightarrow{\;\;f_{\circ}\;\;}S_{1}\times_{U_{1}}U_{2}\xrightarrow{\;\;g_{\circ}\times_{U_{1}}U_{2}\;\;}S_{0}\times_{U_{0}}U_{1}\times_{U_{1}}U_{2}\simeq S_{0}\times_{U_{0}}U_{2}.

if
r
is a retract for
gf_{\circ}
, then
r\circ\left(g_{\circ}\times_{U_{1}}U_{2}\right)
is a retract for
f_{\circ}
, so
f
lies in
\left(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee,s.i.}\right)
, as desired.

Last, we must verify that

\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\left[S\rightarrow U\right]}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee,si,\mathrm{el}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/\left[S\rightarrow U\right]}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee,\mathrm{el}}

is final for all
\left[S\rightarrow U\right]\in\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}},\vee}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
;
in fact, it is an equivalence by
Section

A.1.1
.
∎

From this, we may prove the following proposition.

Proposition A.1
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories over
{\mathcal{C}}

\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}-\oplus}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}},\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})).

Moreover, when
{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{T}}
, there is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*})\simeq\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathcal{T}},\infty},

the latter denoting Nardin-Shah
[
NS22
]
’s
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{T}}
-
\infty
-categories.

Proof of
Section

A.1.1
.

For the first statement, note by
Section

A.1.1
that a Segal
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}
-object in
{\mathcal{C}}
is equivalent to a functor

M\colon\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}

satisfying
M(\bigoplusop\displaylimits_{i}{U_{i}})\simeq\prodop\displaylimits_{i}M(U_{i})
;
taking adjunct maps yields a fully faithful embedding

\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}}({\mathcal{C}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Fun}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}},\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})),

so it suffices to identify which
{\mathcal{T}}
-functors
\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})
satisfy the above condition.
In fact, this follows from the identification of
{\mathcal{T}}
-(co)limits in
\underline{\mathrm{Coeff}}^{{\mathcal{T}}}({\mathcal{C}})
of
Section

1.1.2
.
The second statement follows by comparing definitions with
[
BHS22
, Prop 4.1.7]
in view of
Section

A.1.1
.
∎

We now turn to the remaining statements of
Section

2.2.2
making use of the following theorem, whose main content is due to Shaul Barkan in
[
Bar23
, Cor 2.64]
.

Theorem A.2

(
[
BHS22
, Prop 3.1.16,Thm 5.1.1]
)
.

Suppose
f\colon{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}
is a strong Segal morphism of algebraic patterns such that the following conditions hold:

(1)

f^{\mathrm{el}}\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{el}}
is an equivalence, and

(2)

for every
O\in{\mathcal{O}}
, the functor
\left({\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/O}\right)^{\simeq}\rightarrow\left({\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/f(O)}\right)^{\simeq}
is an equivalence.

Then, the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{C}})
is an equivalence.
Furthermore, if
{\mathcal{P}}
is soundly extendable, then
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathcal{P}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathcal{O}})
is an equivalence, and it suffices to check condition (2) on
O\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}
.

A.1.2.
Global effective burnside patterns

Fix
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
a weakly extensive subcategory.
There is a span pattern analog to
Section

A.1.1
which is proved identically.

Lemma A.2
.

The full subcategory of
\left(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{/S}\right)^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}\times_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/S}
consisting of morphisms
f\colon T\rightarrow S
such that
f
is a summand inclusion is an initial subcategory equivalent to the set
\mathrm{Orb}(S)
.

Unwinding definitions, this demonstrates the following.

Corollary A.2
.

The forgetful functor

\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}({\mathcal{C}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}),{\mathcal{C}})

is fully faithful with image spanned by the product preserving functors.

We call these
global effective Burnside patterns
.
They are generally well behaved:

Lemma A.2
.

The pattern
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is soundly extendable.

Proof.

It is sound by
[
BHS22
, Cor 3.3.24]
.
To see that
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is extendable, it is equivalent to prove that
\mathscr{A}_{\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}
is a Segal
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
-
\infty
-category, i.e. for every
S\in\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
, the associated functor
\varphi
of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\mathrm{act}}_{/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prodop\displaylimits_{V\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}I_{/V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\lim_{V\in\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{S/}}\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\mathrm{act}}_{/V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\lim_{V\in{\mathcal{T}}\times_{{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/S}}I_{/V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

is an equivalence.
In fact, it is an equivalence by
Section

A.1.2
.
∎

A.1.3.
The equivalence

We resume our original generality with
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
an atomic orbital subcategory.

Corollary A.2
.

\varphi\colon\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
induces equivalences of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}}({\mathcal{C}});

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}(\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}).

Proof.

The pattern
\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is soundly extendable by
Section

A.1.2
.
In order to verify that
\varphi
is a strong Segal morphism, we must verify that
\varphi^{\mathrm{el}}_{\left[S\rightarrow V\right]/}
is initial;
in fact, it is an equivalence by

Sections

A.1.1
and
A.1.2
.

It remains to check that
\varphi
satisfies the conditions of
Theorem

A.2
.
First, note that
\varphi^{\mathrm{el}}
is an equivalence by construction.
Second, note that there is a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*,/\left[V=V\right]}^{{\mathcal{P}},\mathrm{act}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\math@bb{F}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})^{\mathrm{act}}_{/V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\math@bb{F}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},/V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\mathrm{act}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

so
\varphi^{\mathrm{act}}_{/V}
is an equivalence for all
V\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}=\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*,/\left[V=V\right]}^{{\mathcal{P}},\mathrm{el}}
.
∎

Theorem

A.1
follows by combining
Sections

A.1.1
,
A.1.2
and
A.1.3
.

A.1.4.
The
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal case

We refer to
\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))
as the
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{P}}
-operads
.

Theorem

A.1
yields two algebraic patterns underlying a
{\mathcal{P}}
-operad:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tot}

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))\rightarrow\mathrm{AlgPatt};

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}})\rightarrow\mathrm{AlgPatt}.

in fact, these yield the same algebraic theories.

Corollary A.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
be a
{\mathcal{P}}
-operad.
Then,
\varphi^{*}
induces equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}({\mathcal{C}});

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\operatorname{Tot}_{{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}).

This will follow immediately from the following proposition.

Proposition A.2
.

Suppose
\varphi\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
is a strong Segal morphism of algebraic patterns satisfying the conditions of
Theorem

A.2
and which is a
\pi_{0}
-isomorphism and let
{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
be a fibrous pattern.
Then, the pullback map

\varphi^{\prime}\colon\varphi^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{Q}}

satisfies the conditions of
Theorem

A.2
;
moreover, if
{\mathfrak{P}}
is soundly extendable, then
{\mathfrak{Q}}
is soundly extendable.

Proof.

First note that strong Segal morphisms are closed under pullback, since initial functors are closed under pullback.
Furthermore, fibrous patterns over soundly extendable patterns are soundly extendable
[
BHS22
, Lem 4.1.15]
, so we’re left with verifying the conditions.
Note that we acquire pullback diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{el}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{el}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{act}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{act}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{el}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{P}}^{\mathrm{el}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{act}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{P}}^{\mathrm{act}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

which imply that
\varphi^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{el}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{el}}
is an equivalence.
Pick some
X\in\varphi^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}}
;
then, we acquire pullback diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/\varphi^{\prime}X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\varphi^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/X}\right)^{\simeq}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left({\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/\varphi^{\prime}X}\right)^{\simeq}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/\pi X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{P}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/\varphi\pi X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left({\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/\pi X}\right)^{\simeq}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left({\mathfrak{P}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/\varphi\pi X}\right)^{\simeq}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the right is the core of the left, implying that
\left(\varphi^{*}{\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/X}\right)^{\simeq}\rightarrow\left({\mathfrak{Q}}^{\mathrm{act}}_{/\varphi^{\prime}X}\right)^{\simeq}
is an equivalence, as desired.
∎

For example, we can quickly acquire a model for
I
-operads akin to
[
NS22
]
.
The global version of this uses the following proposition, whose proof is identical to that of
Section

2.2
.

Proposition A.2
.

Let
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}
be a replete wide subcategory.
Then,
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
presents a
{\mathcal{P}}
-operad if and only if
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}
is a weakly extensive subcategory.

Define the pullback pattern
\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I,*}\coloneqq\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{{\mathcal{T}},*}^{{\mathcal{P}}}\times_{\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})

Corollary A.2
.

\varphi^{*}
induces equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})}({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I,*}}({\mathcal{C}});

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Fbrs}(\operatorname{Tot}\underline{{\math@bb{F}}}_{I,*}).

A.2
.

Let
\mathrm{Orb}\subset\mathbf{Glo}
be the global orbit category including into the global indexing category (see e.g.
[
CLL23
, Ex 4.3.3]
).
As remarked in
[
CLL23
, Rmk 4.3.4]
, atomic orbital subcategories of
\mathbf{Glo}
correspond to
global transfer systems
in the sense of
[
Bar23a
]
;
since
\mathrm{Orb}
is the maximal atomic orbital subcategory of
\mathbf{Glo}
, these correspond canonically with extensive subcategories of
{\math@bb{F}}_{\mathbf{Glo}}^{\mathrm{Orb}}
.
If we interpret weakly extensive subcategories
I\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{\mathbf{Glo}}^{\mathrm{Orb}}
as
global weak indexing categories
, the above work thus constructs
global weak
{\mathcal{N}}_{\infty}
-operads
and an equivalence between two models for
global
I
-operads
.

A.1.5.
I^{\infty}
-operads as operadic coefficient systems

We now prove the following result.

Proposition A.2
.

The map
\varphi\colon{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\times\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{I^{\infty}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
induces equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Seg}_{\operatorname{Span}_{I^{\infty}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})({\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathrm{CMon}({\mathcal{C}}));

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fbrs}\left(\operatorname{Span}_{I^{\infty}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathrm{Op}).

Proof.

The right hand sides correspond with
\mathrm{Seg}_{{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\times\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}})}({\mathcal{C}})
and
\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{op}}\times\operatorname{Span}({\math@bb{F}}))
, so it suffices to verify the conditions of
Theorem

A.2
for
\varphi
.
We already know that the codomain is soundly extendable
Section

A.1.2
, and it is easy to see that
\varphi^{\mathrm{el}}
and
\left(\varphi^{\mathrm{act}}_{/O}\right)^{\simeq}
are equivalences.
Moreover, the fact that
\varphi
is a Segal morphism follows from
Section

1.2.4
, so we are done.
∎

A.2.
Pullback of fibrous patterns along Segal morphisms and sound extendability

Proposition A.2
.

Suppose
\varphi\colon{\mathfrak{O}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{P}}
is functor which is compatible with the inert-active factorization system, and
{\mathfrak{P}}
is soundly extendable..
Then,

(1)

If the precomposition functor

\varphi^{*}\colon\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{P}},\mathrm{Cat})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{O}},\mathrm{Cat})

preserves Segal objects, then the pullback functor
\varphi^{*}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathfrak{P}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathfrak{O}}}

preserves fibrous patterns.

(2)

If
\varphi
is an inert-cocartesian fibration and the left Kan extension functor

\varphi_{!}\colon\operatorname{Fun}({\mathfrak{O}},\mathrm{Cat})\rightarrow\operatorname{Fun}({\mathcal{P}},\mathrm{Cat})

preserves Segal objects, then postcomposition

\varphi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathfrak{O}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{/{\mathfrak{P}}}

preserves fibrous patterns.

In particular, if
\varphi
is an inert-cocartesian Segal morphism with soundly extendable codomain whose left Kan extension preserves Segal categories, then pullback and postcomposition restrict to an adjunction on fibrous patterns

\varphi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{O}})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{P}})\colon\varphi^{*}

Proof.

Our argument is only a minor variation of
[
BHS22
, Lem 4.1.19]
.
In either case, the property of being an inert-cocartesian fibration is always preserved, either by assumption or by
[
BHS22
, Obs 2.2.6]
.

We prove (1) first.
Fixing
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathrm{Fbrs}({\mathfrak{P}})
, by
[
BHS22
, Obs 4.1.3]
, it suffices to prove that the left vertical arrow in the following pullback diagram is a relative Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-category.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{St}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{\mathrm{int}}(\varphi^{*}{\mathscr{F}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}\mathrm{St}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\mathrm{int}}{\mathscr{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

By
[
BHS22
, Lem 3.1.10]
, relative Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-categories are pullback-stable, so it suffices to prove that the right vertical arrow is a relative Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-category.
By sound extendability
\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{P}}
-
\infty
-category, and since
\varphi^{*}
preserves Segal
\infty
-categories,
\varphi^{*}\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-category;
by
[
BHS22
, Obs 3.1.8]
it then suffices to prove that
\varphi^{*}\mathrm{St}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\mathrm{int}}{\mathscr{F}}
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{O}}
-
\infty
-category.
Since
\varphi^{*}
preserves Segal
\infty
-categories, it suffices to prove that
\mathrm{St}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\mathrm{int}}{\mathscr{F}}
is a Segal
{\mathfrak{P}}
-category, which follows by the assumption that
{\mathscr{F}}
is a fibrous pattern.

(2) is similar;
this time, by taking left adjoints to the commutative square of
[
BHS22
, Prop 4.2.5]
, it suffices to prove that the composition

\varphi_{!}\mathrm{St}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{\mathrm{int}}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow\varphi_{!}\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}\rightarrow\mathscr{A}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}

is relative Segal;
since
{\mathfrak{P}}
is soundly extendable,
[
BHS22
, Obs 3.1.8]
again reduces this to verifying that
\varphi_{!}\mathrm{St}_{{\mathfrak{O}}}^{\mathrm{int}}{\mathscr{F}}
is Segal;
this follows from the facts that
{\mathscr{F}}
is a fibrous pattern and
\varphi_{!}
preserves Segal
\infty
-categories.
∎

A.3.
Segal morphisms between effective Burnside patterns

We now fill our grab bag with a wide variety of Segal morphisms between effective Burnside patterns.

Proposition A.2
.

Suppose
I\subset J\subset{\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
are weakly extensive subcategories.
Then, the inclusion

\iota\colon\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{J}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})

is a Segal morphism.

Proof.

We are tasked with verifying that precomposition with
\iota
preserves product-preserving functors, i.e. that
\iota
is a product-preserving functor.
In fact, this is immediate, since a functor
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
is product-preserving if and only if the backwards maps
(S\leftarrow U)_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(S)}
together map to a product diagram, which is obviously true of
\iota
.
∎

Proposition A.2
.

Suppose
\varphi\colon V\rightarrow W
is a morphism in
{\mathcal{T}}
.
Then, the associated functor

\operatorname{Span}_{I}(\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{W})\colon\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{W})

is a Segal morphism.

Proof.

We’re tasked with proving that precomposition along
\operatorname{Span}(\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{W})
preserves product-preserving functors, i.e. it is a product-preserving functor.
Since
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})
and
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{W})
are semiadditive, it is equivalent to prove that
\operatorname{Span}(\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{W})
is coproduct-preserving;
since coproducts in
\operatorname{Span}_{I}({\math@bb{F}}_{V})
are computed in
{\math@bb{F}}_{V}
, it’s equivalent to prove that
\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{W}\colon{\math@bb{F}}_{V}\rightarrow{\math@bb{F}}_{W}
is coproduct-preserving, which follows from the fact that it’s a left adjoint.
∎

Proposition A.2
.

If
f\colon{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}}
is a functor of
\infty
-categories sending an atomic orbital subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
into an atomic orbital subcategory
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
, then the associated functor
\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
is a Segal morphism.

Proof.

By
[
CH21
, Rem 4.3]
, it suffices to verify that
f^{\mathrm{el}}_{X/}
induces an equivalence on the left vertical arrow

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\lim_{\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{T}})^{\mathrm{el}}_{f(X)/}}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prodop\displaylimits_{U\in\mathrm{Orb}(f(X))}F(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\lim_{\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})^{\mathrm{el}}_{X/}}F\circ f^{\mathrm{el}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prodop\displaylimits_{V\in\mathrm{Orb}(X)}Ff(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

whenever
F
is restricted from a Segal
\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})
space.
This follows by noting that the horizontal arrows are equivalences and
\operatorname{Span}(f)
sends the set of orbits of
X
bijectively onto the set of orbits of
f(X)
.
∎

Proposition A.2
.

If
{\mathcal{P}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
is an atomic orbital subcategory such that
{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{T}}
have compatible terminal objects, then the induced functor

\wedge\coloneqq\operatorname{Span}(\times)\colon\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\times\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{P}}})\xrightarrow{\wedge}\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})

is compatible with Segal objects.

Proof.

By
[
CH21
, Ex 5.7]
, a functor
\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\times\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\math@bb{F}}_{{\mathcal{T}}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}
is a Segal object if and only if it preserves products separately in each variable.
Hence we’re tasked with verifying that
\wedge^{*}F
preserves products separately in each variable whenever
F
preserves products.
In fact, this follows by distributivity of products and coproducs in
{\math@bb{F}}^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
;
indeed, we have

\displaystyle\wedge^{*}F\left(\left(X_{+}\oplus Z_{+},Y_{+}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq F\left(\left(X\sqcup X^{\prime}\right)\times Y\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\simeq F\left(\left(X\times Y\right)\sqcup\left(X^{\prime}\times Y\right)\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\simeq F\left(\left(X_{+}\wedge Y_{+}\right)\oplus\left(X^{\prime}_{+}\wedge Y_{+}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq F\left(X_{+}\wedge Y_{+}\right)\oplus F\left(X^{\prime}_{+}\wedge Y_{+}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\wedge^{*}F\left(X_{+},Y_{+}\right)\oplus\wedge^{*}F\left(X^{\prime}_{+},Y_{+}\right).\qed
